Taking the *minimum* instead would usually surface the 52-week quartile, ~25-30% below spot — technically conservative and operationally useless). `zone_fit_score()` is the optional ranking hook wired into `_scoring.technical_score` under the `zone_fit` weight (**ships at 0.0** — display-only by default). Fully fail-soft, never raises, and this module itself **never gates** — it only computes and returns numbers. (D2) `min_credit` is now also read by `risk_engine.validate_candidates` and by both generators as the primary income gate (`income.require_vrp_edge`, reason `premium_below_fair_value`): the Rules Engine, not `fair_value.py`, is what rejects — this module has no reject path of its own, so the "Rules Engine is the sole path to an order" invariant is unchanged. Tunables live in `risk_limits.yaml → ideal_zone` / `income`. **Deterministic tier** — it may read technicals/IV/fundamentals/Black-Scholes and nothing else (enforced by `tests/test_eval_skills.py::test_fair_value_stays_in_the_deterministic_tier`). |
| `sector_context.py` | **Single-ticker `/scan TICKER` only.** Builds a `SectorContext`: the ticker's yfinance GICS sector/industry, the matching SPDR sector ETF (e.g. Technology→XLK), and recent returns (sector ETF, SPY, and the ticker itself) computed from the day-cached `price_data.get_ohlcv` store, plus a relative-strength figure (ticker − sector, 1-month). `render_sector_context()` turns it into a compact "SECTOR & MARKET BACKDROP" prompt block. Fully fail-soft — any missing piece degrades to `None` and the rest still renders; enrichment only, never reaches the engine. |
| `technicals.py` | RSI, MACD, moving averages, ATR (volatility), `atr_ratio` (ATR/price — a normalised volatility measure, renamed from the misleading `trend_strength`/`_adx_proxy`, N16), support/resistance levels, and a market regime classifier (trending up/down/sideways). Settled OHLCV bars come from `price_data.get_ohlcv` (the incremental store — see below), and `_working_frame` overlays today's bar from a live price so `TechnicalStats.price` and the indicators reflect the live session, falling back to the last settled close on error. `get_technical_stats` accepts an optional `spot_override`: when the orchestrator has already inferred a live spot from the IBKR option chain via `iv.infer_spot_from_quotes` (put-call parity) this scan cycle, that value is used and `TechnicalStats.price_source="ibkr"`. It also accepts an optional `cached_yf_price` (S1 follow-up): a `fast_info` price the intraday materiality probe already fetched this cycle for a symbol whose chain was skipped — reused instead of a second `_fetch_last_price` call, still recorded as `price_source="yfinance"`. Failing both, the price comes from a fresh `_fetch_last_price` (`yf.Ticker(symbol).fast_info["lastPrice"]`, a cheap *uncached* live quote) and `price_source="yfinance"`. **Phase classification (modernization Phase 3):** `classify_phase(price, sma_50, sma_200, slope_50, slope_200, rsi)` applies Minervini/Weinstein 4-stage rules — `UPTREND` (price > 50-SMA > 200-SMA, 200-SMA rising), `DISTRIBUTION` (price below a flattening 50-SMA, RSI < 50, 200-SMA still rising), `DOWNTREND` (price below both SMAs, both falling), else `BASE` — into `TechnicalStats.phase`, with each SMA's slope from `_annualized_slope` (a 21-bar geometric annualization). `TechnicalStats.relative_strength` is the **same `_annualized_slope` applied to the stock's own close series** — despite the name and despite `docs/modernization/phase-3-phase-rs.md` specifying RS relative to SPY (`stock_return / spy_return`), no SPY comparison was implemented; it is a plain momentum figure. Both are inert today (see `STATUS.md`'s "Phase classification + relative strength" entry): no test coverage exists, `cash_secured_put.py`'s `reject_downtrend` gate defaults off, and `_scoring.relative_strength_score` (populated on `ScoreCard.relative_strength` by both generators) feeds `blended_score` directly in `engine/scoring.py::score_candidates` — not through `technical_score` — at `scoring_weights.yaml`'s `relative_strength: 0.0` (both `covered_call`/`cash_secured_put` blocks). |
| `price_data.py` | **Incremental OHLCV loader** — the single source of settled daily bars for both `technicals.py` and HV30 (`iv.py`). `get_ohlcv(symbol)` reads the `price_history` table and fetches **only the missing tail** from yfinance (or a full year when the store is empty), persisting new settled sessions; the current forming session is never stored (callers overlay the live price). `@daily_cached`, so within a long-lived process it resolves once per symbol per day and the 15-min loop reuses it with no DB/network round trip. Replaces the old per-scan full 1y (+3mo) yfinance pulls. |
| `fundamentals.py` | Free cash flow, debt levels, dividend safety, earnings quality, and next earnings date (via yfinance) |
| `liquidity.py` | Bid/ask spread quality, open interest, and volume — filters out options that are too thinly traded to sell. **`liquidity_failures(quote, *, enforce_volume=True) -> list[str]`** (Task 7, 2026-09-29) is the primary function: it returns **every** gate the quote fails, as one of seven precise codes — `illiquid_no_quote` (no usable bid/ask), `illiquid_zero_bid` (bid is 0 with a positive ask), `illiquid_spread_wide` (over `max_bid_ask_spread_pct`), `illiquid_oi_missing` / `illiquid_oi_low`, `illiquid_volume_missing` / `illiquid_volume_low` — replacing a bare bool that collapsed all five failure modes into one undifferentiated `illiquid` code (2026-09 post-mortem: "SOXL failed liquidity 684 times" was unactionable). `passes_liquidity_gates(quote, *, enforce_volume=True) -> bool` is now `not liquidity_failures(...)` — same signature, still what `rolling.py` calls directly. The day-volume codes are **time-aware** (N19): before `liquidity.morning_volume_cutoff_et` (default 10:30 ET) `volume_gate_active()` returns False and the volume codes are skipped (OI + spread still apply), since day volume often hasn't printed in the first 30 minutes of RTH. |
| `sentiment.py` | **Composite social + news sentiment scorer.** `SentimentScorer.score(symbol)` returns a `SentimentDetail` (schema) blending up to three independent, fail-soft sources into one 0-100 `overall` (50 = neutral, `None` = no data anywhere): **StockTwits** (public JSON API, no key — users self-tag messages Bullish/Bearish, the strongest signal; untagged messages fall back to VADER), **News** (recent headlines via yfinance, no key, each VADER-scored), and **Reddit** (r/options + r/wallstreetbets via praw — OPTIONAL, contributes nothing unless `REDDIT_CLIENT_ID/SECRET` are set; the `.json` no-auth hack is dead, blocked by Cloudflare). Text is scored with **VADER** (`vaderSentiment`, offline, no key — handles negation/intensifiers, unlike the old keyword bag). StockTwits is fetched via **`curl_cffi`** with Chrome TLS impersonation because StockTwits sits behind Cloudflare, which 403s plain `httpx`/`requests` on TLS fingerprint; falls back to `httpx` if `curl_cffi` is absent. Sources are blended by volume/confidence (`_blend`: StockTwits primary, news secondary, Reddit tie-breaker). **1-day velocity** (`delta_1d`) is computed against a small `data/sentiment_history.json` store (pruned to 7 days, fail-soft). `overall` feeds scoring via `ScoreCard.sentiment_score` (5% weight in `scoring_weights.yaml`); the full breakdown is surfaced to Claude and the Telegram deep-dive card and logged to the verdict ledger — **enrichment only, never gates or sizes** (the fence). Each source function is `@daily_cached` on the symbol (S4), so each API is hit at most once per calendar day per symbol; `SentimentScorer` keeps a per-instance cache for within-scan reuse. |
| `black_scholes.py` | Black-Scholes **full Greeks** (`bs_delta`, `bs_gamma`, `bs_theta`, `bs_vega`, `bs_rho`) and `bs_price`. Pure math — no IBKR connection. Used as a fallback by `src/ibkr/market_data.py` when IBKR returns no `modelGreeks` (delayed-data paper accounts); the fallback paths now fill gamma/theta/vega alongside delta so the card surfaces the full Greeks whenever a chain carries an IV. |
| `american_option.py` | **American-option pricer** (Phase 1) — a Cox-Ross-Rubinstein binomial-tree price (`american_price`) and the resulting `early_exercise_premium` (= American − European BS). Pure math, no external deps. Foundation for economic (not heuristic) assignment triggers; the early-exercise premium is the economic condition under which covered-call early assignment actually happens. |

---

### `src/strategies/` — Trade candidate generators

Each module takes the analytics data and generates specific trades you could place.

| File | What it generates |
|---|
| `covered_call.py` | Covered call candidates: for each stock you own, finds the best call strike to sell (target delta, expiry, annualized yield). `screen_cc_candidates(...)` is the full screen returning a `ScreenResult` (passed **+ rejected + skip reason**); `generate_cc_candidates(...)` is the thin wrapper returning only the passing slate. `uncovered_call_capacity(positions, underlying) -> int` (fixed 2026-09-11) extracts `screen_cc_candidates`'s own sizing formula (`floor(long shares / 100) - existing short calls`) for a caller that only needs the number, not a full screen — `execution/approval.py`'s send-time share-ownership re-check uses it against a fresh position snapshot immediately before an order goes out. |
| `cash_secured_put.py` | Cash-secured put candidates: for `would_own` stocks, finds put strikes that offer good yield without excessive assignment risk. **Sized to headroom, not to the maximum affordable lot (D1):** `screen_csp_candidates(..., *, positions=None)` calls `engine.capital.resolve_caps`/`seed_budgets`/`max_contracts` — the same helper the Rules Engine uses — so contract count is the largest lot that fits every cash and concentration constraint at once (cash, per-ticker/sector risk units, the large-position slot), rather than the min of cash-and-CSP-budget alone; the two paths can no longer disagree about how big a position may be. `positions` seeds the running budgets from what the account already holds (both `scan.py` call sites pass the current snapshot; omitted it defaults to an empty portfolio). When no size fits at all, the candidate still renders at a display-only 1 lot tagged `insufficient_cash` (cash was the binding constraint) or `no_headroom` (a concentration/budget cap was) — the reason names which. Same `screen_csp_candidates` / `generate_csp_candidates` pairing as above. **Optional phase-aware reject (modernization Phase 3, default off):** when `risk_limits.yaml → cash_secured_put.reject_downtrend` is `true` and `tech_stats.phase == Phase.DOWNTREND`, the candidate is rejected with `REASON_PHASE_DOWNTREND` — ships disabled pending paper validation (see `STATUS.md`). `covered_call.py` gets the same `phase` field for display only; it never gates a CC, since the shares are already held. |
| `_evaluation.py` | Shared plumbing for screens that report **why** a contract was rejected. `ScreenResult` carries `passed`, `rejected` (`(candidate, reason_codes)`), `evaluated`, and `skipped` (set when the symbol never reached the per-quote loop at all — `not_in_would_own`, `no_long_shares`, `shares_already_covered`), plus a `tally()` for the diagnostic log. `rank_rejects(...)` orders rejects closest-to-passing: fewest distinct failed gates, then nearest the delta-band midpoint, then highest ROC. `display_premium(...)` prices a contract we only intend to *display* (falling back past `strict_mid` where the trading path never would — the accompanying `no_two_sided_market` reason marks it untrusted). **`liquidity_limits_summary(*, enforce_volume) -> str`** (Task 7) renders the live `risk_limits.yaml → liquidity` thresholds — e.g. `"spread≤10%, OI≥100, vol≥10, volume gate on"` — read from config rather than hard-coded, appended to each generator's aggregate `rejections: …` WARNING line so the granular `illiquid_*` tally (see `liquidity.py` above) is legible without opening the YAML. Reason codes deliberately reuse the risk engine's vocabulary where the meaning matches, so one `_REJECT_REASON_LABELS` table humanizes both stages. `REASON_ILLIQUID = "illiquid"` is kept as a legacy constant — no generator emits it anymore, but the label tables still humanize the ~14 days of pre-existing `risk_verdicts` rows that carry it. **Every quote is evaluated against all gates rather than short-circuiting on the first failure**, so a reject carries its complete failure list and the ranking is meaningful. |
| `rolling.py` | Roll candidates: for existing short options approaching expiry or breaching delta limits, suggests the best roll-forward trade. Roll credit uses a **live quote** for the current contract; if no live quote is found the candidate is skipped. `generate_roll_candidates(..., *, defensive: bool = False)` runs **two separate economics (D4)**: the default **income** path rolls an unchallenged position forward and still requires a net credit clearing `income.min_roc_pct`/`min_annualized_yield_pct`; `defensive=True` (passed by every monitor-triggered caller, via `roll_pipeline.queue_roll_for_approval`) is for rescuing a challenged, deep-delta short — it skips the ROC/yield tests entirely and instead permits a bounded net debit (`monitor.roll_defensive.max_debit`), requires the new leg to cut `|delta|` by at least `min_delta_reduction`, and (when `require_breakeven_improvement` is set) requires the new strike to improve the breakeven whenever the roll isn't itself a credit. Requiring 1% ROC from a defensive roll made the candidate list empty exactly when the monitor's roll trigger fired, since a 0.60-delta short usually cannot be rolled to a safer delta for a credit. Both paths still populate `TradeCandidate.ideal` (Task 5's D2 follow-up), so the risk engine's variance-risk-premium gate reaches ROLL candidates regardless of which economics produced them. DTE is computed from `market_hours.today_et()`, not local `date.today()` — the earlier local-date read could fire `should_roll` a day early for an operator east of US/Eastern. |
| `buy_candidates.py` | Buy-to-own candidates: stocks from the watchlist worth buying specifically so you can sell covered calls against them. Scores each on IV rank, fundamental quality, and technical regime, then applies a score floor and count cap (`scoring_weights.yaml → buy_to_own.min_score` / `max_candidates`) so it surfaces only the strongest few — without the floor it returned every non-held, non-bearish universe name (e.g. 46/46 over a weekend). Also enriches each with price/IV/HV/VRP/RSI/SMA/earnings/dividend, an estimated monthly CC yield, a deterministic rationale, and the `sector` tag looked up from `universe.yaml → sectors` (used by `format_buy_list` to group candidates by sector). |
| `_scoring.py` | Shared scoring helpers used by all strategy modules. `technical_score` aligns regime to **short** premium (we sell): BULLISH favours selling puts (CSP), BEARISH/SIDEWAYS favours selling calls (CC) — the opposite of buy-side alignment. `fundamental_score` is a quality/dividend step function. `make_candidate_id` is a deterministic, **date-free** hash of `strategy\|underlying\|right\|strike\|expiry` (the property that lets the 15-min loop dedupe via `has_active_order`). `technical_score` also takes an optional `IdealZone` and blends in `fair_value.zone_fit_score` under the per-strategy `zone_fit` weight — the "support/resistance proximity" that `scoring_weights.yaml` has always described but never implemented. `zone_fit` **ships at 0.0**, so the default output is bit-identical to the regime-only score; enabling it is a deliberate human config change. |

---

### `src/engine/` — The decision and safety layer

This is where candidates get ranked and filtered.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `capital.py` | **Capital arithmetic (D1, Task 9's `portfolio:` block consumes this).** Pure functions, no I/O, no config reads — callers pass the resolved `risk` dict. `risk_units(collateral, current_iv, dte)` computes capital-at-risk over the option's life (`collateral × IV × √(DTE/365)`; `current_iv` is a **percent**), returning `None` when IV is unavailable so callers fall back to a stricter raw-collateral cap rather than treating the position as risk-free. `resolve_caps(account, risk) -> Caps` resolves `risk_limits.yaml → portfolio` percentages into absolute dollar limits for one account snapshot, in a **fixed, non-configurable order**: the cash reserve (`cash_reserve_pct` / `cash_reserve_absolute`, greater-of) comes off `account.excess_liquidity` first, and the CSP budget (`max_csp_allocation_pct_of_deployable`) is a percentage of what remains — expressing both against gross cash would let the two knobs contradict each other. `seed_budgets(positions, sector_of, iv_of=None, *, caps=None) -> Budgets` seeds running tallies from current positions (a short put charges its assignment liability, matching how a new CSP candidate is charged). A position snapshot carries no IV of its own, so the third argument is a per-symbol IV lookup: supply it and **option** positions also charge the risk-unit tallies (`ticker_risk`/`sector_risk`) at their own DTE — without it those tallies start empty, and existing exposure counts for nothing against the per-ticker/per-sector concentration caps on the (common) path where the *candidate's* own IV is known. Callers that already hold portfolio-wide IV pass it (the full scan sweep via `risk_engine.validate_candidates(..., iv_by_symbol=...)`, `scripts/capacity_report.py`); callers where it would cost a fresh network round-trip on a latency-sensitive path (the order-approval re-validation gate, a single-ticker `/scan SYM` deep-dive) deliberately do not. Stock positions never get risk-unit seeding (no natural DTE) and neither does an expired or IV-less option — those, and every caller that omits `iv_of`, charge the raw-collateral tally only. The keyword-only `caps` (fixed 2026-09-11) seeds `Budgets.large_tickers` too: any symbol whose existing cumulative collateral already exceeds `max_ticker_collateral` starts the cycle already occupying a large-position slot, so a name already over cap purely from the book (no candidate has touched it yet) can't let `max_large_positions` further names go large on top of it; both production callers pass it, resolving `caps` immediately beforehand. `max_contracts(...) -> (contracts, binding)` finds the largest lot that fits every constraint at once (cash, CSP budget, ticker/sector risk units, and the large-position slot measured on **cumulative** per-ticker collateral — the marginal-lot comparison it replaces was blind to the book, so a name already at the ceiling looked empty to every new candidate). `strategies/cash_secured_put.py` sizes with it; `risk_engine.validate_candidates` gates with the same `resolve_caps`/`seed_budgets`/`charge` arithmetic plus an inline copy of `_fits`'s checks (it walks a ranked batch rather than growing one lot, so it can't call `_fits` directly) — the two must be kept in step, and that shared arithmetic is what makes the generator/gate disagreement that caused D1 unrepresentable. `charge(...)` mutates a `Budgets` in place so later candidates in the same batch see reduced headroom (the same greedy cumulative-consumption model the risk engine has always used, extended to risk units). `Budgets.large_tickers` is a **set of symbols**, not a count (fixed 2026-09-11): a slot belongs to a ticker, so `charge` adds a symbol to it whenever that ticker's CUMULATIVE collateral (not the marginal lot alone) crosses `max_ticker_collateral` — a second candidate on an already-large ticker is then a no-op add, never a second slot consumed, and `_fits`/`validate_candidates` only require a *new* free slot for a symbol not already in the set. |
| `scoring.py` | Normalizes raw analytics scores (IV rank, RSI, etc.) into a 0–100 scale |
| `decision_engine.py` | Combines scores with the configured weights, ranks candidates by total score, and selects the top N. `select_top_candidates_detailed(...)` returns `(top, dropped)` where each dropped candidate carries **why** — `"dedupe"` (a better strike on the same name won the slot) or `"top_n"` (`max_new_positions_per_run` was already full). Both used to vanish silently, making a full slate indistinguishable from a gated-out one; `select_top_candidates` remains as the thin wrapper. |
| `risk_engine.py` | **The safety gate.** Checks hard limits and returns PASS/REJECT with reasons. No AI. `validate_candidates` is **portfolio-aware**: it walks the ranked batch in priority order and enforces *cumulative* limits via `engine.capital` — deployable-cash feasibility, per-ticker/per-sector concentration in **risk units** (`max_risk_units_per_ticker_pct`/`max_risk_units_per_sector_pct`, falling back to raw-collateral `max_collateral_per_ticker_pct` when IV is unknown), the large-position slot (measured on **cumulative** per-ticker collateral, which is what makes it the raw-collateral backstop when `iv_by_symbol` is absent — see below), and total cash-secured-put collateral against the deployable-cash CSP budget — so multiple candidates can't each claim the whole account (D1, Task 9; see the `risk_limits.yaml` `portfolio:` block above for the full key reference). **Before any of that cumulative walk runs, `_select_budget_representatives` groups pass-1 survivors by `(underlying, strategy)` and keeps only the single highest-scoring one per group to spend the shared budget** — every other survivor in the group is rejected `dedupe_pre_gate` without ever touching the cumulative checks at all (2026-09-11 fix: a busy scan cycle's dozen-plus strikes/expiries on one name used to compete against each other for that name's own per-ticker budget, which is how 95 of 96 `concentration_limit` rejects over two weeks turned out to be a single symbol, TQQQ, held at zero); covered calls and rolls are excluded from grouping since they never reached the shared budget either way, and cross-symbol competition among the surviving representatives is unchanged. **A keyword-only `dedupe_same_symbol: bool = True` controls whether that grouping runs at all** (final-review fix): the default groups/dedupes exactly as above and is what every batch caller wants — the full scan sweep and the order-approval re-validation gate in `execution/approval.py` (which deliberately keeps it on so two approved-but-unexecuted orders on one underlying, approved in different scan cycles, can't both slip through); `False` skips `_select_budget_representatives` entirely, so every pass-1 survivor is its own representative and none is ever `dedupe_pre_gate`-rejected — the old single-pass greedy behaviour, restored on purpose for `scan._price_and_gate_ticker` (the `/scan TICKER` deep-dive, and the `promote` handler that reuses its pricing path), a browse/compare view whose whole point is showing every strike on one ticker that individually qualifies, not picking a winner among them. The optional `iv_by_symbol=` argument is forwarded to `capital.seed_budgets` so the account's *existing* option positions charge those risk-unit budgets too, instead of the batch's candidates only being measured against each other; the full scan sweep passes the IV map it already computed, and the two paths that would have to buy that data with a new network call (the order-approval re-validation gate, the single-ticker deep-dive) omit it by design — on those, existing exposure reaches the caps through the **cumulative** per-ticker collateral comparison in the large-position check, which bounds any one ticker at `max_pct_per_ticker_large` regardless of what is known about the candidate's own IV. It also gates per candidate: the variance-risk-premium floor (D2, primary — `income.require_vrp_edge`, reject when `premium < candidate.ideal.min_credit`, reason `premium_below_fair_value`; a missing ideal zone never blocks), ROC/yield noise floors (`income.min_roc_pct`/`min_annualized_yield_pct`, now secondary — see `fair_value.py` below for why), IV-rank floor (when known), DTE window, delta (required for income strategies), and the earnings blackout. **Those three income gates (ROC, annualized yield, VRP) apply to the income strategies only.** A `Strategy.ROLL` is a repair, not an income trade: a defensive roll books `roc_pct = 0.0` by construction and pays a premium deliberately under the new strike's fair value, so leaving rolls inside the gates rejected *every* defensive roll — structurally, at the approval-queue re-gate in `execution/approval.py`, after the operator had already approved the card. A roll's economics are bounded by `strategies/rolling.py`'s own `monitor.roll_defensive` knobs (`max_debit`, `min_delta_reduction`, `require_breakeven_improvement`); its `ideal` zone stays populated and visible on the card as display/audit. `validate_live_quote` is the second pass at execution time, re-checking a single candidate against the fresh live quote (delta drift / collapsed mid). |

---

### `src/claude/` — The AI review layer

Invokes Claude to add plain-English reasoning to the top candidates.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `runner.py` | Dispatches `review_candidates`, `review_roll`, and `write_journal_narrative` to a backend chosen by `config/settings.yaml → claude.backend`. **`"cli"`** (code default): shells out to `claude -p`, passing candidate data as JSON. `_build_cmd` assembles the **hardened** command (N3): `--max-turns 1`, `--disallowedTools <denylist>`, optional `--model` pin — so the unattended subprocess (~26+×/day) can't use tools or touch the filesystem. **`"ollama"`** (**active in this deployment** — no `claude -p` access): delegates entirely to `ollama_runner.py` (local `qwen3.5:4b`, Task 10). **`"cli_then_ollama"`**: tries `claude -p` first and falls back to `ollama_runner.py` if it returns nothing (CLI unavailable, timed out, unparseable, or — relevant after the June 2026 Agent SDK credit split — the monthly `claude -p` credit pool is exhausted). `review_candidates` also forwards the scan's `MarketConditions` (VIX) and scan-time `spot_prices` (N17) so the prompt carries the macro-vol regime and *current* price levels — enrichment only, never a deterministic gate. **Task 10:** both `_review_candidates_cli` call sites now pass the requested `candidate_id` list into `parse_claude_output` so a hallucinated or stale id in the model's reply is dropped rather than silently accepted. |
| `ollama_runner.py` | Local-LLM backend mirroring `runner.py`'s three functions against a local `ollama serve` instance (`POST /api/generate`, `temperature: 0.2` (config `ollama_temperature`), `num_ctx: 24576` (config `ollama_num_ctx`, raised from `16384` in Task 10 fix round 1) since the strategist prompt — universe context + history + real analytics + the FACTS/rubric text — *plus the generated JSON* far exceeds Ollama's 4096 default (measured on the production-shaped 10-candidate prompt: 12,461 prompt tokens + up to ~2,630 generated — 13,693-13,863 prompt tokens once Task 11's NEWS block / research turn is included, 2026-09-30; an undersized window silently left-truncates the prompt and/or cuts the JSON mid-object → dropped reviews), `keep_alive: 2m` (config `ollama_keep_alive`, lowered from `10m` in the same fix round — see `settings.yaml`'s comment for why the scan loop's 15-min cadence was already cold-loading under `10m`), `think: false` to disable hybrid-reasoning models' (e.g. `qwen3`) `<think>` traces, which otherwise fight the format constraint and bloat latency — `think: true` was tried and did not finish within 180s on the same prompt). **Task 10 — schema-constrained structured output:** `_generate(prompt, cfg, schema=REVIEW_SCHEMA)` sends Ollama's `format` as a **JSON Schema** (Ollama's structured-output mode grammar-constrains every generated token to it), not the bare `format: "json"` string the review path used before — `REVIEW_SCHEMA` (`{"type": "object", "properties": {"reviews": {"type": "array", "items": <ClaudeReview-shaped `_REVIEW_ITEM`>}}, "required": ["reviews"]}`) is module-level and is `_generate`'s default; `review_roll`/`write_journal_narrative` pass `schema="json"` (the prior unconstrained mode) since their payload is a single object the shared parser already tolerates either way. This replaced the old always-`"wait"`/misread-moneyness behaviour that motivated the fix. Reuses the same prompt builders as the CLI path — `review_candidates` also threads the requested `candidate_id` list into `parse_ollama_review_output` so a review for an id that wasn't asked for is dropped, not silently kept. Any failure (connection refused, timeout, invalid JSON, schema mismatch) returns the same fail-soft `[]`/`None` as the CLI path. Also exposes `probe_ollama()` — a cheap `GET /api/tags` reachability + configured-model check (returns `(ok, message)`, never raises) used by the launcher and healthcheck to surface a down server or unpulled model up front instead of silently degrading to the deterministic-only path. **Task 11 — news-grounded review:** `review_candidates` gates a whole extra step on one flag, `cfg.tool_research_enabled` (compared with `is True`, not truthiness — see `news_context.py`/`ollama_tools.py` below for why). When on: (1) `news_context.news_block_for_candidates` builds the `=== NEWS ===` block for the candidates' underlyings and it's threaded into `build_prompt(..., news_block=...)`; (2) `ollama_tools.research_turn(prompt, cfg)` runs the bounded tool-calling round and returns the accumulated chat messages, or `None` on any failure; (3) on success, `_generate_chat(messages, cfg, schema=REVIEW_SCHEMA)` — a new helper POSTing the message history to `/api/chat` with `format=REVIEW_SCHEMA` and **no** `tools` — makes the final structured call. Any failure at any of these steps (research turn raises, `/api/chat` errors, malformed response) falls through to the original Task 10 `_generate(prompt, cfg)` (`/api/generate`) single-shot path — the feature can only add a review, never block one. **Final review I2 (2026-09-30) — one shared deadline:** the whole path (NEWS fetch → research turn → final chat → single-shot fallback) runs against one deadline `D = tool_research_timeout_seconds + ollama_timeout_seconds` (240s shipped), measured from the start of `review_candidates`. The NEWS fetch gets `deadline=min(D, start + news_fetch_budget_seconds)`; `research_turn(prompt, cfg, deadline=D)` caps its POST at `min(tool_research_timeout_seconds, remaining)` and skips any `search_news` call once `D` has passed; the final chat gets the remaining time, or is **skipped** when less than `review_min_call_seconds` (the floor, 60s) remains; the fallback `_generate(prompt, cfg, timeout=...)` gets `min(ollama_timeout_seconds, max(remaining, floor))`. At most one call can run past `D`, by at most the floor — the review is bounded by `D + floor` = 300s, the figure `market_data.chain_fetch_budget_seconds` is sized against. The old hard-coded 10s `_RESEARCH_MIN_FINAL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` is gone. |
| `news_context.py` | **Task 11.** Builds the strategist prompt's numbered `=== NEWS ===` block. `_fetch(query, days, limit)` combines `get_news_search_provider().search(...)` (Google News RSS) with, for an actual ticker-shaped query, `get_news_provider().get_headlines(...)` (yfinance) — each degrades independently to `[]` on failure. `build_news_block(symbols, *, per_symbol, days, max_items=25)` calls `_fetch` once per distinct symbol plus once for the fixed market-backdrop query `"stock market today"` (3 headlines), dedupes globally by normalised (casefolded, whitespace-collapsed) title, caps the total at `max_items`, truncates each title to 140 chars, and numbers survivors `N1`...`Nk` — returning `("", {})` when nothing was found so the caller simply omits the block. Both take an optional `deadline` (a `time.monotonic()` value, final review I2): no new query starts once it has passed, and whatever was already fetched is still rendered — the NEWS fetch is the first slice of the review's shared deadline. `news_block_for_candidates(candidates, ...)` is the convenience wrapper `ollama_runner.review_candidates` and `scripts/review_eval.py` actually call — the distinct underlyings from a candidate list, wrapped in its own try/except so a bug here can never break a review. Enrichment tier only (CLAUDE.md fence) — never importable from `src/engine/`, `src/execution/`, or `src/strategies/`. |
| `ollama_tools.py` | **Task 11.** The bounded tool-calling research turn. `research_turn(prompt, cfg, *, deadline=None)` POSTs one `/api/chat` request (timeout `min(tool_research_timeout_seconds, deadline − now)`; no `search_news` call starts after `deadline` — a skipped one gets a "(skipped — review time budget exhausted)" tool message; final review I2) with `tools=[search_news]` and an instruction appended to the user turn ("you MAY call search_news up to `cfg.max_tool_rounds` times for anything the NEWS block leaves open... do not call it otherwise"). If the model's response carries `tool_calls`, each is executed against `get_news_search_provider().search(...)` (capped at 5 results, and at `cfg.max_tool_rounds` calls total), and the results are appended as `role: "tool"` messages numbered to continue the NEWS block's `N#` ids (`_next_news_id` scans the prompt text for the highest existing `N#`) so the DECISION RUBRIC's evidence-citation instruction stays valid for whatever the research turn found. Returns the accumulated message list — **not** the final review; `ollama_runner._generate_chat` makes that separate call with `format=REVIEW_SCHEMA` and no `tools`. Returns `None` on any failure (HTTP error, malformed `/api/chat` response) so the caller falls back to the single-shot path — a model without tool support, or a timed-out research round, degrades to Task 10 behaviour rather than dropping the review. Enrichment tier only (CLAUDE.md fence). |
| `parser.py` | Validates and parses Claude's JSON response into a structured `ClaudeReview` object. On any failure, returns an empty result — the pipeline always continues. Also provides `parse_ollama_review_output` / `parse_ollama_roll_output` / `parse_ollama_journal_output`, which run the same `ClaudeReview`/`RollReview` validation against Ollama's response text (no CLI envelope to unwrap). **Task 10:** the shared `_parse_reviews(text, prefix, requested_ids=None)` now unwraps the `{"reviews": [...]}` shape both backends' prompts request (in addition to a bare array or a single object, still auto-wrapped) — `parse_claude_output` and `parse_ollama_review_output` both take an optional `requested_ids` list; when given, any parsed review whose `candidate_id` isn't in that set is dropped and `"<prefix>: reviewed %d/%d candidates"` is logged, so a hallucinated id or a stale echo from prior context never reaches the caller as if it were requested. **Final review M4 (2026-09-30):** repeated `candidate_id`s are deduped (first occurrence kept, repeats logged) before the `requested_ids` filter, and the "reviewed N/M" log counts distinct ids. |
| `memory.py` | The learning-loop outcome recorder. Back-fills each `claude_memory` row with what actually happened — `filled` (executor), `user_rejected` (Telegram reject), `risk_rejected` (re-validation), `expired` (TTL) — so later scans inject real outcomes (not just rejections) into the strategist prompt. |
| `prompts/` | Prompt templates: `strategist.py` (candidate review — injects `_UNIVERSE_CONTEXT`, a compact tier/IV/assignment reference for every ticker in the universe, now banner-flagged as **stale Jun-2026 anchors**; injects a **scan-time spot-price block** (N17) marked authoritative so Claude reasons from current levels; also renders a VIX regime line and each candidate's VRP. Each candidate is also annotated with a **raw-signals block** (`_analytics_lines`, fed by the scan's `analytics_map`): the underlying's Technicals (RSI, regime, position vs SMA-50/200, ATR/price), Fundamentals (P/E, D/E, FCF, dividend yield + safety, ex-div date), and IV microstructure (current IV, HV30, IV-percentile, IV/RV, term-structure shape, put/call skew) — the signals that were previously collapsed into the opaque ScoreCard `Tech=`/`Fund=`/`IV=` composites, so the model can explain *why* a name is favourable. **Task 10 — deterministic FACTS + rubric:** `_candidate_facts(c, spot, *, gate_passed=True)` computes a numbered `F1`-`F7` block per candidate entirely in Python (moneyness/OTM-ITM wording, breakeven cushion, earnings-vs-expiry timing, credit vs the fair-value floor, an IV-rank bucket that states "unavailable" rather than inventing one when `iv_rank is None` — consistent with Task 9's neutral-score treatment, IV/RV read, and the gate-pass line) so the model reads facts instead of deriving — and sometimes misreading — moneyness or earnings timing itself; a `=== DECISION RUBRIC ===` block (inserted before `=== YOUR TASK ===`) tells the model gate-passing candidates default to `sell` unless it can cite a specific `F#`/`N#` id, and to put those ids in the new `evidence` schema field. **Fix round 1:** `F7` ("Passed every deterministic gate") and the rubric's "already PASSED the Rules Engine" opening line are both **omitted** when `single_ticker=True` (`build_prompt` passes `gate_passed=not single_ticker`) — the single-ticker deep-dive can review a near-miss that failed every gate (`scan.py`'s `cc_near_miss`/`csp_near_miss` fallback, sometimes alongside a genuinely passed candidate in the same call), so asserting a pass there was a false premise; the single-ticker rubric instead opens with a neutral "judge them on the FACTS and NEWS... rather than assuming a pass" line, keeping the same sell/wait/skip definitions and evidence instruction. `summary` (a short 2-3 sentence synthesis) is now requested on **every** path, not just the single-ticker deep-dive, which keeps its longer SUMMARY GUIDE variant. The output contract changed to `{"reviews": [...]}` (was a bare JSON array) — the `=== YOUR TASK ===` line was also corrected to say so (fix round 1: it previously still said "return a JSON array" while the closing instruction already said `{"reviews": [...]}`). Enrichment only; never reaches the engine. The intro framing (a separate, pre-existing sentence above the FACTS/rubric blocks) is **gate-neutral for the single-ticker deep-dive** (which may review near-misses) and asserts gate-approval only on the full-universe path), `roll.py` (roll alerts), `eod.py` (EOD journal) |

**Important:** Claude is enrichment only. If it is unavailable or returns bad output, the system
sends the Rules-Engine-approved list to Telegram without Claude commentary. Claude never places,
sizes, or gates orders.

---

### `src/claude/eval/` — The verdict learning loop

Turns Claude's reviews into labeled history and scores them. Read-only with respect to trading:
nothing here can place, size, or gate an order — it only observes and measures.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `ledger.py` | The **outcome ledger**. Maps `VerdictLedgerRow` ↔ `VerdictRecord` so the rest of the loop works in schemas, not ORM. At scan time it stores one row per surfaced candidate: the full signal vector Claude saw, its verdict, and the deterministic baseline. Upserts on `candidate_id` and never clobbers a recorded outcome. **Once an order exists for the candidate** (`_candidate_is_committed`), the pre-outcome signal vector is frozen too (N2b), so the row that receives the realized outcome carries the signals of the scan that produced the fill — not a later re-scan's. |
| `baseline.py` | The **deterministic counterfactual** — what the engine would do without Claude. Pure Python over the engine's own slate: every surfaced candidate is a `sell` ranked by `blended_score`; anything not surfaced is `skip`. No LLM, no new tunables. |
| `reconcile.py` | The **close reconciler**. Deterministic, DB-only: joins the ledger against fills/orders/approvals to set the terminal outcome (`expired_worthless` / `closed_early` / `assigned` / `not_filled` / `user_rejected` / `risk_rejected`) and realized P&L. Runs automatically at EOD and via `scripts.reconcile_outcomes`. **Since P3-P4 M4 Task 4.1, the accounting rule itself — `fill_economics` and `classify_outcome` — lives in `src/reporting/legs.py`; this module imports it from there** so the reconciler and the reporting layer share one implementation by construction (see `src/reporting/` below for the dependency inversion the fence allows). |
| `assignment.py` | **Assignment auto-detection** (Phase 4): `detect_assignments` is a pure function that diffs the prior day's position snapshot against current positions — a vanished short whose underlying stock moved ~100×contracts in the assignment direction (puts → shares appear, calls → shares called away) is `assigned`, not `expired_worthless`. `assigned_candidate_ids` is the DB-backed orchestration the EOD run feeds into `reconcile(assigned_candidate_ids=…)`, replacing the manual `--assigned` flag; `assigned_shorts` (**Phase 6**) returns the same detections as full `OpenShort` records (carrying `underlying`/`strike`/`right`) so the EOD run can pass the assignment price straight into `mark_campaign_assigned` for C6 cost basis. Enrichment-input only — never touches the engine or sizing. |
| `score_metrics.py` | **Score-vs-outcome analysis** (N22): over closed ledger rows, buckets `blended_score` (and each scorecard component) by realized win rate / mean P&L, plus a per-signal Pearson correlation. Read-only evidence for whether the *human-edited* `scoring_weights.yaml` is earning its keep — it never feeds the engine (the fence). Driven by `scripts/evaluate_scores.py`. |

---

### `src/execution/` — Order placement

Handles everything between your Telegram approval and the order reaching IBKR.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `order_builder.py` | Builds the IBKR order objects (never market orders). `build_limit_order`: a single-leg mid-price SELL, rounded to the penny-pilot tick ($0.01 below $3.00, $0.05 at/above). `build_combo_roll_order`: a two-leg **BAG combo** (BUY-to-close old short + SELL-to-open new short) priced at the net — a credit roll is a *negative* net-debit limit (`lmtPrice = -net_credit`). `reprice_limit`: pure chase-price helper — steps a resting limit a fraction toward the bid (SELL) or ask (BUY), tick-rounded and guarded by a floor/ceiling. |
| `executor.py` | Places single-leg (entry SELL) orders via IBKR, monitors for a fill, handles timeouts and cancellations, records the fill (with entry IV) and the `filled` learning-loop outcome. A `ROLL` candidate is delegated to `roll_executor.execute_roll`. When `execution.reprice_enabled` is set, the fill-wait loop chases an unfilled SELL down toward the bid (via `reprice_limit`) up to `max_reprices` times — never below `min_live_premium_ratio × approved premium` — before cancelling on timeout. Each chase step **re-fetches the live bid/ask** (`_refetch_bid_ask`, N11). `_refetch_bid_ask` and `_as_float` are shared helpers also imported by `position_manager` and `roll_executor` for their own reprice loops. |
| `roll_pipeline.py` | **Wires rolls end-to-end** (N20): `queue_roll_for_approval` takes a triggered short + a fresh chain, calls `generate_roll_candidates(..., defensive=True)` — every roll this pipeline generates originates from a monitor trigger, so it is judged on risk reduction, not yield (D4) — persists the best as a `CandidateRow`, and raises a PENDING `ApprovalRow` (frozen snapshot, N2a). Approve then creates a QUEUED ROLL `OrderRow` → `process_queued_orders` → `execute_candidate` → `execute_roll`. Called from the monitor's `fire_alerts` only when `monitor.roll_execution_enabled` is set (default OFF, pending live BAG-sign verification); otherwise rolls stay alert-only. `validate_candidates` treats ROLL as exposure-neutral so the queue re-gate doesn't double-count the replaced leg. Since M5 Task 5.1 the module also owns `fetch_roll_inputs(ib, position)` — the single async chain-fetch path for roll pricing, shared by the monitor's `_try_queue_roll` and the command drain's `roll_request` handler (the milestone is explicit: no second chain-fetch path). The `queue_roll_for_approval` half stays deterministic and synchronous; `fetch_roll_inputs` is the composed fetch (chain + IV + technicals, the latter two offloaded to the default executor). |
| `promote_pipeline.py` | **Wires the web console's promote command end-to-end** (P2 M4 Task 4.2): `queue_promoted_for_approval` takes a `TradeCandidate` the promote drain handler (`src/notify/command_drain.py`'s `_promote`) has already re-priced and re-gated via a fresh single-ticker run — never a strategy generator itself, unlike `roll_pipeline` — persists it as a `CandidateRow`, and raises a PENDING `ApprovalRow` (frozen snapshot, N2a), exactly mirroring `roll_pipeline.queue_roll_for_approval`'s `has_active_order` guard and upsert pattern, so a replayed drain cannot raise a second *order* once the first approval has been turned into one — `has_active_order` checks for an `OrderRow`, not an `ApprovalRow`, so a narrower crash-window race is still possible: a replay landing after the first approval is raised but before the drain marks its command `applied` (no `OrderRow` exists yet) could still raise a second PENDING approval for the same candidate. Pre-existing in the `roll_pipeline` pattern this mirrors, not new here, and not fixed in this pass. The handler itself re-runs `src.orchestrator.scan._price_and_gate_ticker` — the same chain fetch/analytics/CC-CSP-screens/scoring/`validate_candidates` path a `/scan TICKER` uses — so nothing from the stored `RiskVerdictRow` that made the candidate promotable (M4 Task 4.1's API-boundary guard) reaches the approval except the (symbol, strategy, strike, expiry) used to find the matching fresh candidate; every number on the approval comes from the fresh run. A promote that no longer prices, no longer clears the gate, or falls below the score floor fails the command with a machine-readable reason (`docs/web/commands.md`'s promote failure-reason table) instead of raising an approval. |
| `roll_executor.py` | Executes a `Strategy.ROLL` candidate as one atomic BAG combo. Resolves the existing short to buy back from live positions, fetches fresh quotes + qualified conIds for both legs, re-gates the new leg via `validate_live_quote` plus a **net-credit floor** (`min_live_premium_ratio`), requires a LIVE-mode [CONFIRM LIVE] tap, places the combo, cancels on timeout, and records two FillRows (BUY under the original short's `candidate_id`; SELL under the new id). **C5:** when `execution.reprice_enabled` is set, the fill-wait loop re-fetches per-leg bid/ask, recomputes the live net credit, and steps the BAG net-limit toward market (ceiling: `min_live_premium_ratio × approved credit`). The combo sign convention is mock-tested only and is on the STATUS.md live-verification list. |
| `approval.py` | Picks up QUEUED orders → re-validates against the Rules Engine (batched, cumulative-aware) → hands off to executor. `_load_candidate` executes the **frozen approved snapshot** on the OrderRow (N2a), never the latest `CandidateRow` payload, so a 15-min re-scan that mutated `contracts`/`premium` between approval and execution can't change the size the human approved (legacy snapshot-less rows fall back to `CandidateRow`). Phase 1 recomputes DTE from the stored expiry AND (fixed 2026-09-11) re-fetches `next_earnings` via `analytics.fundamentals.get_fundamental_stats` before the Rules Engine pass, so a Friday-approved trade re-validated Monday morning judges the earnings-blackout check (inside `validate_candidates`) against a fresh date, not the scan-time snapshot's frozen one — `get_fundamental_stats`'s own earnings-aware TTL cache (1 day when a known date is within ±2 weeks, else 30) keeps this a DB read in the common case rather than a yfinance round-trip on every ~30s poll. **Share-ownership re-check (fixed 2026-09-11):** immediately after the Rules Engine verdict, a `Strategy.COVERED_CALL` candidate is checked against `covered_call.uncovered_call_capacity(positions, underlying)` — the same fresh position snapshot already fetched for this pass, no extra IBKR round-trip — and cancelled with `insufficient_shares_at_execution` if the book no longer covers it (shares sold, manually or via an assignment the reconciler hasn't caught yet, between scan and this poll). Covered calls are never grouped/deduped by the Rules Engine, so a per-underlying `cc_shares_committed` running tally (greedy, mirroring `capital.py`'s budget consumption) makes a second QUEUED call on the same name see what an earlier one in the same pass already claimed, not just the static snapshot. |
| `profit_take.py` | **Exit orchestration** (extracted from the notify layer, N23): loads short positions, computes entry credit (`net_entry_credit_per_share`, qty-weighted SELL fills less commission), and checks two independent exit triggers: `check_profit_takes` at the 50% threshold, and `check_loss_exits` when cost-to-close reaches `max_loss_multiple` (default 2.0×) the entry credit — **leveraged ETFs only** (`common.universe.is_leveraged_etf`, 2026-10-02); every other short at that line is handled by the monitor's `loss_multiple` roll trigger rather than closed. Both share a common `_quote_short` helper (N14 — polls for a two-sided tick in 0.1s steps, returns early once a market arrives rather than burning full quote timeout per position). Both either auto-buy-to-close (via `position_manager`, when `automation.auto_close_enabled` is true) or send an alert (when false) — independent of the autonomy rung, since closing risk should never wait for a tap; `check_loss_exits` additionally needs `automation.max_loss_multiple`. Telegram sends go through the passed `bot`. |
| `position_manager.py` | Buy-to-close execution for short option positions (profit-take auto-closes). Routes the close through the same `OrderRow`/`FillRow` lifecycle and cancel-on-timeout discipline as entries, and is idempotent at the contract level (a deterministic `close:` candidate id on the `OrderRow`; `_close_blocked` treats a QUEUED/SUBMITTED close as blocking, and a FILLED/PARTIAL one as blocking only until the system sells that contract again, so a re-sale of the same strike/expiry can still be auto-closed). The BUY `FillRow` is attributed to the short it closes (`orders.open_short_candidate_id`), not to the synthetic id, so the verdict ledger and `/pnl/system` see the debit (2026-10-07 fix). An error **after** `placeOrder` (e.g. the Gateway dropping mid-reprice) leaves the order SUBMITTED for `reconcile_orphan_fills` to resolve, instead of the old false REJECTED. **C5:** when `execution.reprice_enabled` is set, the fill-wait loop chases an unfilled BUY-to-close upward toward the ask (no ceiling — profit-takes want to get filled). Buy-to-close is risk-reducing, so it skips the income Rules Engine gate. |
| `circuit_breakers.py` | Automated-trading safety breakers (the risk gate bounds *exposure*; these bound *activity + losses*). `remaining_entry_allowance` enforces `max_auto_trades_per_day`. Two loss breakers feed the kill switch (D3): `mark_based_loss` compares today's summed position `unrealized_pnl` (via `storage.positions.load_latest_position_snapshot`) against the prior day's snapshot and trips on `automation.daily_loss_halt_pct` — a genuine mark-to-market measure, unlike the older `daily_loss_breached`/`realized_cashflow_today` (kept only for its own tests, no longer wired in), which summed FillRow credits/debits and so read a real drawdown as a *profit* on a day the system sold premium. `drawdown_breached` compares live net liquidation against a trailing high-water mark (`storage.system_settings.get_high_water_mark`/`set_high_water_mark`, advanced on every new high) and trips on `automation.drawdown_halt_pct` — this catches a slow bleed no single day's loss trips. Both are read-only except for tripping the persisted halt or advancing the high-water mark — never feed the risk engine, scoring, or sizing. Consumed by `process_queued_orders`, which halts on whichever of the two trips first. |
| `reconciliation.py` | Broker ↔ DB recovery, extracted from the notify layer. `recover_orphan_orders` resets SUBMITTED rows with no `ib_order_id` (crash before `placeOrder`) back to QUEUED; `reconcile_orphan_fills` back-fills FillRows for orders whose fill event was lost, matching `reqExecutions` by broker order id then contract — covering SUBMITTED rows **and** REJECTED/CANCELLED rows that still carry an `ib_order_id` (N8: the executor's except path marks an order REJECTED when its monitor loop throws, but the SELL may already have filled; pre-placement cancels have no `ib_order_id` and are ignored); for the system's own buy-to-closes (`close:` ids, which have no `CandidateRow`) it matches a BOT execution on broker order id **and** contract (order ids restart after a Gateway restart), records the BUY fill on the short it closed, and marks a close still SUBMITTED from an earlier ET day with no execution CANCELLED (closes are DAY orders) so it stops blocking the next close (2026-10-07 fix); `reconcile_external_closes` records *manual* buy-to-closes done in IB Gateway as BUY FillRows attributed to the original short's `candidate_id` (idempotent on IBKR `execId`), so the verdict ledger labels the position `closed_early` rather than `expired_worthless` and EOD cashflow includes the debit (SYSTEM_REVIEW F7). All three are strictly additive (record only proven broker state; never cancel/resubmit) and run at startup **and** periodically from the intraday loop, so fills/closes landing mid-session are recovered on the next cycle, not only on the next restart. |

---

### `src/notify/` — Telegram messaging

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `sender.py` | Stateless message sender: sends text and formatted messages to your Telegram (used by the scan pipeline and EOD report). **Per-thread routing:** each send function takes a caller-provided `thread_id` int (or None) so CC candidates, CSP candidates, buy-to-own, and account snapshots each land in their own Telegram forum topic. `thread_id(raw: str) -> int | None` parses config strings. **Always-send-something rule:** an empty candidate list appends a plain-text diagnostic to the thread's persisted status message rather than being silently dropped (troubleshooting signal). **Assessed-contract block:** `send_candidates` takes an optional pre-rendered `assessed_text` (from `formatters.format_assessed_contracts`) and sends it as its own message after the screen — on both the empty and the full-send path — via the best-effort `_send_assessed_block`, whose failure never changes the screen's return value. Only manual `/scan` and full sweeps pass it; the 15-min intraday loop leaves it `None` and keeps the compact one-line near-miss digest, so it cannot reintroduce the per-cycle message flood S6 removed. **Accumulated status messages (`_append_status`):** "no candidates" and "unchanged" cycles append a `[HH:MM] …` timestamped block to a single persisted Telegram message (edited in-place) so repeated quiet cycles don't spam new messages — the thread accumulates a timestamped log rather than filling up. Cycles are separated by a blank line so each run reads as its own block (a block can span several rows: the header plus a near-miss "closest" line). Body text is stored in `{status_msg_key}_body` in `system_settings`; when the body exceeds 3800 chars the slate is wiped and a fresh message is sent. The same rollover also happens on the first quiet cycle of a new ET calendar day regardless of body length (`{status_msg_key}_date`, 2026-08-28) — so a day boundary always starts a new Telegram message even if yesterday's had room left, keeping each day's cycles visually separated in the thread; every quiet cycle after that first one, same day, keeps editing in place as before. A full candidate/buy-list send clears both the id key and the body key so the next quiet cycle starts fresh. **Output-frequency suppression (S6):** `send_candidates` takes required kwargs `thread_id`, `label`, `icon`, `hash_key`, `time_key` plus optional `empty_reason`/`noun`/`suppress_unchanged`/`near_misses`/`near_miss_more`/`assessed_text`. When the candidate list is empty and `near_misses` (a list of `(TradeCandidate, reason_codes)` tuples, already capped) is supplied, the diagnostic appends one plain-text `format_near_miss_line` per entry naming the closest contracts that failed the gate and why, then a trailing "↳ …and N more that didn't pass" when `near_miss_more > 0` — so even a quiet 15-min cycle names the top near-miss trades and signals how many further rejects were truncated. When `suppress_unchanged=True` it first checks the screen-level content hash (`_screen_hash` over `(candidate_id, score-band)` tuples, stored in `system_settings` under `hash_key`/`time_key`); if the hash matches and every candidate already has a live same-band PENDING approval, a compact line is appended to the status message. `send_buy_list` routes to `telegram_thread_buy` and uses `_screen_hash` over `(symbol, score-band)`, stored in `last_buy_list_hash`/`last_buy_list_time`. **`send_account_snapshot(account, positions)`** sends an account snapshot to `telegram_thread_account`: on the first call each ET calendar day it sends a new message and stores the `message_id` in `account_snapshot_message_id` and the date in `account_snapshot_date`; on subsequent same-day calls it edits that message in place (updating the "last updated HH:MM" footer), so the thread stays tidy. If the stored message can't be edited (deleted, >48h) it falls back to a fresh send. Best-effort: never raises. **`send_order_notification(status, *, candidate, order_id, …)`** sends or edits a plain-text order-status message to `telegram_thread_account` (topic 58); each order gets one persistent message keyed by `order_notification_{order_id}_msg_id` that is edited as the order progresses through `"placed"` → `"update"` → `"filled"` / `"failed"`. Called from `executor.py` and `approval.py`; order-price updates (`"update"`) are pushed to each SUBMITTED order after every intraday scan via `_update_pending_order_notifications` in `approval_service.py`. |
| `approval_service.py` | The long-running daemon: runs the Telegram polling loop, handles Approve/Reject callbacks, drives order execution, serves all interactive commands, and runs the 15-minute intraday scan + profit-take loop during RTH. The profit-take *trading logic* now lives in `execution/profit_take.py` (N23) — the daemon just drives the loop and re-exports it. **The scan itself runs as its own task, not inline (2026-08-13):** `_intraday_scan_loop` spawns `_run_intraday_scan` via `asyncio.create_task` (guarded by the same `bot_data["scan_running"]` lease manual `/scan` already checks) instead of `await`ing it directly — a full, materiality-gated-off sweep (e.g. right after a restart, before any symbol has a `scan_state` baseline) can legitimately run past 15 minutes on a perfectly healthy connection, and awaiting it inline meant the loop could not return to `asyncio.sleep` for the *next* aligned mark until it finished: every later mark's profit-take/loss-exit checks and skip notifications were silently never attempted, with no log line (the 2026-08-13 01:15–01:34 ET stall, distinct from the half-dead-socket circuit breaker below, which only helps when the connection is actually dead). The loop itself still runs its per-mark checks (profit-takes, loss-exits, reconciliation, the health probe) synchronously and quickly; only the scan's own execution and result-handling are decoupled. **Guaranteed full sweep at start (2026-08-21):** `market_data.force_full_scan_minutes`'s staleness timer only *incidentally* covers a restart, since the overnight/multi-day gap since the last fetch almost always exceeds it — that's not a guarantee, and a same-day restart shortly after a full sweep would otherwise fall straight into a narrow materiality-gated cycle with no fresh full picture since the restart. So the loop separately tracks `bot_data["startup_full_sweep_done"]` (in-memory, unset on every process start) and passes `force_full_sweep=True` into `run_scan`/`_compute_material_symbols` on the first cycle it actually gets to spawn a scan — a cycle that `continue`s earlier (halted, past `entry_cutoff`, unhealthy data-farm probe, or a still-running previous scan) leaves the flag unset, so the *next* eligible cycle forces it instead; the flag is set only at the point a scan is actually spawned, mirroring the `scan_running` lease pattern. A manual `/scan` (`handle_scan_command`) already sweeps every symbol and sets the same `bot_data` flag, so a full sweep run manually right after a restart isn't redundantly repeated by the next intraday cycle. **Buy-to-own sent once per day, not every cycle (2026-08-28):** the buy-to-own screen is still scored every intraday cycle (cheap — it reuses analytics already fetched for CC/CSP) but the loop only forwards `include_buy_list=True` into `run_scan` on the first cycle each ET calendar day that actually completes; `_run_intraday_scan` records that date in the `buy_list_gate_last_sent_date` system setting only after a cycle finishes cleanly (neither `lease_skipped` nor `aborted_unhealthy`), so a failed/skipped cycle leaves the gate open for the next one instead of silently losing the day. Manual `/scan` always leaves it `True`. **Overrun observability (S9):** when an intraday cycle is lost to an overrun (the prior scan still running) or scan-lease contention, `_note_intraday_skip` increments a per-session skipped counter and sends a throttled (≤ once/30 min) Telegram warning (routed to `telegram_thread_scan`); successful cycles increment a run counter. Both counters live in `bot_data` and are surfaced in `/status`. A manual `/scan` truncated mid-run by the half-dead-socket circuit breaker (`ScanResult.aborted_unhealthy`) is reported the same way as the intraday loop's own abort — `_notify_manual_scan_outcome` edits the `/scan` progress message with the processed/total symbol count and forces a reconnect — instead of silently sending a partial results screen that looks like a complete sweep. A pre-scan health-probe failure sends a 🛑 *Scan blocked* message carrying the probe's **diagnosis** — root cause classified from the IBKR error codes observed during the probe window (1100 lost-farm vs 10197 competing-live-session vs no-subscription vs flap vs generic), plus the matching action hint — rather than the pre-2026-09-09 hardcoded "Error 1100" label, which mislabelled a real 10197 competing-session block on 2026-09-08 (a case where a reconnect fixes nothing; the hint tells the operator to close the other login or use a second username). The scan-started ping (`🔄 Scan started · HH:MM ET`) and startup notification also route to `telegram_thread_scan`. **Premarket snapshot loop:** `_premarket_snapshot_loop` is a background task (started alongside the intraday loop when `ib_scan` is available) that sleeps until 09:00 ET each trading day, then calls `send_account_snapshot` to post the day's first account snapshot to `telegram_thread_account` (topic 58) before market open; subsequent same-day intraday-loop calls edit that message in place. **Market holiday loop:** `_market_holiday_loop` is an always-on background task (no IBKR connection required) that fires at 9:30 ET on NYSE full-day holidays — or immediately on startup if the service starts after 9:30 ET on a holiday — and sends a `format_market_holiday` notification to `telegram_thread_scan` explaining that no scans will run and naming the next trading session. Weekends are not notified. **Order poll loop (Task 8, 2026-09-29, R6):** `_order_poll_loop` — the background task that calls `process_queued_orders` on `execution.poll_interval_seconds` — now also calls `storage/approvals.py::expire_stale_approvals()` (default `force=False`) at the top of every cycle, before `process_queued_orders`, so a `pending` card nobody tapped still expires once its own `expires_at`/TTL passes instead of sitting forever (see `storage/approvals.py` above); a fresh, still-in-TTL card is left alone on this path. `/expire` (`handle_expire_command`) reuses the same helper with `force=True`, which drops the TTL filter entirely and flips every `pending` row — keeping `/expire`'s documented "clears every pending approval" contract (Telegram command menu, `/help`, README, SETUP) rather than narrowing it to the poll loop's staleness-only sweep; `decided_at` is stamped with the real current time in both cases, never a fabricated future one, so `force=True` can't corrupt the timestamp the web /pnl approvals view reads. **Live-autonomy startup check (Task 12 fix round 1):** the very first line of `_run_service`, before any IBKR connection or background loop, calls `system_settings.enforce_live_autonomy_evidence()` — demoting a stored rung above MANUAL to MANUAL if this is a live process and the fill/fill-rate/close evidence for it doesn't hold, so the intraday loop and order-poll loop below can never act on a rung reached via the paper-only bypass (or an earlier promotion) that simply carried over in the shared DB; a demotion also sends a best-effort `format_autonomy_demotion` Telegram notice right after the regular startup notification. |
| `formatters.py` | Converts all data types into MarkdownV2 messages. Covers: trade candidates (with VRP and **C1: IV/RV ratio** displayed on the approval card, plus a `💡` premium-environment read from `_premium_read(iv_rank, vrp)` — "IV rank low — poor premium · VRP negative — options cheap vs realized" — so the 15-min CC/CSP cards interpret the IV environment, not just print numbers), positions, account, health, status, startup, EOD report, fill confirmations (`format_fill_confirm`), roll combo fill confirmations (`format_roll_fill_confirm`), live-order confirmation requests (`format_live_confirm_request`), roll alerts (`format_roll_alert` — trigger codes are rendered through `_humanize_trigger`, which since M5 Task 5.3 is an alias for `src.monitor.triggers.humanize_trigger` (the mapping moved there so the Telegram formatter and the web API share the one copy), so a card reads "Triggers: delta drift + nearing expiry" rather than `delta_drift + dte`; the alert bodies themselves are prose written by `monitor/triggers.py`, not debug output), **C4: assignment-risk alerts** (`format_assignment_alert` — frames decision as roll / close / let-assign, dispatched by `fire_alerts` when the only trigger is `assignment_risk`), pending approvals list (`format_pending_approvals`), fills history (`format_fills_history`), autonomy-rung status (`format_mode_status(level, *, fills, fill_rate, closed_once, blockers)` — the current rung + description, and, unless already at FULL, the next rung's promotion evidence: blockers from `promotion_blockers` when unmet, or an "eligible — send /autonomy LEVEL" line when clear, always trailed by the raw fills/fill-rate/closed-once counts), **`format_autonomy_demotion(old_level)`** (Task 12 fix round 1 — the best-effort startup notice sent when `system_settings.enforce_live_autonomy_evidence` demotes a live process's stored rung to MANUAL; states what was demoted and why in one paragraph, and points at the server log for the specific unmet criteria rather than repeating them, keeping the message's job to "make the demotion itself impossible to miss"), auto-trade summary (`format_auto_trade_notification` — per trade: score/VRP, then contracts × premium/sh, total credit, ROC and annualized yield), profit alerts (`format_profit_alert`), auto-close results (`format_auto_close_result`) — both take the contract's *parts* (`underlying`, `strike`, `right`, `expiry`) rather than the position's OCC symbol and render it through **`contract_label(underlying, strike, right, expiry)`** → `AAPL $245C Aug 18`, the one place a contract is named for a human; these two alerts fire on live positions and used to be the only cards printing the raw `AAPL  260818C00245000`, market holiday notification (`format_market_holiday(name, next_open_date)` — "🔴 Market Holiday — {name}" card with next trading session date, sent at 9:30 ET on NYSE full-day holidays), buy-to-own screen (`format_buy_list` — candidates grouped by sector (up to 10 total, capped via `scoring_weights.yaml → buy_to_own.max_candidates`); each sector block has a visible header with icon + count and its candidate cards wrapped in a Telegram spoiler (`||…||`) that collapses until tapped; each card shows price, IV-rank/IV-vs-HV/VRP premium richness, trend + RSI, estimated monthly CC yield, earnings/dividend notes, and a deterministic rationale; sector icons/labels are driven by `universe.yaml → sectors` via the `BuyCandidate.sector` field), and the S6 suppression digests (`format_unchanged_cards_digest` — a compact list of still-pending unchanged CC/CSP candidates with "since HH:MM"). `format_status` takes optional `scans_run`/`scans_skipped` counters (S9) and renders a 🔁 run · ⚠️ skipped line when provided. `format_data_provenance` — the end-of-scan "📊 Data sources this scan" summary sent after a full sweep (`/scan`): option-chain fetch counts (IBKR ok/failed/skipped), spot-price source split (IBKR chain parity vs. yfinance vs. unavailable), VIX availability, and the IBKR-vs-Black-Scholes split of option Greeks. **Telegram multi-thread routing formatters:** `format_screen_unchanged(icon, label, count, since, noun)` — generic "N {noun}(s) unchanged since HH:MM" digest, used by the buy-to-own, CC, and CSP per-thread screens (replaces the old `format_buy_list_digest`); `format_account_snapshot(account, positions, updated_at)` — the per-thread account snapshot: net liq + total unrealized P&L $/%, a *Stocks* section (each holding with any covered calls sold against it nested below via `└ `), and a *Cash-Secured Puts* section for standalone short puts (omitted if none); each position shows its unrealized P&L % (`unrealized_pnl / abs(avg_cost * position * multiplier)`, multiplier=100 for options, "N/A" if cost basis is zero), with a "Source: IBKR · last updated HH:MM" footer. **Per-message data-source footers:** every trading-data message now ends with a `_Sources: …_` line identifying which systems contributed: CC/CSP candidate cards use `_candidate_sources(c)` (derives "IBKR option chain" always, plus "yfinance spot/Greeks (fallback)" when `c.price_source == "yfinance"` or `c.greeks_source != "ibkr"`); buy-to-own always shows "yfinance (prices · IV · technicals · fundamentals) · IBKR (IV rank history)"; account snapshot shows "Source: IBKR"; auto-trade notifications aggregate sources across all queued candidates; roll alerts and EOD summaries show "Source: IBKR";  **EOD report layout (`format_eod_summary`):** the numeric block is rendered inside a ``` code fence ``` so columns actually align in Telegram's proportional font (the old manual space-padding did not) and needs no per-char escaping — it shows premium cashflow, unrealized P&L with the day's Δ as both $ and **% of NLV**, position count + net Δ, and an NLV/BP line; a "Drivers:" line names the top movers with their P&L; the "premium cashflow = … excludes assignment P&L" disclaimer is shown **only** when there was cashflow or fills (suppressed on flat days); the watchlist prints in full only when it changed, otherwise collapses to "N names (unchanged)"; the narrative is interpretation only (the prompt forbids restating the figures); **Single-ticker formatter:** `format_ticker_scan_result(ticker, iv_stats, tech_stats, fund_stats, cc_candidates, csp_candidates, buy_candidate, is_held, quotes_available, reviews, cc_reject_reasons, csp_reject_reasons, cc_near_miss, csp_near_miss, greeks_fallback, market_conditions, sector_context)` — compact summary card for the `/scan AAPL` command: price/IV-rank/VRP header followed by a `💡` premium-environment read (`_premium_read` turns IV rank + VRP into plain English, e.g. "IV rank low — poor premium · VRP negative — options cheap vs realized"), technicals (SMAs, RSI, earnings DTE), then a deterministic **"🌐 Market & Sector"** block (`_market_sector_lines` — VIX regime via `_vix_regime`, sector/SPY/ticker 1-month returns, and relative strength; omitted entirely when neither VIX nor any sector datum is available) and a **"🧠 Read"** block (the LLM `ClaudeReview.summary` plain-English synthesis, rendered once from the first review that has one), then best CC candidate (or "not held" / "no qualifying options"), best CSP candidate, and buy-to-own score+rationale if a candidate scored. When `reviews` (Claude/Ollama `ClaudeReview`s, matched by `candidate_id`) are supplied, the best CC/CSP card renders a `🤖` verdict line (recommendation + confidence + why/risks + a `📌` assignment-considerations line). When a strategy has no qualifying option, `cc_near_miss`/`csp_near_miss` (the best-scoring contract that *failed* the gate) render a "_Closest contract (did not qualify)_" block (`_near_miss_lines`) showing the strike/premium/score marked `✗`, followed by a `_Rejected: …_` line that humanizes the top 3 distinct `cc_reject_reasons`/`csp_reject_reasons` codes (e.g. `iv_rank_below_minimum` → "IV rank too low (poor premium)") via `_humanize_reject_reason`/`_reject_reason_line`; with no near-miss it falls back to just the reason line — so an empty result always explains *why* rather than going silent. `_near_miss_lines` also appends `_ideal_lines(cand)` beneath the near-miss's own strike/score row — the rejected contract's `.ideal` zone (strike band + fair-value credit floor), so a `premium_below_fair_value` rejection shows what fair value actually was rather than just the code. **Hypothetical fair value (no contract priced at all):** when a strategy has *neither* a qualifying candidate *nor* a near-miss — the symbol never reached a single quote, e.g. a CSP on a name outside the `would_own` allowlist (a leveraged ETF like `SOXL`) or a CC with no shares held — `cc_hypothetical`/`csp_hypothetical` (an `IdealZone` computed by `run_ticker_scan` straight from `tech_stats`/`iv_stats`/`fund_stats` via `fair_value.compute_ideal_zone`, no option chain needed, priced at the strategy's configured mid-DTE) render through `_hypothetical_zone_lines`: the same 🎯 ideal-strike/💰 fair-credit numbers, always prefixed "_Informational fair value — not a recommendation:_" so it can never read as an endorsement. When the CSP skip was `not_in_would_own`, `csp_skip_reason` additionally prints a one-line note naming the allowlist exclusion. The "🎯 Levels" (`buy_below`/`action_price`) block falls back to `csp_hypothetical or cc_hypothetical` when no real candidate ever populated it, so the share-entry level still surfaces for an off-list or not-held symbol. Purely display — neither hypothetical zone is scored, gated, or written to the allowlist. The footer (`_ticker_sources`) is honest about provenance: it lists "IBKR option chain" only when quotes were available, the actual spot source ("IBKR spot (parity)" vs "yfinance spot") from `tech_stats.price_source`, "yfinance Greeks (fallback)" when `greeks_fallback`, and "Claude/Ollama review" when a verdict was rendered. **Order notification formatter:** `format_order_notification(status, *, underlying, strategy, strike, right, expiry, contracts, order_id, action, limit_price, underlying_price, option_mid, filled_qty, avg_price, failure_reason, updated_at)` — plain-text (no MarkdownV2) order-status message for thread 58; status is one of `"placed"`, `"update"`, `"filled"`, or `"failed"`; `action` is `"SELL"` (open) or `"BUY"` (close): SELL filled shows "📈 Opened", BUY filled shows "📈 Closed" (C8 richer trade-feed cards). **C7 skip-reasons formatter:** `format_skip_reasons(per_symbol_skip, *, max_symbols)` — MarkdownV2 "⏸️ Skipped symbols" card showing the top rejection reason(s) per symbol that was scanned but produced no approved candidate; sorted alphabetically, max 2 reasons shown inline, rest as "+N more". Sent once per full `/scan` sweep in the scan thread. **Near-miss line:** `format_near_miss_line(cand, reasons)` — a **plain-text** (not MarkdownV2) one-liner naming a contract that *failed* the gate this cycle ("↳ closest: NVDA $200P Jul18 (30d) $3.00/sh · ROC 1.5% · score 60 — annualized yield below floor"). Called once per near-miss by `send_candidates`' empty-screen diagnostic (which sends plain text via `_append_status`) for the top `_NEAR_MISS_LIMIT` (1) reject, followed by a "↳ …and N more that didn't pass" line, so even a 15-min cycle where nothing qualified names the single closest trade and why; reason codes are humanized via the shared `_humanize_reject_reason`. **Ideal-zone + assessed-contract formatters:** `_ideal_lines(cand, indent)` renders the deterministic ideal zone beside the strike actually on offer — 🎯 the ideal strike band with an `✓ in zone` / `outside` verdict, 💰 the minimum credit worth accepting against the live premium (`below fair value` / `clears fair value`), the zone's human-readable drivers ("resistance $192.00 · 35% vol → 1σ ±$12.50"), and 📍 the action level. It returns `[]` when no zone was derivable, so cards degrade to their previous shape rather than printing empty scaffolding. Wired into both `format_candidate` (the approval card) and the CC/CSP blocks of `format_ticker_scan_result`. `format_assessed_contracts(assessed, *, strategy, max_rows, title)` renders the **"assessed but not approved"** listing: one `✗ SYMBOL $STRIKE·EXP (Nd) · $premium · Δ` row per contract, its ideal band beneath, and the humanized reasons it was set aside, truncating to `…and N more assessed`. **Share-entry fallback (D1 follow-on):** when a contract's reasons include a cash-affordability code (`insufficient_cash`, `no_headroom`, `buying_power_buffer`) and it carries an `IdealZone` with a `buy_below` level, a `↳ share entry level $X` line follows the reason line — a CSP the account can't secure still has a nameable alternate route to the same exposure (buying shares outright) instead of a bare rejection. Expects the list pre-ranked by `scan._rank_assessed`. Sent as its own message by `send_candidates` on manual `/scan` and full sweeps only (never intraday — see `sender.py`), and rendered inline on the `/scan TICKER` card as "Other contracts considered". **C8 P&L calendar formatter:** `format_pnl_calendar(rows, *, days)` — MarkdownV2 per-day cashflow table; each row has `date`, `cashflow` (signed net premium: credits − debits), `fills` (count). Exposed via `/calendar` Telegram command. **C6 wheel-campaign formatter:** `format_campaigns(campaigns)` — MarkdownV2 card listing each symbol's campaign: status (open/closed), leg count, total premium collected, net premium, adjusted cost basis (when assigned), and closed date (when closed); used by `/campaigns`. **`fmt_strike(strike)`** renders a strike as listed (`235`, `13.5`) — every card, order notification and roll message uses it; `{strike:.0f}` used to show a $13.50 strike as "$14". |

| `command_drain.py` | (P2-M1) **The command-drain loop's handlers and ``drain_once`` entry point.** Runs inside ``approval_service`` (the process that holds the exec connection). ``drain_once(ib, bot, chat_id)`` reads all ``pending`` ``AppCommandRow``s, expires stale ones past ``approval.ttl_minutes``, and applies each through its registered handler. ``HANDLERS`` is a dict populated by ``register(kind)``; since M3 it carries ``approve`` and ``reject``, and since M4 ``promote`` — later milestones add theirs.
