A handler may be sync or a coroutine function — ``drain_once`` awaits the result when it is awaitable (M4's ``promote`` needs this; every earlier handler is unaffected). **Never raises:** a handler that throws marks its own command ``failed`` and the loop continues; a handler raising ``CommandFailed`` fails its own command with that machine-readable reason (rendered humanised on the web receipt); an unknown kind is ``failed`` (not left pending); a command with an outstanding ``confirm_token`` is **skipped** (not failed — it's waiting for a human); the loop runs even when ``ib is None`` so broker-free commands (reject, halt, resume, set_autonomy, universe edits) apply with TWS down. **(P2-M3) The approve/reject handlers call ``approval_service._process_button(approval_id, action)`` and reimplement nothing** — the web decide performs exactly the ``ApprovalRow`` mutation Telegram performs, in the same function, with the same N2a snapshot freeze and the same idempotency layers (spec §4.5); a test monkeypatches the drain's ``_process_button`` indirection so a future refactor cannot fork the mutation without failing the suite. An already-decided approval is **applied, not failed** — ``_process_button`` returns "Already {status}" and mutates nothing, and the losing surface (Telegram or web) reports it neutrally in ``result.decision``. Neither handler needs ``ib``: an approve with TWS down still creates a ``QUEUED`` ``OrderRow`` that ``process_queued_orders`` submits when the exec connection returns. The handlers send no Telegram message — notification stays with the existing order poll loop (two paths for one order is how duplicates start). **(P2-M4 Task 4.2) The ``promote`` handler is the belt of the belt-and-braces** behind M4 Task 4.1's API-boundary stage guard: it fails immediately with ``broker_unavailable`` when ``ib is None`` (a promote needs a fresh chain), then awaits ``src.orchestrator.scan._price_and_gate_ticker(ib, payload.symbol)`` — the same chain-fetch/analytics/CC-CSP-screens/scoring/``validate_candidates`` path a ``/scan TICKER`` uses, extracted so the promote handler pays for none of that command's Claude review or Telegram formatting — and searches its gate-passed ``scored`` list for the exact ``(strategy, strike, expiry)`` the payload names. A chain-fetch failure fails with ``chain_unavailable`` (the captured error text); a `TickerPricingAborted` — the analytics or portfolio fetch failed outright, before a chain was even screened — folds into that same ``chain_unavailable`` reason too, carrying ``{"stage": ..., "detail": ...}`` rather than inventing a fifth reason code (both mean "no fresh, honest price for this ticker" to the operator); no matching contract fails with ``contract_not_priced`` (``priced_count`` = the fresh chain size); a match whose fresh verdict is not ``pass`` fails with ``gate_rejected`` and the verdict's ``reasons`` **verbatim** — the gate changing its mind is the mechanism working, not a bug to soften; a passing match below ``weights.min_candidate_score`` fails with ``score_below_minimum``. Only on a clean pass does it call ``execution.promote_pipeline.queue_promoted_for_approval`` with the fresh candidate — never a field from the stored ``RiskVerdictRow`` — raising a PENDING approval; a ``None`` return (an order already active for this candidate) is **applied, not failed**, mirroring the approve/reject "already decided" idempotency above. **(P2-M5 Task 5.1) The ``roll_request`` handler** asks the system to *propose* a roll for one open short (two steps, never one: the operator approves the proposal through §6.1's path). It fails with ``broker_unavailable`` when ``ib is None``, resolves the position from a live ``get_positions(ib)`` read (not held → ``position_not_found``; held but not an open short option → ``not_an_open_short``), fetches the chain + IV/technical stats through ``execution.roll_pipeline.fetch_roll_inputs`` (the shared path the monitor's ``_try_queue_roll`` also uses — no second chain-fetch path), and calls ``queue_roll_for_approval`` **unchanged**. To disambiguate the pipeline's ``None`` *before* calling it (the milestone: "rather than changing the pipeline's return type"), the handler runs ``generate_roll_candidates(..., defensive=True)`` itself to derive the same candidates the pipeline will choose from — same function, same inputs, same deterministic ids — and checks ``_existing_roll_in_flight`` against **every** one of them, not just the top-ranked candidate (post-M5 verification fix, 2026-09-07: under ``defensive=True`` every candidate's ``roc_pct`` is hardcoded 0, so ``generate_roll_candidates``'s ROC-desc sort is a no-op tie and "the best candidate" is really just whichever qualifying quote the chain happened to list first — two fetches taken minutes apart can list the same set of qualifying strikes in a different order, so the monitor's earlier PENDING candidate can still be in this fetch's list without being first; checking only ``candidates[0]`` let a web request race the monitor into a second approval whenever ranking shifted between the two fetches, closed by ``tests/test_drain_roll.py::test_an_in_flight_roll_is_caught_even_when_no_longer_top_ranked``). The check covers both an active order (``active_order_for``) and an existing PENDING ``ApprovalRow`` for the matched candidate. Either → ``roll_already_working`` with ``detail.approval_id`` (so the receipt links to the in-flight proposal); the PENDING-approval check is what closes the monitor-vs-web race ``has_active_order`` alone would miss (the monitor's roll raises a PENDING approval, no ``OrderRow`` until the operator approves it) — the milestone's headline acceptance criterion ("a monitor alert and a web request produce exactly one approval") requires it. Empty/failed chain → ``chain_unavailable``; no candidates → ``no_qualifying_roll``. No roll economics leak into the handler (the token grep in ``tests/test_write_path_invariants.py`` pins it);  **(P2-M6 Task 6.1) The ``halt`` / ``resume`` / ``set_autonomy`` handlers** are the control plane, and the lightest handlers in the file: thin resolvers over the same ``system_settings`` helpers Telegram's ``/halt`` / ``/resume`` / ``/autonomy`` use (``set_halted`` / ``set_autonomy_level``), so there is exactly one halt flag and one autonomy rung, both read through ``system_settings.HALT_KEY`` / ``get_autonomy_level`` by every consumer (a token-grep test in ``tests/test_write_path_invariants.py`` pins that the ``execution_halted`` literal never appears in this file — two halt flags would be a catastrophe, one of which nothing reads). None of the three touches an order path, and all three apply with ``ib is None``: halting matters most exactly when something is wrong, and TWS being unreachable is a common shape of wrong. ``halt`` and ``resume`` are idempotent in both directions — halting an already-halted system, and resuming an un-halted one, are both ``applied`` (the operator's intent is satisfied, not an error); an empty or missing halt reason is stored as ``"halted from the web console"`` so ``/status`` and the console always say something useful; ``resume`` records who released the switch in its result (``released_by``). ``set_autonomy`` accepts only the four ``AutonomyLevel`` rungs (enforced at parse time by M1's schema; the handler never coerces) and calls ``promotion_blockers`` exactly as Telegram's ``/autonomy`` does — unmet evidence criteria fail the command with ``promotion_refused`` + the blockers in ``detail``, so the web is not the rung ladder's back door; demotion always applies. These are the one place a drain handler sends a Telegram notification (best-effort, fire-and-forget — a down Telegram never fails a control), because control-plane changes have no order-poll loop to report them later: a halt raised from the browser must be visible to the operator wherever they are. **(P2-M7 Task 7.4) The ``universe_add``/``universe_remove`` handlers** are the lightest in the file, lighter even than the control-plane ones: all real validation (non-overridable list, unknown symbol, an `actively_wheeling` removal from `would_own`) already happened at the API boundary (`routers/universe.py`) before the command could ever be created, so neither handler raises ``CommandFailed`` — a command that reaches either always applies. Both call ``src.storage.universe_overrides.set_override`` (never ``clear_override`` — a remove always records an explicit suppressing row, even for a YAML-base symbol) and then ``src.common.universe.invalidate_universe_cache()`` so the edit is live in this process at once rather than up to the composer's 60s TTL later. Neither sends a Telegram notification, unlike halt/resume/set_autonomy — a universe edit is reversible, non-urgent config, not a safety-critical control. **(P3-P4 M1) ``refresh``** is now a registered kind (Task 1.4, 2026-09-09 — previously the one kind with no handler): the ``_refresh`` handler captures current positions + account values through the drain's live IB connection and appends one ``portfolio_snapshots`` row (``source="refresh"``), ignoring the monitor's interval gate — the gate is the monitor's cadence control, not a rate limit on the operator — while the row it writes still counts toward the gate (which reads the table's newest ``captured_at``). It creates no candidate, no approval, and no order (pinned by ``tests/test_drain_refresh.py``), needs no live-mode ``confirm_token``, and sends no Telegram notification (the same silence discipline as the universe edits). ``ib is None`` fails ``broker_unavailable`` — it must not write a row of whatever the last known state was; a failed fetch fails the same way, and a failed write fails ``snapshot_failed`` rather than ``handler_error``. ``result`` carries ``captured_at``/``positions``/``snapshot_id`` so the receipt says what was actually captured. **Writes one more flag at the end of every drain cycle:** an `ibkr_connected` flag (`command_drain_ibkr_connected`), alongside the existing heartbeat, from the same `ib` the cycle just ran with — read by `routers/system.py`'s "IBKR connection" row. |

**Telegram commands served by `approval_service.py`:**

| Command | What it does |
|---|
| `/scan` | Run a full on-demand pipeline scan (CC/CSP/buy opportunities) |
| `/scan AAPL` | Single-ticker scan: fetch option chain for one symbol, run analytics + risk gate, and return the best CC/CSP/buy-to-own assessment in a single Telegram message. Works for any IBKR-listed ticker (not just universe symbols). |
| `/autonomy` | Show the current autonomy rung (🔭 OBSERVE / 👤 MANUAL / 📋 WHITELIST / 🚀 FULL, `system_settings.get_autonomy_level()`) and promotion progress toward the next rung. |
| `/autonomy <level>` | Change rungs. Promotion (moving up) is refused — and the unmet criteria listed — when `promotion_blockers(target)` is non-empty (evidence gate: >=20 fills, >=60% fill rate, >=1 risk-reducing close); demotion always succeeds, no confirmation. Persists across restarts in SQLite (`autonomy_level` key). |
| `/halt` | 🛑 Master kill switch: immediately stop queuing/transmitting all orders. Profit-take *closes* still run (risk-reducing). Persisted in SQLite, so it survives a restart and must be lifted with `/resume`. Optional free-text reason. |
| `/resume` | Release the kill switch; QUEUED orders resume on the next poll cycle. |
| `/status` | Compact overview: account + active short options + pending approvals, plus the per-session intraday-scan tally (🔁 N run · ⚠️ M skipped) so intended (~26/session) vs actual scan count is visible (S9). Shows a 🛑 HALTED banner when the kill switch is engaged. |
| `/positions` | Show live portfolio positions (stocks + options) with P&L |
| `/account` | Show account balances: net liquidation, buying power, margin, excess liquidity |
| `/pending` | List all pending approvals with score and time-to-expiry |
| `/fills` | Recent fills from the last 7 days with quantity, price, and credit received |
| `/calendar` | **C8** Per-day P&L calendar for the last 30 days — net option-premium cashflow per calendar day, with fill count |
| `/campaigns` | **C6** Wheel campaigns for all symbols — CSP→assignment→CC chain with cumulative net premium and adjusted cost basis |
| `/campaigns open` | **C6** Same as `/campaigns` but filtered to open (in-progress) campaigns only |
| `/expire` | Expire all pending approvals (clears the queue without acting on them) |
| `/health` | System health check: IBKR connection status, DB, last scan time, open orders |
| `/help` | List all available commands |
| `✅ Approve` / `❌ Reject` inline buttons | Tap to approve or reject each trade candidate. Shown only for candidates the current autonomy rung routes to a human (i.e. not auto-opened at WHITELIST/FULL) — and withheld entirely at OBSERVE, where the card carries a "proposal only" footer instead. |
| `[CONFIRM LIVE]` inline button | Second-confirmation tap required for each order when `LIVE_TRADING=true`. Appears as an inline keyboard button on the pre-execution message; times out after `fill_timeout_minutes` if not tapped. |

---

### `src/monitor/` — Intraday position watching

Watches your open positions during market hours and fires alerts when action may be needed.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `intraday.py` | Subscribes to live IBKR price feeds for each open position; runs checks every tick. **Qualifies each newly-opened position's contract before subscribing** (fixed 2026-09-11, via `contracts.qualify_options_async`): the unqualified contract `build_option` returns (`conId=0`) made ib_async's own `Contract.__hash__` raise inside `reqMktData`'s ticker bookkeeping before any network call — silently caught by the surrounding `except Exception`, so every short-option subscription had always failed and no `pendingTickersEvent` tick for an option ever reached the monitor through this path. The QUALIFIED contract (not the original unqualified one) is what gets stored in `_subscriptions` for `cancelMktData` to reuse later. On subscribe it loads each position's **entry IV** (from the originating fill's `FillRow.entry_iv`, matched by contract) as the IV-spike baseline, and caches the underlying's **fundamentals** (ex-dividend date) — so all six triggers can actually fire. `fire_alerts` sends the roll alert, de-duplicated two ways: the persisted `roll_alerts` history through the alert policy in `_select_fresh` (2026-10-01 — `manage_dte`/`dte` alert once per position; other triggers at most once per ET day while they still fire; at most one alert per position per ET day except the first `strike_breach`; history scoped to the current holding via `_load_opened_at`, the entry fill's time, which also gives `check_all` its `entry_dte`), and an in-process `_IN_FLIGHT` claim on `(position_symbol, trigger)` taken before any await — ib_async runs each `pendingTickersEvent` batch as its own task, so without the claim a tick burst sent one alert per batch before the first review finished and persisted (2026-09-30: ten TQQQ alerts in a minute). When `monitor.roll_execution_enabled` is set it first fetches the chain and queues an **approvable** roll candidate (N20, via `execution.roll_pipeline`) so the message carries Approve/Reject buttons instead of being alert-only. For **C4 assignment-risk alerts** (exclusively `assignment_risk` trigger), `fire_alerts` dispatches to `format_assignment_alert` instead, which frames the decision as roll / close / let-assign. **P3-P4 M1: the monitor also writes portfolio snapshots** for the web layer — `_maybe_write_snapshot`, called at the *end* of `_refresh_subscriptions` with the positions that pass already fetched (never a second `get_positions` round-trip). Rate-limited by `market_data.portfolio_snapshot_interval_minutes` (the gate reads `portfolio_snapshots`' newest `captured_at` from the DB, so a monitor restart can't burst snapshots and a `refresh` command's row counts toward the interval), RTH-only (`is_rth`), and **never at the expense of alerting**: the whole body is wrapped so a failed write logs and returns — an exception escaping into `_refresh_loop` would kill the refresh task and silently stop roll alerts for every position opened after that moment (pinned by `tests/test_monitor_snapshot.py`). The account fetch is awaited with `asyncio.wait_for` at the connection timeout so a hung `accountSummaryAsync` can't stall the refresh loop. **Writes a heartbeat** (`_write_heartbeat`, called from a `finally` wrapping `_refresh_subscriptions`'s body so it still fires even when `get_positions` raises and the rest of the cycle takes its early return) recording `monitor_heartbeat` and an `ibkr_connected` flag every cycle, connected or not — read by `routers/system.py`'s "Intraday monitor" and "IBKR connection" rows. **2026-10-02:** cancels its own market-data line by the reqId it recorded at subscribe time (`_cancel_mkt_data`) — ib_async maps a contract's Ticker to only the latest reqId, so the roll-alert chain fetch on the same connection used to orphan the monitor's stream and leak the line after the position closed; loads each position's net entry credit (`_load_entry_credit`, skipped for leveraged ETFs) for the `loss_multiple` trigger; and fills a roll proposal's position delta from the live quote, since `get_positions` carries no greeks and a defensive roll is gated on delta reduction. |
| `triggers.py` | Defines stateless trigger-check functions: delta drift, **strike breach** (`check_strike_breach` — underlying below a short put's strike / above a short call's, from the ticker's `undPrice`; the one trigger that means a roll is needed rather than worth considering), DTE threshold, management point (21-DTE decision window), IV spike, ex-dividend risk, and **C4: assignment-risk** (`check_assignment_risk` — fires when `|delta| ≥ assignment_alert_delta` AND `DTE ≤ assignment_alert_dte`; targets deep-ITM shorts near expiry), and the **loss line** (`check_loss_multiple`, 2026-10-02 — cost-to-close ≥ `automation.max_loss_multiple` × entry credit; the monitor passes `entry_credit=None` for leveraged ETFs, which the loss exit closes instead, so this fires only for shorts that should be rolled rather than bought back; an escalation trigger in the alert policy, like strike breach). The management-point trigger fires inside the `manage_at_dte` window (default 21 days, configurable) to create a decision point before the 7-day gamma window tightens — when closing, rolling, or holding are all still viable. `check_all(..., entry_dte=)` skips both DTE-window triggers for a position opened already inside that window (entries run 7–28 DTE). These are pure functions — they do **not** call Claude or Telegram. Claude review and Telegram delivery are handled by `intraday.py::fire_alerts` after triggers fire. Every `RollAlert.detail` is **prose written for the operator**, not debug output: "Delta 0.46 has drifted past the 0.45 roll line — the short is tracking the underlying more closely than intended", not `delta=0.46 > ceiling=0.45`. All DTE arithmetic uses `market_hours.today_et()` so a trigger's DTE always matches the `OptionQuote.dte` printed beside it on the same card. Since M5 Task 5.3 the module also owns `TRIGGER_LABELS` + `humanize_trigger(code)` — the human-readable labels for the trigger codes it produces, moved here from `src/notify/formatters.py` so the Telegram formatter and the web API import the one mapping (no second copy anywhere). The API router populates `RollAlertSummary.trigger_label` through it; the web renders that field verbatim. |

---

### `src/backtest/` — Offline income backtest

A standalone, deterministic simulator for the CC/CSP income strategies. It is **fully isolated
from the live path** — it imports nothing from `engine/` or `execution/` and is never imported by
them — so it can never influence a real order. **v1** synthesises premiums with Black-Scholes from
trailing realised vol (a fair-value IV proxy → expected edge ≈ 0 by construction). **v2 (N21)**
prices the entry premium from the symbol's *stored* daily IV (`iv_history`), so the result actually
measures the variance-risk premium (IV−HV); it also simulates the 50% profit-take and IV-rank
gating. Use it to compare parameter choices, not as tick-accurate truth.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `engine.py` | The pure core. `simulate(symbol, prices, params, *, iv_series=None)` walks a daily-close series, writes non-overlapping short-premium cycles, settles each cash-style at expiry, and returns a `BacktestResult`. With `iv_series` it prices entries at the stored IV (measuring VRP) and reports `mean_vrp_pct` + `iv_source`; `params.profit_take_pct` closes a cycle early when its daily mark decays to the take level (`profit_take_rate`); `params.min_iv_rank` only opens cycles whose backtest IV rank clears the bar. Takes in-memory series → unit-testable offline. |
| `data.py` | yfinance loaders: `load_price_series` (daily closes), `load_iv_series` (forward-filled `iv_history` IV aligned to the price dates, the v2 entry-IV source), and **C10** `load_earnings_dates` (returns sorted historical earnings dates from `ticker.earnings_dates`; empty list for ETFs / network failures, never raises). Kept out of `engine.py` so the core needs no network/DB. |
| `report.py` | Plain-text renderers for the CLI. `format_report` — full `BacktestResult` summary (pricing-IV source, mean VRP, profit-take rate). **C11** `compact_report` — 4-line summary of a `BacktestResult` suitable for injecting into Claude prompts or displaying at decision time. **C10** `format_earnings_cycle_report` — human-readable summary of an `EarningsCycleBacktestResult`. |
| `earnings.py` | **C10** Earnings-cycle backtest + vol-crush mode. `simulate_earnings_cycles(symbol, prices, params, earnings_dates, *, blackout_before, blackout_after, vol_crush_dte, iv_series)` segments the price series by historical earnings dates and evaluates strategy performance across each inter-earnings window, gating entries when the window is too narrow for the DTE. The optional vol-crush mode also enters a short-DTE trade right after each earnings release while IV is still elevated. Returns an `EarningsCycleBacktestResult` with per-cycle detail (pre-earnings trade, vol-crush trade, gate flag) and aggregate win-rate / P&L. Pure and offline: takes in-memory series, never touches the DB or network. |
| `on_demand.py` | **C11** Reusable, candidate-aware core behind the CLI **and** the strategist-prompt injection. `params_from_candidate(cand, *, profit_take_pct, min_iv_rank)` maps a live `TradeCandidate`'s delta/DTE/contracts into `BacktestParams`; `run_backtest(symbol, params, *, …, earnings, …)` loads history and runs the right simulation, returning a `BacktestOutcome` (standard / earnings result / human error message — never raises); `summarize(outcome)` and `backtest_candidate(cand)` return a compact text block. **Fence:** enrichment for verdict/ranking only — reaches Claude solely through the prompt builder, never imported by `engine/`, `execution/`, or the sizing path. |

Driven by `scripts/backtest.py` (`--use-stored-iv`, `--profit-take`, `--min-iv-rank` enable v2) and **C11** `scripts/backtest_candidate.py` (on-demand per-candidate backtest, supports `--earnings` mode and `--compact` output for decision-time review) — both now thin wrappers over `on_demand.run_backtest`. The same core is the **Claude-invocable action**: `strategist.build_prompt` injects a per-candidate `backtest_candidate(cand)` line when `claude.backtest_in_prompt` is enabled (default OFF — it adds a yfinance fetch per candidate at prompt-build time). The Black-Scholes price (`analytics.black_scholes.bs_price`) was added for this harness; `analytics.black_scholes.bs_delta` is reused to delta-target strikes.

---

### `src/orchestrator/` — Daily workflows

Ties everything together into the daily automated routines.

The scan is deliberately split three ways, so that *producing* a scan's data and *presenting* it
are separable concerns: `scan_pipeline.py` produces per-symbol data, `scan_progress.py` owns
everything a human sees (both the live progress messages and the end-of-run sends), and `scan.py`
sequences the two, aggregates the run-level state, gates and persists. The load-bearing rule is
that **`scan_pipeline.py` imports nothing from `src/notify/`** — enforced by
`tests/test_eval_skills.py::test_scan_pipeline_does_not_import_the_notify_layer` — so any caller
that only wants a `ScanResult` (the 15-min daemon loop, a future HTTP API) can trigger a scan
without acquiring a Telegram dependency it could never shed.

`scan_progress.py` is intended to be the only module of the trio importing `src/notify/`, and it
holds every Telegram call *except three*: `scan.py`'s module import block still names
`send_candidates`, `send_buy_list` and `send_account_snapshot`, which it injects into
`send_scan_results` as a `SendDeps` bundle. That is a **test constraint, not a design choice** —
`tests/test_scan_timeout.py`, `tests/test_scan_materiality.py` and `tests/test_scan_review_reuse.py`
monkeypatch those three as attributes of `src.orchestrator.scan`, so moving the references would
make the stubs silently miss and let the suite send real Telegram traffic. Same reason
`scan_pipeline.SymbolDeps` injects its screens. (`run_ticker_scan` also imports
`format_ticker_scan_result` function-locally; that path is untouched by the split.)

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `eod_report.py` | Spawned by `scripts.start`'s built-in scheduler at 4:15 PM ET (configurable via `scheduler.eod_report`; no cron job needed). Pulls the day's **option premium cashflow** (SELL credits − BUY debits — surfaced as "premium cashflow", not a paired realized P&L; assignment stock-leg P&L is excluded, N13), generates a journal entry via Claude, sends an end-of-day summary to the **account snapshot thread** (thread 58). Also appends today's ATM IV observation per symbol (N4, `_append_daily_iv`) so the IV-rank window stays current, and reconciles the verdict ledger + saves the position snapshot. **Task 4 (2026-09-29):** the symbol list is `_iv_symbols(held=…)` — universe ∪ currently-held stock symbols (from the positions this same run already fetched), so a position outside the watchlist/would-own lists still gets its IV tracked — ordered **never-observed first, then oldest-observation first** rather than alphabetically, so a mid-run abort starves whichever names are actually stalest instead of the same alphabetical tail every night (the 2026-09 TQQQ/UPRO/MAGS incident). `_append_daily_iv` also now retries every symbol that failed in the main pass exactly once, at double the per-request timeout (`_append_one_iv` factored out for this) — but only when the main pass finished without tripping the dead-farm abort; retrying after a confirmed-dead health probe would just re-grind the same request the breaker exists to avoid. **Phase 6**: for each detected assignment (`assigned_shorts`) it also calls `mark_campaign_assigned(symbol, assignment_price=strike, right=…)` so the wheel campaign's adjusted cost basis is maintained (C6) — previously `mark_campaign_assigned` was unreachable in production. **The whole run is idempotent**: `_write_journal` (step 6) upserts the day's `JournalRow` — query by `entry_date`, replace every field but `created_at` — mirroring `storage/positions.py::save_position_snapshot`'s pattern instead of the bare `session.add()` it used to be. `JournalRow.entry_date` carries a unique constraint, so the old insert-only write raised `IntegrityError` on a second same-day run and aborted before step 6b ever executed, silently skipping the reconciler, assignment auto-detection, and tomorrow's position-snapshot baseline for the rest of that day. A repeat now replaces the journal row with the re-run's numbers (a re-run exists to produce better numbers, not to be ignored) and reaches step 6b every time. **Task 4b (2026-09-29 — the EOD run must never hang):** right after connecting, `run()` applies `suppress_account_summary_on_reconnect` (`src/ibkr/connection.py`) to the EOD `IB` object — the same 1102 account-summary guard already applied to the approval service's exec connection — so ib_async's own auto-resubscribe can't race the explicit account-summary fetch during a Gateway 1100/1102 connectivity flap (evidence: a ~4h Error-322 loop, "Maximum number of account summary requests exceeded"). That fetch (`_fetch_account_snapshot`) is itself bounded by `_ACCOUNT_SUMMARY_TIMEOUT_S` (120s); on timeout it logs a WARNING and falls back to the latest `portfolio_snapshots` row (`load_latest_portfolio_snapshot`) instead of hanging, re-raising only if no snapshot exists to fall back to. On a successful run, the final step writes `EOD_COMPLETED_KEY` (`src/storage/system_settings.py`, `"eod_completed"`) as an ISO-8601 UTC timestamp — a completion heartbeat a later watchdog check can compare against "today" (ET) to detect a run that silently never finished. The launcher-side half of this fix — the hard ceiling on how long a run may occupy the EOD slot before `scripts/start.py` kills it — is `scheduler.eod_timeout_minutes` (see the `settings.yaml` row above) and `_supervise_eod`/`_eod_tick` in the `scripts/` section below. |
| `scan.py` | The canonical scan orchestrator shared by the 15-min daemon loop and the on-demand `/scan` Telegram command, and the public entry point of the trio: `run_scan` + `ScanResult` (plus `run_ticker_scan` / `TickerNotFoundError`, which `notify/approval_service.py` imports). It owns everything that spans symbols — the scan lease and its per-symbol heartbeat, the half-dead-socket circuit breaker, the candidate/analytics/`fetched_spots` accumulators and the `ScanResult.provenance` tallies — and folds each `SymbolOutcome` returned by `scan_pipeline.scan_symbol` into them, reporting progress through `_Tracker` itself so the pipeline never has to. Scoring, the risk gate, the score floor, top-N, the Claude review and persistence also live here; the final send stage is handed to `scan_progress.send_scan_results` along with the three injected `send_*` coroutines and `include_buy_list` (default `True`; the intraday loop sets it `False` on every cycle after the first that completes each day, so `send_scan_results` skips only the buy-to-own Telegram send — `result.buy_candidates` is scored every cycle regardless, 2026-08-28). Claude-memory injection is bounded (N9): `_load_memory` keeps at most `_MEMORY_ROWS_PER_SYMBOL` (3) rows per symbol, outcomes first; `_persist_memory` upserts one `claude_memory` row per (symbol, strategy, day) instead of inserting one per surfaced candidate per 15-min cycle. Interactive `/scan` progress is driven by `_Tracker` (defined in `scan_progress.py` and imported back here). The 15-min daemon loop runs the scan with both callbacks `None` (silent) and `intraday=True`, which enables the **S1 materiality gate**: `_compute_material_symbols` restricts the per-symbol option-chain fetch to held positions that *rose* past `held_position_move_pct` (2%, directional — up only, since that's what opens room for a new OTM CC strike; a drop doesn't, and existing-position risk is handled continuously by the separate event-driven monitor, not this gate — previously this was unconditional every cycle regardless of direction, the single biggest fixed IBKR chain-fetch cost in the loop), `actively_wheeling` names (`universe.yaml`) whose live spot moved past `intraday_rescan_move_pct` (0.5%), `would_own`-but-not-`actively_wheeling` ("dip-watch") names whose live spot *dropped* past `dip_pull_in_pct` (3%, directional — a rally never counts), names that cleared the score floor last cycle, and any `actively_wheeling`/held name whose *own* last fetch exceeds `force_full_scan_minutes` — checked **per symbol** (2026-08-27), not "sweep the whole core together the instant the single stalest one crosses the line," so refreshes desynchronize and spread out over time instead of bursting all 19 at once; dip-watch names are never swept on this timer, only event-triggered by a genuine drop. **The bucket rules are UNIONed, not selected between (2026-08-28):** a symbol in more than one bucket is tested against every rule that applies to it and any one firing is enough, so holding shares can never *reduce* a name's coverage — it only adds the rally trigger. (The old `if held / elif dip_watch / else` chain let `held` win outright, which silently demoted every held `actively_wheeling` name from the 0.5% either-way gate to 2%-up-only, shrinking the core rotation to just the names the operator didn't hold; the CSP screen runs for every `would_own` name regardless of holding, so a held wheel name has a live CSP reason to refetch on a dip that the CC-oriented held rule cannot see.) Each fetched symbol also records **its own** fetch timestamp rather than one shared run-level stamp, so a long sweep's cohort goes stale spread over the span the sweep took instead of all inside one later cycle — a burst longer than `intraday_loop_minutes` overruns the cycle and costs the next one. A per-cycle ceiling, `market_data.chain_fetch_budget_seconds` (600s, 2026-08-28; **350s** since 2026-09-30 — re-derived around the bounded review, see `How the scan works.md` §5b), caps how long one cycle may spend fetching chains: the gate's filtering collapses when correlation goes to 1 (a broad sell-off makes every `would_own` name material at once, ~23 min of fetching inside a 15-min cycle), and an overrun sets `scan_running`, skipping the NEXT cycle's scan outright. Over-budget symbols are demoted to immaterial (analytics still run) and carried in `ScanResult.unreached_symbols` to the next cycle's `must_include_symbols`, where `_fetch_priority` ranks them first — so a cluster drains over consecutive on-time cycles. Spending order is retry > cleared-floor > movers by multiple of their own bar (`_move_ratio`, the same function the gate uses as its boolean, so the two cannot drift) > staleness-only. Sized from a measured fit, `duration = 56s + 29.9s x fetches`: 900 - 56 - 150 (`symbol_timeout_seconds` of un-preemptable overshoot) = 694, rounded down to 600. Never applies to a manual /scan. Immaterial names are skipped this cycle (analytics still run for every name in `would_own` ∪ holdings, so the buy-to-own list stays complete). Each fetched symbol's `scan_state` baseline is upserted after scoring (S10). In `intraday` mode it also enables the **S5 LLM-skip**: `_candidates_review_hash` hashes the top candidates' `candidate_id` + signal vectors, and when that matches the prior cycle's hash (stored in the `last_review_hash` system setting) the `claude -p` review is skipped and the persisted `ClaudeReview`s are reused via `_load_prior_reviews` — enrichment-only, the risk gate already ran, so this never affects gating (the fence). Manual `/scan` leaves `intraday=False` and always sweeps the full universe (dip_watch names seed-only unless gapped ≥3% overnight — see below), reviews fresh, and sends in full. After the risk gate, `run_scan` also computes per-strategy **near-misses** — the top `_NEAR_MISS_LIMIT` (1) highest-scoring contract that did *not* make `passed` (failed the gate, or cleared it but fell below the score floor), plus a count of any further rejects — and passes them (`near_misses`/`near_miss_more`) to `send_candidates`, so an empty CC/CSP screen on any cycle (including the 15-min loop) names the single closest trade, why it was rejected, and "…and N more" when the rest were truncated. **Single-ticker scan:** `run_ticker_scan(ib, ticker, *, bot, chat_id, progress_msg_id)` is a slimmer on-demand variant for the `/scan AAPL` command: validates the ticker via `qualifyContractsAsync` (raises `TickerNotFoundError` if unknown), fetches the option chain, runs analytics and CC/CSP/buy-candidate generation (CC only if the ticker is held), applies scoring and the risk gate, and edits the Telegram progress message in place with `format_ticker_scan_result`. For any strategy that turned up no qualifying option it identifies the best-scoring *failed* contract (the near miss) and its specific gate reasons (from `scored` — sorted desc — and the verdict map), so the card shows the closest strike and exactly what it failed on. When a strategy has *neither* a qualifying candidate *nor* a near-miss — the symbol never reached a single quote, e.g. `LABU` sitting outside `would_own` for a CSP or shares not held for a CC — it separately computes `cc_hypothetical`/`csp_hypothetical`, an `IdealZone` from `fair_value.compute_ideal_zone` priced directly off `tech_stats`/`iv_stats`/`fund_stats` (no option chain needed) at the strategy's configured mid-DTE, purely so the card still has a fair-value number to show; it also threads the CSP screen's `skipped` reason through as `csp_skip_reason` (e.g. `not_in_would_own`) so the card can say *why* rather than just going quiet. Neither reaches the risk gate, the score, or the allowlist — informational only. It also derives a `greeks_fallback` flag from the chain quotes for the honest sources footer. And — like the full scan, enrichment-only — it runs the best CC + best CSP through `review_candidates` (the configured Claude/Ollama backend) off-thread for a `🤖` verdict; a review failure is non-fatal and the card simply renders without it. The single-ticker call is a **holistic deep-dive**: the macro/sector backdrop is **always** gathered (off-thread, in parallel: this ticker's prior-recommendation memory `_load_memory([ticker])`, the macro `MarketConditions` via `get_market_conditions`, and the `SectorContext` via `get_sector_context`) regardless of whether any contract qualifies — so the deterministic "🌐 Market & Sector" line never depends on a tradeable trade existing. The LLM "🧠 Read" reviews the best CC + best CSP; **when neither cleared the gate it falls back to reviewing the closest near-miss per strategy** so the deep-dive still produces a synthesis instead of going silent (these near-misses surface only as the overall `summary`, never as a per-candidate verdict — the card shows verdicts only beside a qualifying contract; the `/scan TICKER` path never executes, so the fence is untouched). `review_candidates` is called with `sector_context=...`, `single_ticker=True`, and `analytics=analytics_map` (the per-symbol raw signals). `single_ticker=True` switches the prompt to a gate-neutral framing and additionally requests a plain-English `summary` that *explains what each metric means* and synthesizes sentiment from the VIX regime + sector backdrop. The card renders the deterministic "🌐 Market & Sector" line (VIX regime + sector/SPY/ticker returns + relative strength) and the "🧠 Read" block (the LLM `summary`, shown once). A full sweep also tallies `ScanResult.provenance` (a `ProvenanceCounts`) — per-symbol option-chain success/failure/skip, which source produced each symbol's spot price (IBKR chain parity vs. yfinance), VIX availability, and the IBKR-vs-Black-Scholes split of option Greeks — and sends it as a final `format_data_provenance` Telegram message once the scan completes, so an operator can see at a glance which data sources were live this run. **Dip_watch seed-only at startup / manual /scan (2026-08-28):** the full-sweep path (the first cycle since process start, and manual `/scan`) no longer unconditionally chain-fetches dip_watch names — each gets a yfinance probe, and only names that gapped ≥3% overnight (bidirectional — a gap-up is a legitimate CSP setup at the open via IV expansion / news; a gap-down is the dip rule) get a chain fetch. Quiet names and names with no persisted baseline (first-ever run) are seed-only: the probe price is persisted as `last_spot` with `last_scanned_at=NULL` (so the per-cycle rule (c) drop gate works from cycle 1 against a yfinance-sourced baseline, with no IBKR/yfinance mismatch), at the cost of forgoing the first-cycle IV/Greeks set for quiet dip_watch names — acceptable, since a dip_watch name that hasn't gapped overnight isn't a candidate anyway. `/scan TICKER` still force-fetches a single named ticker on demand. **(P2-M4 Task 4.2) `_price_and_gate_ticker(ib, ticker)`** is `run_ticker_scan`'s chain-fetch-through-risk-gate steps (chain fetch, analytics, positions/account fetch, CC/CSP screens, scoring, the `validate_candidates` gate, and the `record_assessments` audit-trail write) extracted into their own async helper returning a `TickerPricingResult` (`quotes`, a new `chain_error` field nothing previously surfaced, `scored`, `verdict_map`, `min_score`, `cc_passed`/`csp_passed`, `ticker_assessed`, plus the analytics/portfolio data `run_ticker_scan` needs downstream). `run_ticker_scan` calls it and continues unchanged with near-miss detection, hypothetical zones, the buy candidate, the Claude/Ollama review, and the Telegram card — a pure refactor, zero behavior change. `TickerPricingAborted` (carrying a `"analytics"`/`"account"` stage) replaces the old inline early-return-with-Telegram-edit for those two failure points, so `run_ticker_scan` still edits the same error message, just from a caught exception instead of an inline `return`. This is what `src/notify/command_drain.py`'s `promote` handler calls to re-price and re-gate a single contract without a live `bot`/`progress_msg_id` and without paying for the review or the formatting. |
| `scan_pipeline.py` | The per-symbol half of a scan, and **data only** — no Telegram, no progress tracker, no run-level state. `scan_symbol(ib, symbol, *, material, account, positions, would_own, probed_spot, symbol_timeout_seconds, deps)` runs one symbol's option-chain fetch (bounded by `symbol_timeout_seconds`, draining leaked market-data lines on timeout/error), spot resolution (`infer_spot_from_quotes`), analytics + sentiment concurrently off-thread, and the CC/CSP screens with `apply_sentiment` stamped onto every contract they produced (passed *and* rejected — both are rendered on the same cards). It never raises: every failure is folded into the returned `SymbolOutcome` — a `ChainStatus` (`SKIPPED` / `FETCHED` / `EMPTY` / `TIMEOUT` / `ERROR`), the passed/rejected candidates, the analytics triple (`None` ⇒ analytics failed, drop this symbol), per-quote Greeks provenance, and an `errors` list of operator-facing strings the caller pushes to whatever progress surface it owns. `ChainStatus` is what lets `scan.py` drive both the provenance tallies and the circuit breaker without this module knowing either exists: only `TIMEOUT` advances the breaker, `SKIPPED` (the S1 gate declined to look) leaves it untouched, and anything else proves the socket answered. External collaborators — chain fetch, analytics, sentiment, the two screens — arrive via the frozen `SymbolDeps` dataclass rather than being imported and called directly, so the orchestrator stays in charge of which implementation runs (and the scan tests keep a single seam to stub). |
| `scan_progress.py` | **All scan presentation** — the live progress messages *and* the end-of-run sends — split out so the pipeline above has no dependency on it. **While the scan runs:** `_Tracker` edits **two** Telegram messages in place: a *checklist* (one line per stage — account, market data, scoring, Claude, notify) and a *dashboard* (a progress bar + ETA, a current-activity line, and a running log of per-symbol errors flagged 🔴). The dashboard's per-symbol updates are throttled (`_DASHBOARD_MIN_INTERVAL`) to stay under Telegram's edit-rate limits; stage changes, errors, and completion always flush. Both surfaces are written through async callbacks supplied by the caller (`_ProgressCB`), either of which may be `None` — the 15-min daemon loop passes neither and runs silently — so the tracker needs no Telegram SDK of its own and a non-Telegram front-end can reuse it by passing its own sinks. **When the scan finishes:** `send_scan_results(tracker, result, *, bot, chat_id, intraday, account, positions, cc_empty_reason, csp_empty_reason, cc_near_misses, cc_near_miss_more, csp_near_misses, csp_near_miss_more, per_symbol_skip, sends)` drives the whole `notify` stage — the CC and CSP candidate cards (with the `_ASSESSED_LIMIT`-capped "assessed but not approved" listing on full sweeps only, S6), the buy list, the C7 skip-reasons card, the best-effort account snapshot, and the full-sweep `format_data_provenance` summary — plus that stage's `tick`/`error`/`complete` transitions. It never raises: the candidate sends are wrapped so a Telegram failure marks the stage failed instead of aborting the scan, and the account snapshot and provenance summary are each independently best-effort. `thread_id` and the three formatters are imported here directly; the three `send_*` coroutines arrive injected via the frozen `SendDeps` (see the note above the table). The empty-screen reason strings are rendered by `scan.py` (`_no_candidates_reason`) and passed in, since they read `ScanResult` plus the per-strategy rejection tallies. |

---

### `src/storage/` — The database

All data is stored in a SQLite database at `data/income_system.db`.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `db.py` | Database connection and session management via SQLAlchemy. For SQLite it enables WAL mode + a 30 s busy-timeout (so the concurrent processes don't hit "database is locked"), applies a lightweight ALTER-in for columns added after the original schema, rebuilds `app_commands` on startup if it still carries the pre-2026-09-11 global `UNIQUE(dedupe_key)` (`_ensure_app_commands_pending_only_dedupe` — SQLite has no `DROP CONSTRAINT`, so this copies the table's rows into a fresh one without it), and creates partial indexes that the model metadata can't express portably (`uq_orders_active_candidate` — one working order per candidate; `uq_app_commands_dedupe_key_pending` — one pending command per dedupe key, not "ever"). |
| `models.py` | Defines the database tables: `candidates`, `risk_verdicts`, `claude_reviews`, `approvals`, `orders`, `fills`, `iv_history`, `price_history`, `scan_state`, `position_snapshots`, `portfolio_snapshots`, `roll_alerts`, `claude_memory`, `verdict_ledger`, `journal`, `system_settings`, `campaigns`, `buy_candidates`, `app_commands`, `universe_overrides`. `portfolio_snapshots` (P3-P4 M1, `PortfolioSnapshotRow`) is the append-only intraday portfolio-capture table backing the web portfolio — separate from `position_snapshots` on purpose (that table's one-row-per-ET-day contract is what assignment auto-detection diffs), with no unique constraint and a `source` of `"monitor"`/`"refresh"`/`"eod"` (see `portfolio_snapshots.py` above). `universe_overrides` (unique on `symbol`+`list_name`, `uq_universe_overrides_symbol_list`) is the **M7 universe-editing store**: one row per operator add/remove of a symbol in the `would_own`/`watchlist` lists, layered on top of `config/universe.yaml` rather than replacing it — the YAML stays the base list, this table holds only the deltas. `symbol` is upper-cased at write time so `nvda`/`NVDA` collide into one row; `list_name` is `"would_own"` or `"watchlist"` and nothing else; `action` is `"add"` or `"remove"`; `created_by` is the audit trail. `set_override` is a full-replace upsert — a later call for the same `(symbol, list_name)` overwrites `action`, `created_by`, and `created_at` in place, so an add followed by a remove is one row with `action="remove"`, never two rows to reconcile at read time. Composed onto the YAML by `src/common/universe.py::effective_universe()` (M7 Task 7.2, 60s TTL cache, fails safe to the YAML base on a broken read) and exposed read-write over HTTP by `routers/universe.py` (M7 Task 7.4: `GET /universe`'s `lists[]` carries override provenance per entry, `POST`/`DELETE /universe/{list_name}/{symbol}` write through the `app_commands` intent queue like every other web action). `app_commands` (`dedupe_key`, nullable, uniqued only among `pending` rows via the partial index `uq_app_commands_dedupe_key_pending` — not a plain column constraint) is the **P2 intent queue**: one row per web-click intent the API enqueues for the exec process to apply. The API inserts here through the write-scoped command engine (§4.2 — the only table it may write), and `approval_service`'s command-drain loop applies each row in the process that holds the exec connection, so mutating `ApprovalRow`, inserting `OrderRow`, and marking the command `applied` is one transaction. `dedupe_key` is `f"{kind}:{target}"` for kinds whose handler is not itself idempotent (approve, reject, promote, roll_request) and `NULL` for kinds that may repeat harmlessly because their handler is an idempotent upsert (halt, resume, set_autonomy, refresh, and — since the M7 final-review fix round — universe_add/universe_remove, whose `set_override` upsert made a stable dedupe key actively wrong: it could match an already-`applied` row from an earlier, semantically different request); a `NULL` key never collides. `promote`/`roll_request` key on a target (`candidate_id`/`position_symbol`) that outlives one approval cycle — a global unique constraint on that column (the pre-2026-09-11 schema) permanently blocked a fresh request once the first had been applied, so `db._ensure_app_commands_pending_only_dedupe` rebuilds a table still carrying that old constraint into the partial-index shape on startup (SQLite has no `ALTER TABLE ... DROP CONSTRAINT`). `confirm_token` is set for live-mode order-reaching intents awaiting a second human confirm (§4.6). `buy_candidates` (one row per recommendation per run, written by the orchestrator at both call sites) persists the `generate_buy_candidates` output so the web recommendations view reads it read-only — display data only, never read back into a gate. `scan_state` (unique per `symbol`) is the S1 intraday-materiality store: one row per symbol holding `last_spot` + `last_scanned_at` (at its last option-chain fetch) and a `cleared_floor` flag, upserted for every fetched symbol so the 15-min loop can decide which chains to re-fetch and which to skip. `price_history` (unique per `symbol`+`obs_date`) stores settled daily OHLCV bars backing the technical indicators and HV30, so scans read history from SQLite and only fetch the missing tail from yfinance. `position_snapshots` (unique per `snapshot_date`) holds the daily portfolio JSON the EOD run writes for assignment auto-detection (position diffing). The `fills` row records `action` (SELL credit / BUY debit) and `entry_iv`. `approvals` and `orders` each carry a `snapshot` JSON column — the frozen `TradeCandidate` payload at approval time (N2a) — so execution runs exactly the size/premium that was approved, immune to re-scan payload drift. `verdict_ledger` (the **outcome ledger**) stores, per Claude-reviewed candidate, the signal vector Claude saw + its verdict + the deterministic baseline, with the realized trade outcome back-filled on close (unique on `candidate_id`, upserted per scan). `system_settings` is a key-value table for runtime toggles and state (`autonomy_level`/`autonomy_whitelist` — the Task 14 autonomy ladder, the `execution_halted` kill switch + its reason, the `scan_lease_expiry` cross-process scan lease, the S1/S6 intraday-suppression keys `last_buy_list_hash`/`last_buy_list_time`/`last_cc_hash`/`last_cc_time`/`last_csp_hash`/`last_csp_time`/`last_candidates_hash`/`last_candidates_time`/`last_review_hash`, and the account-snapshot edit-in-place state `account_snapshot_message_id`/`account_snapshot_date`). `risk_verdicts` is the **assessment audit trail**: one row per contract a scan priced, carrying `run_id`/`symbol`/`strategy`/`strike`/`expiry`, the `verdict`, the `stage` it stopped at (`generator` / `risk_gate` / `score_floor` / `dedupe` / `top_n` / `passed`), the raw `reasons` list, a denormalized copy of the ideal zone (`ideal_lo`/`ideal_hi`/`min_credit`) so a historical row stays readable after the analytics have moved, and (Task 7, 2026-09-29) a `liquidity` JSON column — `{bid, ask, spread_pct, open_interest, volume}` as the candidate's quote stood at assessment time, so a granular `illiquid_*` reject (see `analytics/liquidity.py` above) is auditable without re-fetching the chain; `None` on rows predating this column or on a candidate with no quote microstructure. It sat defined-but-unwired for a long time — every rejection was ephemeral — and is now written by `storage/risk_verdicts.py` and pruned to 14 days at EOD. Write-only forensics: no production code reads it, so it can never influence a trading decision. `candidates`, `orders`, and `journal` carry unique constraints to prevent duplicate rows. |
| `models.py` (trade ledger — docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-05-trade-ledger.md) | **Six new ORM classes, written only by `src/ledger/`.** `BrokerExecutionRow` (one execution — `source_kind="exec"` — or one order-level statement row — `"order"`; uniqued on `dedupe_key`; `superseded_by` points at the higher-priority-feed twin the order/exec twin pass matched it to; `book` is `"system"`/`"manual"`; `trade_time` naive UTC, `trade_date` the US/Eastern calendar date). `BrokerCashEventRow` (dividend/withholding/deposit/withdrawal/fee/interest, uniqued on `dedupe_key`). `BrokerCorporateActionRow` (flagged `reviewed` for human review — never auto-applied to cost basis). `FxRateRow` (USD per unit of `currency` on `rate_date`, uniqued on `(rate_date, currency)`). `LedgerImportRunRow` (one ingest call: `source` csv/flex/live, `status`, `reason`, `counts`, capped `errors`, `period_start`/`period_end`). `TradeAnnotationRow` (operator `notes`/`tags`/`outcome_override`/`exclude_from_stats`, uniqued on `order_key` — the source-independent key `src/reporting/trade_ledger.py::group_orders` derives, so an annotation survives any re-import). |
| `system_settings.py` | Helper functions for the `system_settings` table. **Autonomy ladder (Task 14):** `get_autonomy_level()`/`set_autonomy_level()` (persisted under the `autonomy_level` key, default `AutonomyLevel.OBSERVE` — the safe rung for a fresh install), `may_auto_open(symbol)` (True iff not halted and the rung — WHITELIST checks `_autonomy_whitelist()`/`set_autonomy_whitelist()`, backed by the comma-joined `autonomy_whitelist` key; FULL always; OBSERVE/MANUAL never — clears the symbol for auto-open), `promotion_blockers(target)` (empty list ⇒ promotion allowed; demotion is always `[]`; promoting to WHITELIST/FULL is blocked until the account shows >=20 fills, >=60% fill rate, and >=1 `close:`-prefixed buy-to-close `OrderRow` — **unless Task 12's `automation.paper_skip_promotion_gate` bypasses it**, checked right after the demotion early-return: a paper-mode process (`get_config().is_live` false) with the flag set logs a warning and returns `[]` unconditionally; a live-mode process logs a different warning and ignores the flag, falling through to the fill/fill-rate/close check above untouched — Telegram `/autonomy`, the web `set_autonomy` drain handler, and `scripts/autonomy.py` (Task 12, backs `./ibkr autonomy [level]`) all call this same function, so none of them is a separate back door around the gate or its bypass), `enforce_live_autonomy_evidence()` (**Task 12 fix round 1** — closes the gap the promotion-time check alone leaves: a rung reached earlier, on paper, via the bypass is simply *stored* in the same DB a live process reads, with nothing previously re-checking it once that process starts. Returns `AutonomyLevel | None`. No-op unless `get_config().is_live` and the stored rung is above MANUAL; when both hold, re-validates that *current* rung's evidence (counted by `_evidence_counts()` from `FillRow`/`OrderRow` rows whose `is_live` matches `Config.is_live` only — final review I1, 2026-09-30: paper fills are never live evidence, on the promotion path, this backstop and `autonomy_progress()` alike; entry `OrderRow`s are stamped with `is_live` by `sender._auto_queue_candidates` and `approval_service._process_button`) via a private `_evidence_blockers(target)` helper factored out of `promotion_blockers`'s body — called directly, not through `promotion_blockers` itself, since `promotion_blockers(stored_rung)` would trivially return `[]` via its own demotion/no-op early-return when `target` equals the current rung. Unmet evidence → demotes to MANUAL persistently via `set_autonomy_level`, logs a WARNING naming the blockers, returns the OLD level; otherwise `None`. Called once at the top of `approval_service._run_service`, before any IBKR connection or background loop, so a demotion always precedes the intraday scan loop — where `sender.py`'s `may_auto_open` decides auto-queue vs. a human tap — and the order-poll loop; a demotion also sends a best-effort Telegram notice via the new `format_autonomy_demotion` formatter (see the `formatters.py` row). `sender.py`/`command_drain.py` are the only other autonomy-rung readers and both run inside `approval_service`'s own event loop, not a separate process; the monitor process never reads the rung at all — confirmed by an exhaustive `may_auto_open`/`get_autonomy_level` call-site grep), and `autonomy_progress()` (read-only `(fills, fill_rate, has_closed_once)` tuple for the `/autonomy` status display — the same evidence `promotion_blockers` checks, never itself used to gate anything). The kill switch (`is_halted()`/`set_halted()`/`get_halt_reason()`) overrides every rung — `may_auto_open` always returns False while halted. Also: the cross-process scan lease (`acquire_scan_lease()`/`renew_scan_lease()`/`release_scan_lease()` — serialises full scans so concurrent `/scan` commands can't compete for the market-data line cap, F5), and `get_setting()`/`set_setting()`. The lease is **owner-token + heartbeat** (N7): `acquire` returns a stable token; the scan loop calls `renew` each symbol to extend the TTL so a long ~60-symbol scan can't expire mid-run; and `renew`/`release` are compare-and-swap, so a scan that overran and was re-claimed can't clobber the new holder. All persist across restarts. **Also holds three keys the status card depends on**, all read by `routers/system.py`: `monitor_heartbeat` / `monitor_ibkr_connected` (written by `intraday.py`) and `command_drain_ibkr_connected` (written by `command_drain.py`, alongside its existing `command_drain_heartbeat`). **`intraday_scan_completed`** (`SCAN_COMPLETED_KEY`, Task 4, 2026-09-29) is a fourth heartbeat: `approval_service.py` writes it (ISO-8601 UTC) at the end of every intraday scan cycle that actually completes (the `else` branch after "Intraday scan complete" — skipped or circuit-breaker-aborted cycles do not advance it). Distinct from `monitor_heartbeat`, which the always-on position monitor writes every refresh regardless of whether a scan ran. **`eod_completed`** (`EOD_COMPLETED_KEY`, Task 4b, 2026-09-29) is a fifth heartbeat, the EOD run's counterpart: `orchestrator/eod_report.py::run()` writes it (ISO-8601 UTC) as its very last step, only once every prior step — positions, account snapshot, IV/price backfill, the journal write, the Telegram send, ledger reconciliation, and the DB backup — has actually completed without raising. Neither heartbeat is read by anything yet; both exist for the scan-loop and EOD watchdog checks a later remediation task (Task 5) adds. |
| `orders.py` | Order-creation idempotency: `has_active_order(session, candidate_id)`. Because `candidate_id` is a deterministic hash, a re-scan (especially the 15-min automated loop) regenerates the same candidate; both order-creation paths (`sender._auto_queue_candidates`, `approval_service._process_button`) consult this guard so a candidate with an order already QUEUED/SUBMITTED/FILLED/PARTIAL is not re-queued — preventing stacked duplicate positions (naked short calls for CCs, which bypass the cumulative exposure gates). A partial unique index `uq_orders_active_candidate` (created in `db.py`) is the DB-level backstop: at most one order in a *working* state (queued/submitted) per candidate. Since M5 Task 5.1, `active_order_for(session, candidate_id)` is the row-level companion: it returns the most recent active `OrderRow` for a candidate (same active-state set as `has_active_order`) so the `roll_request` handler can link a `roll_already_working` receipt to the approval the working order came from. `open_short_candidate_id(session, underlying, right, strike, expiry)` returns the most recent system candidate that SOLD that exact contract and is still net short — the attribution target for a buy-to-close fill (`position_manager`, `reconciliation`). |
| `approvals.py` | **Task 8 (2026-09-29, R6) — the stale-approval sweep.** `expire_stale_approvals(now=None, *, force=False)` flips `pending` `ApprovalRow`s to `expired` in one UPDATE, stamping `decided_at=now` on every row it touches; returns the count. Default (`force=False`, the unattended-sweep case): a row is stale when `expires_at` has passed, or — for a legacy row with `expires_at` still `NULL` — when `created_at` is older than `approval.ttl_minutes`. With `force=True` the TTL filter is dropped entirely and every `pending` row is flipped, regardless of `expires_at` or age; `decided_at` is still the real current time in both cases, never a fabricated future one. Before this module, the only path that ever expired an approval was `execution/approval.py::process_queued_orders` — and only for approvals the operator had already tapped "Approve" into a queued `OrderRow`. A card left untapped had no expiry path at all and sat `pending` forever (33 such rows found in the 2026-09-29 post-mortem, root cause R6). Called at the top of every `_order_poll_loop` cycle in `notify/approval_service.py` with the default TTL-scoped sweep, and reused by the `/expire` command with `force=True` so `/expire` keeps its documented "clears every pending approval" contract instead of narrowing to the poll loop's staleness-only scope — one implementation of the flip, two call sites with intentionally different scope. Never touches non-`pending` rows — `approved` stays `process_queued_orders`' job. **Datetime convention:** `ApprovalRow`'s `expires_at`/`decided_at`/`created_at` are naive `DateTime` columns storing UTC wall-clock components (SQLite's bind/result processors copy a datetime's components verbatim and ignore `tzinfo`); `_to_naive_utc` normalises an aware `now` (converting to true UTC first) before it's compared or stamped, so a `now` passed in a non-UTC zone can't silently compare against the wrong wall clock. |
| `maintenance.py` | Periodic DB upkeep, called from the EOD run. `backup_database(keep=7)` takes a consistent online snapshot (SQLite backup API) into `data/backups/` and rotates to the last 7 — the system of record (orders, fills, learning history) is no longer a single point of failure. |
| `positions.py` | Daily position-snapshot persistence for assignment auto-detection. `save_position_snapshot(date, positions)` upserts one JSON row per ET day (written by the EOD run); `load_latest_position_snapshot(before=…)` returns the most recent prior snapshot to diff against. |
| `portfolio_snapshots.py` | **P3-P4 M1 Task 1.1 — the web portfolio's data source.** `save_portfolio_snapshot(account=…, positions=…, source=…, captured_at=…)` appends to `portfolio_snapshots`, a point-in-time capture table the API reads because it cannot ask IBKR anything. **Deliberately separate from `position_snapshots`**: that table is one row per ET trading day and assignment auto-detection diffs its rows (see `src/claude/eval/assignment.py`); this one is append-only on an intraday cadence, has **no unique constraint** (two rows may share a `captured_at` — a `refresh` command moments after a monitor write must not fail; newest `id` wins a tie), and is pruned by retention (EOD run, `storage.portfolio_snapshot_retention_days`) rather than by a constraint. `source` is `"monitor"` (intraday monitor), `"refresh"` (operator command), or `"eod"` (EOD run). `load_latest_portfolio_snapshot()` → `PortfolioSnapshot` (the read-side schema — raw ORM rows never cross a boundary), `latest_capture_time()` (newest `captured_at` without deserialising the payload — the monitor's interval gate reads it so a restart can't burst snapshots), `prune_portfolio_snapshots(days)`. **Never raises** — the monitor calls `save_portfolio_snapshot` from inside a live event loop whose real job is firing roll alerts, so every failure path logs and returns `None`/`0`. |
| `campaigns.py` | **C6 wheel-campaign tracking.** `attach_fill_to_campaign(symbol, candidate_id, strategy, action, avg_price, filled_qty)` — called by the executor after every fill; opens a new campaign on the first SELL, appends candidate IDs to the existing open campaign on subsequent fills, then calls `_rollup` (re-aggregates `total_premium_collected`, `total_debit_paid`, `net_premium` from all `FillRow`s for the campaign's legs) and `_auto_close` (marks the campaign closed when `buy_qty ≥ sell_qty` and not assigned). `mark_campaign_assigned(symbol, assignment_price, right=None)` — sets `assigned=True` and, when shares are *acquired* (short put, or `right` unknown), computes `adjusted_cost_basis = assignment_price − net_premium/100` per share; a short **call** assignment (`right="C"`, shares called away) flags the campaign assigned without altering the basis. Assignment blocks `_auto_close` so the CC follow-on can attach. Wired into the EOD reconciler in Phase 6 (see `eval/assignment.py` / `eod_report.py`). `load_campaigns(limit, *, open_only, since)` — returns dicts for the `/campaigns` Telegram command; **since P3-P4 M2 Task 2.3** each dict also carries `leg_candidate_ids` (the JSON list off `CampaignRow`), the key the web portfolio's `GET /portfolio/campaigns` leg join reads — every consumer indexes by key, so the addition is invisible to `approval_service` and the Phase 4/6 tests. **`adjusted_cost_basis_for(symbol) -> float | None`** (D5, Task 6) — the open assigned campaign's per-share adjusted basis, or `None` when no such campaign exists or the basis isn't positive; read by `strategies/covered_call.py` (falling back to IBKR's raw `avg_cost`) so the premium already collected on the wheel is visible to the `min_strike_vs_basis` gate deciding whether more may be earned, not just to the `/campaigns` display. Backed by the `CampaignRow` ORM model (`status`: `"open"` / `"closed"`, `leg_candidate_ids` JSON, `assigned`, `adjusted_cost_basis`, `realized_stock_pnl`). Never raises — all failures are logged and swallowed. **`total_premium_collected`/`total_debit_paid`/`net_premium` are gross of commissions** — `_rollup` sums `avg_price * filled_qty * 100` per fill and never reads `FillRow.commission`, unlike `src/reporting/legs.py`'s `classify_outcome` (`credit - debit - commissions`, net) which is the accounting rule M4's `src/reporting/` is built on; a campaign's `net_premium` and the net realized P&L of its legs differ by exactly the commission total. Deliberate, not a bug — pinned by `tests/test_campaign_rollup_semantics.py` so a future decision to net commissions into the rollup is made on purpose, not by drift. |
| `scan_state.py` | Per-symbol intraday-scan materiality state (S1/S10). `get_scan_state(symbols)` returns each symbol's `last_spot`/`last_scanned_at`/`cleared_floor`; `bulk_upsert_scan_state(updates)` writes baselines for all fetched symbols in a **single DB transaction** (replaces per-symbol `upsert_scan_state` calls that opened N separate sessions per cycle); `upsert_scan_state` remains for single-symbol updates. The 15-min loop reads this to gate the (expensive) option-chain fetch; the first intraday cycle sweeps all symbols and writes baselines for every one. All failures are swallowed — it is an optimisation, never a correctness dependency (a read miss degrades to "treat as material"). |
| `risk_verdicts.py` | **Assessment audit trail.** `record_assessments(run_id, assessed)` writes one `RiskVerdictRow` per contract the scan priced — approved or not, with the stage it stopped at and the raw reason codes — so "why did NVDA never produce a CSP in July?" is answerable after the Telegram status message has rolled over. `_to_row` also denormalizes the candidate's quote microstructure into the `liquidity` JSON column (Task 7: `{bid, ask, spread_pct, open_interest, volume}`, `spread_pct` recomputed from `quote_bid`/`quote_ask` with `OptionQuote.spread_pct`'s own formula since a persisted row never holds an `OptionQuote`). `purge_old_risk_verdicts(days=14)` prunes it at EOD. Best-effort throughout: a write failure is logged and swallowed, because losing an audit row must never abort a scan. Nothing reads the table back, so it cannot affect a trading decision. |
| `buy_candidates.py` | **Buy-to-own recommendations persistence.** `save_buy_candidates(run_id, candidates)` writes one `BuyCandidateRow` per `generate_buy_candidates` result so the web `GET /research/recommendations` route reads the precomputed list without recomputing (which would mean a full scan per page load). `latest_buy_candidates(limit=25)` returns the most recent full-scan run's candidates ranked by score; `latest_buy_candidates_from(session, limit)` is the read-only variant the API uses (reads through the `mode=ro` trading engine, §4.3). Single-ticker `/scan TICKER` runs persist under a `scan-`-prefixed `run_id` that `latest_buy_candidates` filters out — a `/scan NVDA` must never replace the whole list with one name. An empty list writes nothing and never clears the previous run. Display-only data; nothing reads it back into a gate, score, or sizing decision. |
| `app_commands.py` | **P2 intent-queue helpers.** `enqueue_command(session, *, kind, payload, requested_by, dedupe_key, confirm_token)` inserts an `AppCommandRow` and returns `(row, created)`; on a `dedupe_key` collision against a still-`pending` row it returns that row with `created=False` — never a second row, never an exception (two clicks on Approve produce one command). Scoped to `pending` (fixed 2026-09-11) rather than "any row with this key ever": `promote`/`roll_request` key on a target that outlives one approval cycle, so matching an already-`applied`/`failed`/`expired` row would make a later, genuinely new request silently no-op with the OLD command's stale result. A `None` `dedupe_key` is never deduped (refresh, halt, resume, set_autonomy, universe_add, universe_remove may repeat). `pending_commands(session, limit)` reads the queue oldest-first. `mark_applied`/`mark_failed` set the terminal state plus a `result` JSON (`mark_failed` records `{"reason", "detail"}`). `expire_stale_commands(session, older_than_minutes)` marks past-TTL pending rows `expired` so a command that can no longer be applied is visible rather than invisible. All helpers take an existing `Session` so the caller owns the transaction — the API's write path and the drain loop both need that. |
| `universe_overrides.py` | **M7 universe-editing storage layer.** `set_override(session, *, symbol, list_name, action, created_by)` upserts a `UniverseOverrideRow` — `symbol` is upper-cased before the lookup/write, and the upsert is a total replace (`action`, `created_by`, `created_at` all overwritten), so the row always describes the latest override for that `(symbol, list_name)`, never the first. `clear_override(session, *, symbol, list_name)` deletes the row (reverting to the YAML base) and returns whether one existed. `all_overrides(session)` returns every override row, unordered, for the caller to compose onto the YAML. All three take an existing `Session`, mirroring `app_commands.py`'s shape. Composed onto `config/universe.yaml` by `src/common/universe.py::effective_universe()` (M7 Task 7.2) and written by the `universe_add`/`universe_remove` drain handlers (M7 Task 7.4, `src/notify/command_drain.py`) — `set_override` on every add and every remove alike, never `clear_override`, since a remove must always leave an explicit suppressing row even for a YAML-base symbol. |
| `iv_history.py` | Accessors for the `iv_history` table (N4): `latest_iv` (most recent IV, used by the IV-scaled strike band), `append_observation` (idempotent daily insert, called from the EOD run so the IV-rank window stays current), and `latest_obs_dates`/`stale_symbols` (powering the `/health` staleness warning). All swallow storage errors — derived data must never take the pipeline down. |
| `price_history.py` | Accessors for the `price_history` table: `load_bars` (settled OHLCV, ascending), `latest_bar_date`, and `append_bars` (idempotent — skips dates already stored). Source for `analytics/price_data.get_ohlcv`; same error-swallowing discipline as `iv_history.py`. |

Every stage of the pipeline writes its results here. This means:
- If a scan crashes halfway through, the next run can pick up where it left off.
- The dashboard reads from this database — no IBKR connection needed.
- You can inspect any trade decision by querying the database.

---

### `src/api/` — The FastAPI web layer

A separate process (`scripts/run_api.py`, port 8787 by default) that holds **no `ib_async`
connection and no clientId** — it cannot reach the broker, by construction
(`tests/test_web_fence.py::test_the_api_never_constructs_a_broker_connection` greps for
`ib_async` under `src/api/` and `src/research/` and fails the build if it finds any). The
import direction is one-way: the web layer imports from the trading system (`src.storage`,
`src.common.config`), but nothing under `engine/`, `execution/`, or `strategies/` may import
`src.api` or `src.research` — `test_web_fence.py::test_trading_path_never_imports_the_web_layer`
enforces it by grepping those three packages' source text.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `models/common.py` | `Source` (`edgar`/`yfinance`/`stooq`/`ibkr`/`computed`), `Sourced[T]` (`value`, `source`, `as_of`, `stale`) and `Envelope` (the `as_of`-stamped response base). Every headline number in every response ships as a `Sourced` value; `stale` is **derived** by `Sourced.of(..., fresh_for=...)` from how old `as_of` is, never passed in by a caller who might guess wrong. `Sourced.of` also normalises `as_of` itself to UTC-aware before storing it (not only for the staleness comparison) — a naive SQLite-read timestamp serialises without a UTC offset, which a browser's `Date` parser reads as local time, silently shifting a delayed quote's displayed age by the viewer's UTC offset. |
| `models/research.py` | `Section[T]` (PEP 695 generic) — one region of a page with its own `state`/`data`/`reason`; a slow or missing section never fails the whole response. `AnalysisResponse` (an `Envelope`) is the `GET /research/{symbol}` body: `symbol`, `name`, `exchange`, `is_etf`, `fundamentals: Section[NormalizedFinancials]`, `technicals: Section[TechnicalStats]`, `sentiment: Section[SentimentDetail]`, `news: Section[list[NewsItem]]`, `checks: Section[ChecksPayload]` (M5, `src.research.checks.payload`), and `quote: Sourced[float] | None` (stale after 30 min). `NewsItem` carries `title`, `url`, `published_at`, `source`, `sentiment` (VADER compound). `Bar` and `BarsResponse` are the `GET /research/{symbol}/bars` response: lightweight-charts' field names (`time` as `YYYY-MM-DD`), with server-side `sma50`/`sma200` overlays. **M6 adds:** `BuyCandidateOut`/`RecommendationsResponse` (`GET /research/recommendations` — the full `BuyCandidate` shape, rendered exactly as scored); `SectorCard`/`SectorMover`/`SectorsResponse` (`GET /research/sectors` — `avg_iv_rank` + `iv_rank_count`, `change_pct` null-not-zero when unpriced); `WatchlistItem`/`WatchlistResponse` (`GET /watchlist`); `OptionsCoverage`/`OptionsLensResponse` (`GET /research/{symbol}/options`, Task 6.3 — `checks: list[CheckResult]` is the catalogue's `options` category only, `warnings: list[Warning]` reuses `checks/warnings.py`'s leverage catalogue, `tier: Literal["hot", "cold"]` mirrors `in_universe`). |
| `models/commands.py` | (P2-M1) Command schemas for the intent queue: `CommandKind` (the 10 kinds), typed `Payload` models per kind (`ApprovePayload`, `RejectPayload`, `PromotePayload`, `RollRequestPayload`, `HaltPayload`, `ResumePayload`, `SetAutonomyPayload`, `UniversePayload`, `RefreshPayload`), `PAYLOAD_FOR` map, `validate_payload(kind, raw)`, `dedupe_key_for(kind, payload)`, and `CommandStatus` (an `Envelope`). The rule that matters: `UniversePayload.list_name` is `Literal["would_own", "watchlist"]` — spec §7.2's narrow surface is enforced at the type level, so `sectors`/`leveraged_etfs` fail parsing and can never become a command row. |
| `auth.py` | `Role` (`owner`/`viewer`), `User`, and `authenticate(token) -> User \| None` — a constant-time (`secrets.compare_digest`) bearer-token check against `WEB_API_TOKEN`. Fails closed: an unconfigured or empty token rejects every request rather than accepting anything. Single-owner today; the seam is deliberate so a future multi-user model only changes `authenticate`. |
| `deps.py` | FastAPI dependencies: `current_user` (401 on missing/invalid token), `require_owner` (403 unless `Role.OWNER` — the gate P3/P4 routes apply once they expose the book), and `research_db` (yields a `research_session()`). `assert_known_symbol(research, symbol) -> str` (M7 final-review Fix 2) 404s unless `symbol` is a known SEC filer in the research symbol directory, returning it upper-cased otherwise — shared by `routers/universe.py`'s thin wrappers and `routers/commands.py`'s generic `POST /commands` boundary check for `universe_add`/`universe_remove`, so the same unknown-symbol refusal applies at both entry points. |
| `trading_db.py` | Two engines on `data/income_system.db`. `get_trading_engine()` opens it via SQLite's `mode=ro` URI, so a write raises `OperationalError` at the database layer — not by convention, structurally; `trading_session()` yields a session on it. `get_command_engine()` opens the same file read-write — **for `app_commands` and nothing else** (§4.2). `command_session()` yields a write session guarded by a `before_flush` listener that raises `WriteFenceViolation` if any object outside `AppCommandRow` is in the flush set, naming the offending table. The read-only engine is still the read path even for commands — `commands.get_status` reads through `trading_session`, not the write handle. **Deliberate exception:** `src/common/universe.py`'s `effective_universe()` reads `universe_overrides` through `src.storage.db.session_scope()` — a full read-write SQLAlchemy session, not this module's `mode=ro` engine — because it must work correctly from both the exec process and this API process without giving `src/common/config.py` a storage dependency (see that module's own docstring for the full reasoning). It never writes, so this is not a fence violation, just a different session than the rest of `src/api/` uses. `src/research/checks/warnings.py`'s `_would_own_set()` helper (M7 Task 7.3) transitively depends on the trading DB the same way, through `effective_universe()`, for one `would_own` membership check — safe for the same reason (and because the composer fails safe to the YAML base on any read error), but worth naming so it doesn't read as an accidental layering violation. |
| `commands.py` | **The only module allowed to import `get_command_engine`.** `submit(*, kind, payload, user, dedupe_key, confirm_token) -> (command_id, created)` enqueues an intent through `command_session`; `get_status(command_id) -> CommandStatus \| None` reads it back through the read-only engine. The asymmetry (writes through the write engine, reads through the read-only one) is spec §4.2's structural guarantee that the read path stays read-only even after the write path exists. |
