| `portfolio_source.py` | **P3-P4 M1 — the portfolio fallback chain, the pattern every M2 portfolio route reads through.** `read_portfolio(db) -> PortfolioReading` resolves the freshest portfolio state the API can find, through three rungs: (1) the newest `portfolio_snapshots` row (`source` = that row's writer, `degraded=False`); (2) the newest `position_snapshots` row plus the account block out of the newest `journal.payload["eod_summary"]["account"]` (`source="eod"`, `degraded=True`; the account is **optional on this rung** — validated whole through `AccountSnapshot.model_validate` so a schema drift falls through to `None`, never a `KeyError` at render time); (3) an explicit empty reading (`source="none"`, `snapshot=None`, `degraded=True`) — never a zeroed-out `PortfolioSnapshot`, because a portfolio page rendering zeros is indistinguishable from an account that is genuinely empty, and the difference is the whole account. An exception on any rung falls through to the next — a corrupt newest row must not take the portfolio down when yesterday's data is fine. `as_of` is always the capture time (coerced through `as_utc_opt` — naive SQLite timestamps would otherwise serialise without an offset and be read by the browser as local time), never request time. Never raises. |
| `routers/meta.py` | `GET /health` (no auth — research/trading DB reachability + worker heartbeat + `providers` map of circuit-breaker states; an open breaker degrades the status to `"degraded"`), `GET /me` (the current user), `GET /nav` (the left-rail manifest: one `NavSection{key, label, available, note}` per product area — an unbuilt section renders `available=False` with a `note` rather than being hidden, so the finished shape of the product is visible from M1; as of P4 M5 every section has flipped `true` — none carries a note). |
| `routers/research.py` | `GET /research/search?q=<ticker>&limit=<1-50>` — fuzzy-search the SEC filer symbol directory in `data/research.db`. Ranking: exact ticker match first, then ticker prefix, then name substring, each alphabetical within its band. A blank `q` returns no results (never the whole table). **`GET /research/{symbol}`** (M3, registered after `/search` so the path route can't shadow it) returns `AnalysisResponse`: a 404 only when the symbol is not a known filer, otherwise `materialize(symbol)` builds the fundamentals section inside a wall-clock budget — `Section{state, data, reason}` with `state` one of `ready`/`pending`/`unavailable`. A slow section is a 200 with `reason`, not an error; the client polls. M4 adds `technicals`/`sentiment`/`news`/`quote` sections, each degrading independently; `quote` is stamped with the warm-tier `QuoteRow`'s own `as_of` (not the request time), so a stale quote reads as stale. **On-demand quote seed (2026-09-14):** a symbol with no `QuoteRow` at all — its first-ever view — is no longer left blank until the next 15-min `refresh_quotes` sweep; `materialize()`'s `_quote()` falls through to `ingest/quotes.py::refresh_quote_for()` on that one cache miss, the same on-demand pattern `/bars` already uses for daily bars below. M5 adds `checks: Section[ChecksPayload]`, forwarded straight from `result.checks`/`checks_state`/`checks_reason` (`materialize()` already assembled it via `_section()`, so the router does no extra work beyond the `Section` wrapper). Every call also upserts a `RecentlyViewedRow(user_id, symbol)` — the only signal `warm_symbols()` (Task 4.3) has for its "recently viewed" half of the warm tier. **`GET /research/{symbol}/bars?range=1mo\|3mo\|6mo\|1y\|2y\|5y`** (M4) returns `BarsResponse`: daily OHLCV in lightweight-charts' field names plus server-side SMA 50/200. SMA200 needs 200 prior closes, so the query fetches an extra `_SMA_SEED_BUFFER_DAYS=400`-day lookback beyond the display range to seed both SMAs before the window starts, then slices bars and SMAs together back to the display range — without this, most of a 1y chart's SMA200 would be null. **Cold-symbol backfill (2026-09-11):** `daily_bars` is otherwise only populated by the nightly `refresh_warm_tier` cron, which only covers already-warm symbols — a symbol with zero rows (its first-ever view) now gets one on-demand, best-effort `ingest_daily_bars` call before the query runs, so the chart is never permanently blank; a provider failure still falls through to the existing "no bars → empty lists" contract rather than a 500. **M6 (2026-09-05) adds three more routes.** `GET /research/recommendations?limit=<1-100>` reads `BuyCandidateRow` through the **read-only** trading engine (`latest_buy_candidates_from`, §4.3) — no re-scoring, every field is what `generate_buy_candidates` produced at scan time, so the site and Telegram can never disagree; `computed_at` is filtered to exclude `scan-`-prefixed single-ticker runs so a `/scan NVDA` cannot re-stamp the page with the wrong run's time (fixed 2026-09-05 — see the M6 plan doc's "Verification and fixes"). `GET /research/sectors` aggregates `universe.yaml → sectors` membership against warm-tier `QuoteRow.change_pct` (null, never `0`, for an unpriced sector) and a read-only `iv_history` rank replica (`_iv_rank_for`-style, mirroring `analytics/iv.py`), ordered by `avg_iv_rank` descending. **`GET /research/{symbol}/options`** (Task 6.3, registered directly ahead of `/{symbol}` so the path route can't swallow it) is the on-demand options-income lens: `_iv_stats_for`/`_hv30_for` replicate `analytics/iv.py::get_iv_stats`'s rank and HV30 formulas read-only against `iv_history`/`price_history` (never `get_iv_stats`/`get_ohlcv` themselves, which use a write-capable session and can fetch-and-cache on a miss); an off-universe symbol with no `iv_history` returns `IVStats(symbol=symbol)` (all-`None`, same as the real function's own empty-history case) rather than a rank invented from a short series. The flat metrics dict is then assembled by the existing `checks/metrics.py::build_metrics` and evaluated by `checks/payload.py::build_checks_payload` — no new metric or check code, only new read-only inputs — and the response keeps just the `options` category's checks plus the shared leverage `warnings_for`. `coverage.option_chain` is always `false` in P1 (no IBKR connection in the API process). `in_universe`/`tier` are `true`/`"hot"` iff the symbol is in one of `universe.yaml`'s four lists (`indexes`/`watchlist`/`would_own`/`actively_wheeling`). See `docs/web/api.md` for every response shape. |
| `routers/watchlist.py` | (M6) `GET /watchlist`, `POST /watchlist/{symbol}`, `DELETE /watchlist/{symbol}` — the user's tracked symbols, scoped to `user_id` (default `"owner"`; a second user's items are never returned). POST is idempotent (201 first add, 200 `{added: false}` on a repeat) and 404s on a symbol that isn't a known filer, so no row is ever created that can't resolve to a name; DELETE is idempotent (204 either way). Adding a symbol promotes it to the warm tier immediately (`ingest/quotes.py::warm_symbols()` reads `WatchlistItemRow`). Row assembly reads three sources: the research DB for `SymbolRow`/`QuoteRow` (name + `Sourced[float]` price), and the **read-only** trading engine for `_iv_rank_for` (replicates `analytics/iv.py`'s rank formula against `iv_history`) and `_next_earnings_for` (`FundamentalCacheRow.next_earnings_date`, fixed 2026-09-05 — was hardcoded `None`). `checks` ships as a placeholder `{passed: 0, evaluable: 0, unknown: 0}` — the full per-check payload lives on the ticker page (M5), one click away. |
| `routers/universe.py` | (P1-M6 §4.6, write path added P2-M7 Task 7.4) `GET /universe` now returns `lists: [{name, overridable, entries}]` — `indexes`/`watchlist`/`would_own`/`actively_wheeling`, in that order, only the latter two `overridable: true` — plus `sectors` (symbol → sector tag) and `strike_bands` (symbol → override fraction). Each overridable list's `entries` is the union of the YAML base and every override row for that list (not just the filtered effective list), so a removed base symbol stays visible with `removed: true` and its `created_by`/`created_at` provenance, giving the UI a revert path. `editable` is now `true` — `POST`/`DELETE /universe/{list_name}/{symbol}` (owner-only) are thin wrappers over the same `app_commands` intent queue every other write route uses: `list_name` is a `Literal["would_own", "watchlist"]` path parameter, so any other value is `422` before this module's code runs at all; an unknown symbol (against the research symbol directory) is `404` — via `src/api/deps.py::assert_known_symbol`, shared with `routers/commands.py`'s generic `POST /commands` route so that route cannot bypass this check (M7 final-review Fix 2); removing an `actively_wheeling` symbol specifically from `would_own` is `409`. The actual mutation happens later, in `command_drain.py`'s `_universe_add`/`_universe_remove` handlers. **`POST`/`DELETE` have no dedupe key** (M7 final-review Fix 1): both handlers upsert via `set_override`, which is idempotent by construction, so a repeated request always creates a fresh command and re-applies rather than reusing a possibly stale, already-`applied` row — every response is `201`, never a dedupe `200`. **A YAML-base entry's `removed`/`overridden` fields are computed directly from the override row's `action` plus the `actively_wheeling` guard** (matching `src/common/universe.py::_compose_list`'s own guard condition exactly), never from `effective_universe()`'s cached composed list (M7 final-review Fix 3) — that cache is invalidated only in the exec process that runs the drain, so deriving `removed` from it here could read up to 60s stale in this separate API process right after a removal was applied elsewhere. `created_at` is normalized through `routers/options.py`'s `_as_utc` before serializing (M7 final-review Fix 4), since SQLite returns a naive `datetime` for this column and a naive ISO string is parsed as local time by a browser's `Date`. |
| `routers/commands.py` | (P2-M1, M3, M4) `POST /commands`, `GET /commands/{id}`, `POST /commands/{id}/confirm` — the first write routes. Owner-only (`require_owner`, 403 for viewers). `POST` validates the payload against the typed schema for the `kind` (422 on a bad shape), computes the `dedupe_key`, and enqueues through `src/api/commands.py::submit` (201 new, 200 dedupe). In live mode, `approve`/`promote`/`roll_request` get a `confirm_token` and `needs_confirmation: true` (§4.6); the drain skips them until `confirm` supplies the token. `GET` reads through the read-only engine (never the write handle) and surfaces the outstanding `confirm_token` so the owner can complete the second confirmation round-trip (cleared on confirm, absent in paper mode). **M3 confirm contract:** a wrong or missing token is **403 and does not clear the field** — the command stays `pending`, fail closed; confirming a command not awaiting confirmation is **409**; the token expires with `approval.ttl_minutes` (the drain's sweep moves an unconfirmed command to `expired`, never applies it). **M4 Task 4.1 — the promotable-stage guard:** for `kind == "promote"`, `assert_promotable` runs inside `post_commands` (through a `trading_session`, i.e. the read-only engine — never the write handle) before `submit` is called, so a refused promote leaves no `app_commands` row at all. It looks up the candidate's **most recent** `risk_verdicts` row (`RiskVerdictRow.candidate_id` is not unique — 14 days of per-run history are kept — so "the assessed row" means newest by `created_at`, `id` as tiebreaker) and 404s if none exists. `PROMOTABLE_STAGES = frozenset({"score_floor", "dedupe", "top_n"})` (spec §5.2); any other stage (`risk_gate`, `generator`, `passed`) is refused with `409 {"reason": "stage_not_promotable", "stage": ...}`. **M7 final-review Fix 2 — the same API-boundary principle, for `universe_add`/`universe_remove`:** before `submit`, `post_commands` runs `deps.assert_known_symbol` against the research DB (through `research_session()`, not the trading engine) and 404s on an unknown symbol — closing the gap where this generic route, unlike `routers/universe.py`'s thin wrappers, previously accepted a `universe_add`/`universe_remove` payload for any symbol string with no existence check. The `actively_wheeling`-removal `409` is deliberately **not** duplicated here: the composer's own remove-guard (`src/common/universe.py::_compose_list`) makes that direction safe regardless of which route an override row came in through. See `docs/web/commands.md` for the full per-kind runbook, the promotable table, and the live-mode flow as an operator-ordered list. |
| `routers/options.py` | (P2-M2) The options console's read surfaces, all owner-only, all through the read-only engine. `GET /options/approvals` + `/approvals/{id}` join `ApprovalRow` → `CandidateRow`/`RiskVerdictRow`/`ClaudeReviewRow`; a pruned candidate still renders from the approval's frozen `snapshot`, and the verdict/review lookups take the newest row by `created_at` (a candidate assessed across more than one scan run has more than one `risk_verdicts`/`claude_reviews` row — an early build used `scalar_one_or_none()` here and 500'd with `MultipleResultsFound` on exactly that, ordinary, case). `GET /options/assessed` implements the promotable table from spec §5.2 exactly (`risk_gate`/`generator` never promotable, `score_floor` promotable with the live configured minimum in its note), ranked like `scan.py::_rank_assessed`, and caps `limit` across all groups, not per symbol. `GET /options/orders` / `/fills` render `avg_fill_price`/`mark`/`unrealized_pnl` as `null` when unfilled/unknown, never a fabricated `0`. `GET /options/shorts` stamps `as_of` from the position snapshot's own capture time, not request time, and carries `delta`'s BS-vs-IBKR provenance through `Sourced`. **Since M0 Task 0.3**, `assignment_risk` is computed through `src/common/assignment_risk.py::is_assignment_risk`, reading the monitor's own configured thresholds (`monitor.assignment_alert_delta`/`assignment_alert_dte`, 0.70/21) via `assignment_risk_thresholds(get_config())` — not a hardcoded `0.70`/`7` as before, which silently disagreed with the monitor's real 21-DTE alerting window. This deliberately widened what the route reports: a short between 8 and 21 DTE that the monitor was already alerting on now also flips `assignment_risk` to `true` here. Since M2 Task 2.4 each short carries its fired `RollAlertRow`s as `RollAlertSummary`; since M5 Task 5.3 each alert carries `trigger_label` (humanised server-side via `src.monitor.triggers.humanize_trigger` — the one mapping the Telegram formatter also uses, so no second copy) and `claude_recommendation` (passed through from the row, nullable, so the web can label it a model opinion). `GET /options/controls` reads `command_drain_heartbeat` (written by `command_drain.py`) against twice `execution.poll_interval_seconds` — never `/health`'s research-worker heartbeat — and defaults to unhealthy, never `true`, on a drain that has never run. Since P2-M6, it also derives `halted_at` from the most recent APPLIED `halt` command row, bounded to after the most recent APPLIED `resume` row so a resumed, older halt can never shadow a fresh command-row-less one (a circuit-breaker trip or a Telegram `/halt`); a halt with no qualifying row renders `null` rather than a fabricated time. |
| `routers/portfolio.py` | **P3-P4 M2 (Tasks 2.1-2.4) — the portfolio read surfaces**, all owner-only (`require_owner`), all rendering what `read_portfolio` (the M1 fallback chain) found — no second data path. Every snapshot-derived route carries the `source`/`degraded`/`note` triple so a client can tell which rung served and read the degradation in words; `as_of` is the reading's capture time (request time only on the `none` rung, where nothing claims to be a measurement). `GET /portfolio/summary` — account values (net liq / cash / buying power / maintenance margin / excess liquidity) each as `Sourced` with `Source.IBKR` and `fresh_for` twice the snapshot interval (one missed capture reads stale), beside the exposure block: open positions/shorts/campaigns, net delta (the same Σ the EOD summary computes), cash secured against **short puts only** (`strike × contracts × 100`), buying-power utilisation (`null` when buying power is zero — never `0.0`, which would read "nothing committed"), and the assignment-risk count. `GET /portfolio/positions` — grouped by underlying (an option whose `underlying` is `null` groups under its own symbol, never dropped), reporting **both cost bases**: `avg_cost` from IBKR and `adjusted_cost_basis` from `campaigns` (assignment shares only; both adjusted fields are `null` otherwise), with `unrealized_pnl` against the former and `unrealized_pnl_adjusted` against the latter — a profitable assigned position must not look like a loss. The basis is read through the API's own read-only engine, mirroring `storage.campaigns.adjusted_cost_basis_for`'s newest-open-assigned-campaign rule exactly — the route never opens the storage engine's read-write `session_scope` (the universe composer is the one documented exception to that invariant, and this route is not it). `OptionLeg.right`/`strike` are `null` when the snapshot carried them — never a fabricated `"C"`/`0.0` pair — and `moneyness` uses the group's stock price, else the latest `price_history` close (the same table `routers/research.py::_hv30_for` reads read-only); `null` when unknown, never guessed from the strike. `dte` is `null` when expiry is unknown, never `0`. `GET /portfolio/campaigns` — the wheel threads with `?status=`/`?symbol=`/`?limit=` filters (limit default 50, max 200 — campaigns are never pruned, so the read is capped, the same discipline `load_campaigns` and the other list routes follow), ordered `opened_date` desc with open before closed at the same date; `as_of` is request time here (campaigns are written on fill, not captured — documented so nobody "fixes" it); financials are **gross of commissions** (M0 Task 0.4's pinned semantics); a pruned `CandidateRow` still renders as a leg with `known: false` so the leg count cannot silently disagree with the financials that survive pruning. `GET /portfolio/calendar` — expiries grouped by date over `?horizon_days=` (default 45, max 365; the horizon gate and `dte` both measured on the ET exchange calendar — one clock, so a rendered entry's `dte` always agrees with the gate that let it in), each entry carrying the `consequence` table exactly: ITM short put → `assigned`; ITM short call → `called_away` only when the stock is held to deliver, else `assigned` (a naked short call assignment is short stock); any short OTM/ATM → `expires_worthless`; unknown moneyness → `unknown` — a real value, never defaulted to `expires_worthless`. Every route's `none` rung is a **200 with explicit emptiness** (`account: null` / `groups: []` / `days: []` plus `source="none"`), never a 404 and never zeros — zeros would be a claim about the account. |
| `routers/pnl.py` | **P3-P4 M5 (Tasks 5.1-5.3) — the P&L read surfaces**, all owner-only, all thin renderers over `src/reporting/pnl.py` (M4): `build_legs` feeds `build_campaigns`, `build_summary`, and `equity_curve` alike, so no route computes a P&L figure of its own — the single-accounting-rule invariant. `as_of` is **request time** on every P&L route (the ledger is computed on read); `marks_as_of` is a **different field** — the capture time of the portfolio snapshot backing every unrealised figure, coerced through `as_utc_opt`, from the same single `read_portfolio` call the portfolio page's routes read, so the two surfaces' unrealised figures agree by construction; the `none` rung passes `snapshot=None` and returns `marks_as_of: null` with every unrealised field null — an unmarked position is not worth zero. `GET /pnl/ledger` — legs grouped under campaign threads (campaign-less legs land in M4's synthetic per-symbol threads, so `n_legs` always equals the sum of every campaign's leg count); filters (`symbol`/`strategy`/`outcome`/`since`/`until`/`book`) are echoed back verbatim so a client can prove what it is looking at. `GET /pnl/summary` — the totals and breakdowns from the same `PnlLeg` list; **`book=all` with both paper and live fills is refused `422 {"reason": "mixed_book"}`** — M4 `build_summary`'s `ValueError` becomes a user-facing answer here, never a 500 and never a wrong number; a single-book summary over an empty list is a valid empty summary. `GET /pnl/equity` — the curve plus its `gaps`, untouched; `book=all` is fine here **deliberately** (a curve is a display choice, not a headline total — the asymmetry with the summary is documented so nobody "fixes" one to match the other). `GET /pnl/ledger.csv` — the same rows under the same filters as CSV, built from the same `build_legs` call so the JSON and CSV cannot drift; unit-suffixed headers (`credit_usd`, `net_pnl_usd`, ...), a `book` column, `None` serialised as an empty cell **never `0`**, and a dated `Content-Disposition` filename. |
| `models/pnl.py` | **P3-P4 M5** — `LedgerFilters` (echoed on every response), `LedgerResponse` (`filters`, `campaigns: CampaignPnl[]`, `marks_as_of`, `n_legs`), `SummaryResponse`, `EquityResponse`. The heavy shapes (`CampaignPnl`, `PnlLeg`, `PnlSummary`, `EquityCurve`) come from `src/common/schemas.py` — M4's settled types, not duplicated here. |
| `routers/ledger.py` | **Trade ledger (Tasks 1-12), read-only, owner-only.** Thin renderers over `src/reporting/trade_ledger.py::build_book` — every route computes nothing of its own, so the trades table, ticker pages, CSV export, and the Google Sheets mirror agree by construction. `GET /ledger/summary`, `/ledger/tickers`, `/ledger/tickers/{symbol}` (404 for a symbol with no ledger history), `/ledger/trades` (filters: `symbol`/`right`/`outcome`/`book` — `system`/`manual`/`spreads` — /`tag`/`since`/`until`/`sort`), `/ledger/trades.csv` (same filters, same `sheet_row`/`SHEET_HEADER` the Sheets mirror uses), `/ledger/trades/{order_key}` (404 for an unknown key; returns the trade plus its raw executions, including any row a twin-pass supersession folded into it), `/ledger/imports` (the last 50 import runs, the Flex/Sheets feed-status pair — `configured`/`last_run`/`last_status`/`last_error`, secrets never included — and up to 100 corporate actions newest-unreviewed-first). Every *write* is a `POST /commands` intent (`ledger_import`/`ledger_annotate`/`ledger_ca_reviewed`, see `docs/web/commands.md`) the drain applies — this router never calls `src.ledger.ingest`/`annotations` directly. The `web/app/ledger/*` pages (Tasks 13-16) are the consumers — see the `web/` folder guide. |
| `models/ledger.py` | Response shapes for `GET /ledger/*`: `LedgerSummaryResponse`, `LedgerTickersResponse`, `LedgerTickerResponse`, `LedgerTradesFilters`/`LedgerTradesResponse`, `LedgerExecutionOut`/`LedgerTradeResponse`, `LedgerImportRunOut`/`LedgerFeedStatus`/`LedgerCorporateActionOut`/`LedgerImportsResponse`. The heavy shapes (`LedgerTrade`, `LedgerTicker`, `LedgerTickerDetail`, `LedgerSummary`, `LedgerOutcome`) come from `src/common/schemas.py`, not duplicated here — same pattern as `models/pnl.py`. |
| `models/portfolio.py` | **P3-P4 M2** — the response shapes for the four portfolio routes: `AccountBlock`/`ExposureBlock`/`PortfolioSummaryResponse` (2.1), `OptionLeg`/`StockLeg`/`PositionGroup`/`PositionsResponse` (2.2), `CampaignLeg`/`CampaignSummary`/`CampaignsResponse` (2.3), `CalendarEntry`/`CalendarDay`/`CalendarResponse` (2.4). All built on `Envelope`/`Sourced` from `models/common.py`. |
| `settings_read.py` | `read_setting(db, key) -> str \| None` / `parse_setting_dt(raw) -> datetime \| None` — the system_settings-row reader shared by `routers/options.py`'s `drain_healthy` and `routers/system.py`'s heartbeat rows, extracted so the two routers can't drift on how a setting is read or parsed. |
| `routers/system.py` | **The left-rail status card's backend.** `GET /system/status` (owner-only) returns 7 rows — Trading DB / Research DB (a live `SELECT 1` each), Data providers (`src.data.breaker.breaker_states()`, rolled up to the worst state), Command drain / Intraday monitor / Research worker (each a heartbeat-age check — `ok` within 2x that process's own poll interval, `down` if stale, `unknown` if the heartbeat has never been written this run — never a false "down" on a cold start; the Research worker is the one exception, with its own fixed `_RESEARCH_WORKER_MAX_AGE` of 2 minutes rather than 2x a poll interval, since research jobs only heartbeat on success), and IBKR connection (an aggregate of the two `_ibkr_connected` flags `intraday.py` and `command_drain.py` now write alongside their heartbeats, `down` if either daemon disagrees or its heartbeat is stale). No row for `web_api` itself (a failed fetch of this very endpoint *is* that signal) or `eod_report` (a daily batch job, not a thing that's "up" at a point in time). **A broken `trading_db`/`research_db` connection degrades gracefully rather than 500ing the whole endpoint** (final review fix #1): the initial `SELECT 1` reachability check for each DB sets a `trading_ok`/`research_ok` flag, and every later read of that same DB — `read_setting` for the drain/monitor/ibkr-connected keys, `read_heartbeat()` for the research-worker row — is skipped when its flag is `False`, in favour of an honest `"unknown"` row with a `"... DB unreachable — cannot read heartbeat"` detail, since a `Session` that already raised once re-raises (`PendingRollbackError`) on any further use. `GET /system/{name}/log` tails that daemon's log file (`name` through a fixed allowlist — `approval`/`monitor`/`research`, mapped to the real paths in `scripts/start.py`'s `SERVICES` dict, never built from the request), filtered server-side to WARNING+ by default (`level=info` widens to INFO+), capped at 150 lines, and carries a `file_exists` bool distinguishing a genuinely missing log file from one that exists but has no lines at the requested level (final review fix #3) — the frontend renders "No log file yet" only for the former. |
| `main.py` | `create_app()` — the FastAPI factory: CORS from `cfg.research.api.cors_origins`, a 404 handler that returns JSON (never HTML, since the client parses every response as JSON), and the router mounts (M6 adds `routers/watchlist.py` and `routers/universe.py`; P2-M1 adds `routers/commands.py`; P2-M2 adds `routers/options.py`; P3-P4 M2 adds `routers/portfolio.py` under the `/portfolio` prefix; P3-P4 M5 adds `routers/pnl.py` under the `/pnl` prefix; this plan adds `routers/system.py`; the trade-ledger plan adds `routers/ledger.py` under its own `/ledger` prefix, declared on the router itself). |

Config lives in `config/research.yaml` → `api.host`/`api.port`/`api.cors_origins` (loaded by
`ResearchApiCfg`) and `summary.backend`/`summary.model`/`summary.timeout_seconds`/
`summary.cache_ttl_hours` (loaded by `ResearchSummaryCfg`, M7). Secrets (`WEB_API_TOKEN`,
`SEC_CONTACT_EMAIL`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`) live in `.env`, never in YAML.

---

### `src/research/` — The research data layer (web tier)

A **second** SQLite database at `data/research.db`, separate from `data/income_system.db` on
purpose. SQLite permits one writer per database; a nightly research ingest writing into the
trading database would serialise against `approval_service`'s writes, and the failure mode is
a delayed trade approval. Two databases means two writers never contend. The research
`Base` (in `src/research/store/models.py`) is a distinct `DeclarativeBase` from
`src/storage/models.Base` — `create_all()` can never build the wrong schema against the wrong
engine, and the import is one-way: **the trading system never imports `src.research`** (the
`tests/test_web_fence.py` fence test enforces this — see §4.7 of the web design).

Ingestion runs as its own process (`scripts/run_research_worker.py`) — an APScheduler
`BackgroundScheduler`, the same as `src/api/`, it holds **no `ib_async` connection and no
clientId**. It writes only to `data/research.db`; the fence test above also covers it. A job
that raises is logged and swallowed (`ingest/jobs.py::run_job`) so one failing source can
never take the scheduler, or every other registered job, down with it — a heartbeat
(`worker_heartbeat` table) is written only after a job **succeeds**, so `/health`'s
`worker_heartbeat` field cannot lie about an outage.

| File / package | What it does |
|---|
| `schemas.py` | The shapes the research layer passes between its own modules: `Fact` (frozen dataclass — one XBRL fact; `start` is `None` for instant/balance-sheet facts), `LineItemValue` (one normalised line item for one period, traceable to its filing via `concept`/`accn`/`filed`/`form`), `PeriodStatement` (every line item for one period), and `NormalizedFinancials` (a filer's annual + quarterly statements). |
| `store/models.py` | Research-database ORM: the symbol directory (`symbols`), raw EDGAR companyfacts (`company_facts_raw` — carries an `etag` so a 304 is free on re-ingest), normalised line items (`financials` — carries `form` alongside `concept`/`accn`/`filed` so the cache can rebuild `LineItemValue` fully without a network fetch), daily bars (`daily_bars`), delayed quotes (`quotes`), news items (`news_items`), the assembled analysis cache (`analysis_cache`), deterministic check results (`check_results`), AI summaries (`summaries`), watchlists + items, recently-viewed, the ingest job queue (`ingest_jobs`), and the single-row `worker_heartbeat` (`beat_at`, `last_job`) written only on a successful job. 14 tables. |
| `store/session.py` | Engine + session factory for `data/research.db` (`get_research_engine`, `init_research_db`, `research_session` context manager). WAL mode + `foreign_keys=ON`, matching the trading database, so the API can read while a worker writes. `init_research_db()` also backfills columns a later milestone added to an already-existing table (`ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN` against a diff of the ORM metadata vs `PRAGMA table_info`) — `create_all()` alone only creates missing tables, so a database file left over from an earlier milestone would otherwise keep the old schema and raise `OperationalError: no such column`. No migration framework in this project; this is deliberately the minimal fix. |
| `ingest/symbols.py` | `refresh_symbol_directory() -> int` — upserts the SEC filer directory from `get_symbol_directory_provider()` into `symbols`. An upsert (not truncate-and-reload) so locally enriched columns (`sector`, `industry`, `is_etf`) survive a refresh; a failed fetch (empty list) leaves existing rows intact rather than emptying the table search depends on. Returns the provider-sourced row count only. **After** the provider upsert, it also seeds `config/symbol_directory_overrides.yaml` (2026-09-13) — a small hand-maintained list for tickers EDGAR's directory omits entirely (verified against both `company_tickers.json` and `company_tickers_exchange.json`: leveraged/inverse ETFs like `TQQQ`/`SOXL`/`UPRO`, share classes of a shared trust CIK rather than individually-registered filers, appear in neither). An override is inserted only when the symbol has no row yet from the provider or a prior run — it never overwrites one, so real EDGAR coverage always wins going forward — and carries no `cik` (there isn't one to give it) and `is_etf: true`. This step runs even when the provider fetch itself failed, since the override list has no network dependency. |
| `ingest/concepts.py` | XBRL fact parsing and concept resolution. `LineItemSpec` (Pydantic) maps a canonical line item to an ordered list of us-gaap concept candidates and a `kind` (`duration` for start+end facts — income/cash flow, `instant` for end-only — balance sheet). `load_concept_map()` reads and caches `config/research_concepts.yaml` — adding a mapping is a config change, not a code change. `parse_facts()` skips malformed entries individually (one bad date in a filer's history must not cost the other forty facts). `resolve_line_item()` pools facts across **every** candidate concept in the list rather than stopping at the first one with any data — a filer can switch which us-gaap tag it reports a line item under between fiscal years (NVIDIA tags revenue `RevenueFromContractWithCustomerExcludingAssessedTax` for FY19-22 and `Revenues` for FY23-26; stopping at the first match used to silently drop the other tag's periods entirely), so each `Fact` now carries its own `concept` and `select_periods()`'s existing latest-`filed`-wins rule is what arbitrates the rare case where two aliases both report the same period. Returns `None` when no alias has any facts (honest absence — the caller renders UNKNOWN, never a zero). `select_periods()` picks one fact per period, newest first, with two correctness rules: `kind` is enforced (summing four quarterly durations into "total assets" produces plausible nonsense), and ties on `end` are broken by the **latest `filed`**, so a restatement becomes the current answer instead of silently reverting. `assert_units_supported()` rejects units outside `{USD, USD/shares, shares, pure}` — a EUR revenue silently treated as USD is worse than no number at all. |
| `ingest/fundamentals.py` | `normalize(payload, symbol)` turns a companyfacts document into `NormalizedFinancials` (annual + quarterly `PeriodStatement`s, newest first); unmapped line items are absent, never zero. `load_cached_financials(symbol)` rebuilds `NormalizedFinancials` from the `financials` table alone — the **warm path**: no network, no SEC fetch, no re-normalisation. `ingest_fundamentals(symbol, cik)` is the **cold path**: fetches via `get_filings_provider()` honouring the cached ETag (a 304 reuses the cached raw payload rather than treating it as "no facts"), caches the raw payload in `company_facts_raw` (so normalisation is replayable when the concept map gains a mapping), normalises, and persists `FinancialRow`s (carrying `form` so the cache can rebuild `LineItemValue` fully) — replacing the symbol's rows wholesale so a re-ingest leaves exactly one row per (period, line item). Returns `None` when the filer has no XBRL facts (ETFs, foreign filers). |
| `ingest/materialize.py` | Cold-tier materialisation with a wall-clock budget. `SectionState` (`ready`/`pending`/`unavailable`), `MaterializeResult`, `materialize(symbol, *, budget_seconds)` — **cache-first**: a warm view serves from `load_cached_financials` with no SEC fetch; only a cache miss calls `ingest_fundamentals` inside `research.tiers.materialization_budget_seconds` (default 8s). A finished ingest is always `READY` with its data, even if it overran the budget (a background `IngestJobRow` is queued so the *next* view is instant; `PENDING` never carries data — the old PENDING-with-data contract caused the client to poll and re-fetch indefinitely). `reason` is a fixed user-facing string, never `str(exc)` — an exception carrying a CIK or transport detail must not reach the browser. `enqueue()` deduplicates on `(symbol, kind, pending)`. `drain_ingest_jobs()` runs queued jobs, records the error and increments `attempts` on failure, and gives up at `MAX_ATTEMPTS=3` (`status=failed`). M4 adds the enrichment sections (`_technicals`/`_sentiment`/`_news`/`_quote`) each guarded by `_section()` so one outage cannot cascade — a sentiment failure leaves technicals rendering; a news outage leaves sentiment rendering; and so on. Sentiment is enrichment-tier and is never passed into technicals or fundamentals (a structural test inspects `materialize`'s source to enforce this). `_quote()` returns `(price, as_of, reason)` — `MaterializeResult.quote_as_of` carries the warm-tier `QuoteRow`'s actual capture time through to the API layer, which is what lets `GET /research/{symbol}` stamp the quote's `Sourced` value with its real age instead of the request time. M5 (Task 5.5/5.7) adds `metrics`/`checks`/`checks_state`/`checks_reason`: `_checks_metrics()` calls `get_iv_stats`/`get_fundamental_stats` (`src/analytics/`) and `build_metrics()` (`src.research.checks.metrics`) to assemble the flat metrics dict — a failure here degrades to `{}`, never a page failure — and `_checks()` (guarded by `_section()`, same as the other enrichment sources) turns that dict into a `ChecksPayload` via `build_checks_payload()`. `is_etf` is read once alongside `cik` in `materialize()` (same `SymbolRow` query) and threaded through every `_enrich()` call site, since `build_metrics` needs it to skip XBRL extraction and gate the ETF-only fund fields. **`materialize()` distinguishes "no directory row at all" from "a known symbol with no CIK"** (2026-09-13): the former (nothing search or the watchlist could ever have found) returns immediately with only `fundamentals_state=unavailable` set, same as always; the latter (e.g. an override-seeded symbol like `TQQQ` — see `ingest/symbols.py`) still marks fundamentals `unavailable`/`No SEC filer record for this symbol` but proceeds into `_enrich()`, since technicals/sentiment/news/quote/checks need a price and a symbol, never a CIK. Without this split, a symbol the override file makes searchable would still render an entirely blank stock page. |
| `ingest/prices.py` | `ingest_daily_bars(symbol) -> int` — pulls daily OHLCV from the active `BulkPriceProvider` and upserts into `daily_bars` on `(symbol, date)`. Returns 0 and **never deletes existing history** when the fetch is empty (a transient provider outage must not erase a ticker's price series). |
| `ingest/quotes.py` | Warm-tier data: watchlisted + recently viewed symbols. `warm_symbols()` = watchlisted + recently viewed (last 7 days, via `RecentlyViewedRow` — written by `GET /research/{symbol}` in `routers/research.py`). `refresh_quotes() -> int` is guarded by `is_rth()` (no-op outside regular trading hours) and registered on a 15-min `IntervalTrigger` in `jobs.py`. A missing quote (`None`) is not a price of zero and writes no row. **On-demand single-symbol seed (2026-09-14):** `refresh_quote_for(symbol) -> bool` is `materialize()`'s cold-path counterpart to `refresh_quotes`'s periodic sweep — called from `ingest/materialize.py::_quote()` the first time a symbol with no `QuoteRow` at all is viewed, so a first-ever view doesn't sit blank for up to 15 minutes waiting on the next RTH poll. Deliberately **not** RTH-gated (a quiet-hours view should still show the last close). `change_pct` (both here and in the periodic sweep) is `_day_change_pct()`: the change versus the most recent *completed* trading session's close in `daily_bars` (via `_prior_close()`, which itself seeds `daily_bars` on demand — the same cold-path backfill the `/bars` route uses — when none exist yet), never the delta between two consecutive polls that the field used to carry (a number that drifted further from a real day change every cycle, and stayed `None` for a symbol's entire first cycle since there was no earlier poll to diff against). The **nightly warm-tier refresh** also lives here: `refresh_warm_bars(symbols=None)` re-ingests daily bars and `refresh_warm_news(symbols=None)` persists news for every warm symbol, each with per-symbol isolation (one symbol's outage never stops the rest), and `refresh_warm_tier()` runs both as one job (never raises; returns per-part counts for the log). Scheduled by `jobs.py` at `research.tiers.warm_refresh_hour_et` (default 04:00 ET) — the P0-P1 design's "warm tier — refreshed nightly," which fills `daily_bars`/`news_items` for warm names so the ticker page's chart and persisted news have data. |
| `ingest/news.py` | `ingest_news(symbol, limit=25) -> int` and `recent_news(symbol, limit=25) -> list[dict]` — reads `get_news_provider().get_headlines`, handles both yfinance shapes (legacy flat `title` and newer `{"content": {...}}` nesting), scores each headline with the VADER helper in `src/analytics/sentiment.py`, sorts newest-first (the provider's own ordering isn't a documented contract, so this isn't relied on; an unparseable date sorts last), and upserts `NewsItemRow` deduplicated on `(symbol, url)`. Enrichment tier — never reaches the deterministic engine. |
| `ingest/jobs.py` | The worker's job runner and scheduler. `run_job(name, fn) -> bool` calls `fn`, logs and swallows any exception, and records a heartbeat (`record_heartbeat`) only on success; `read_heartbeat()` returns the last successful run's timestamp, or `None` before the worker has ever completed a job — surfaced at `GET /health → worker_heartbeat`. `build_scheduler()` registers four jobs: `refresh_symbol_directory` on a weekly cadence from `research.tiers.directory_refresh_days` (the default 7 is the Sunday 03:00 ET cron; any other value is an interval from process start), `drain_ingest_jobs` on a 30s `IntervalTrigger` (M3), `refresh_quotes` on a 15-min `IntervalTrigger` (M4, warm-tier quote refresh during RTH only), and `warm_refresh` — the nightly warm-tier bars+news pass (`refresh_warm_tier`) on a `CronTrigger` at `research.tiers.warm_refresh_hour_et` (default 04:00 ET). The scheduler is built with `job_defaults={"misfire_grace_time": None}` (2026-09-11): this is a background process routinely left running unattended on a laptop, and APScheduler's own default (1 second) silently drops — forever, not just once — any job discovered late, which is every job after a macOS sleep freezes the process for any length of time; `None` removes the grace window entirely so a job runs the moment the process next gets CPU time, however late, and `coalesce` (already the default) still collapses a multi-day backlog into a single catch-up run. This makes the worker recover from a sleep/wake cycle without a manual restart — it does not prevent the sleep itself; run it under `caffeinate` or launchd with sleep disabled for that. |
| `checks/` | Deterministic checks engine. **M5 (complete, 2026-09-05):** `definitions.py` loads the check catalogue from `config/research_checks.yaml` (`CheckOp`, `CheckDef`, `load_checks`, `checks_for`); `engine.py` evaluates every check against a metrics dict into one of four states — `PASS`/`FAIL`/`UNKNOWN`/`NOT_APPLICABLE` — where `UNKNOWN` is never coerced to `FAIL` and `NOT_APPLICABLE` means the question does not apply to the instrument (fundamental categories against an ETF); `metrics.py` holds the registered metric functions (`METRICS` registry, `build_metrics` assembling the flat dict from `NormalizedFinancials` + quote + IV stats + fundamentals — the options/IV and ETF-fund fields are populated from real `get_iv_stats`/`get_fundamental_stats` calls, Task 5.5); `warnings.py` surfaces structural caveats (leveraged-ETF daily-reset decay) as `Warning` objects alongside the checks, never as checks themselves; `payload.py` (Task 5.7) assembles `evaluate()` + `warnings_for()` into `ChecksPayload`/`CategoryPayload` — the shape `materialize()` and the API return — with an asymmetric exclusion rule: a stock never gets a `fund` category at all (not even `NOT_APPLICABLE`), while an ETF gets all seven categories with the five fundamental ones `NOT_APPLICABLE` and a `note` explaining why. Must never import `src.research.summary` — the AI summary is enrichment and must not reach the deterministic checks (fence test). Full check catalogue with every threshold and its rationale: `docs/web/checks.md`. |
| `summary/` | Pluggable AI summary backends. **M7 (complete, 2026-09-05):** `protocol.py` (`Summary` Pydantic model + `SummaryProvider` Protocol — `generate(context) -> Summary | None`, fail-soft), `context.py` (`ResearchContext` + `build_context` — carries computed values only, never asks the model to calculate; `UNKNOWN` checks preserved as `UNKNOWN`; `caveats` always carries the quantitative limit), `prompt.py` (`build_prompt` — the contract every backend shares: every number must appear in the context, never calculate, never recommend a trade, output strict JSON), `claude_cli.py` (`parse_summary` — shared parser across all backends, tolerates JSON wrapped in prose/fenced blocks, returns `None` on any failure; `ClaudeCliSummaryProvider` reuses `src/claude/runner.py`'s subprocess shape), `anthropic.py`/`openai.py`/`ollama.py` (three transport backends — Anthropic Messages API and OpenAI Chat Completions via `httpx` with no SDK dependency; `ollama` reuses `src/claude/ollama_runner.py`'s host/timeout config; each fails soft: missing key logs once and returns `None`, timeout/malformed returns `None`), `service.py` (`summary_for` + `cached_summary_for` + `is_watchlisted` — cache key `(symbol, model, prompt_hash, data_as_of)`, a changed `data_as_of` misses, a row older than `research.summary.cache_ttl_hours` is `stale`; the cache-key `model` is config-derived so GET and POST agree on the row, while the provider's self-reported `Summary.model` is carried in the payload for attribution. `GET` calls `cached_summary_for` first — on a hit it returns the cached row with **no `materialize` call, no model call, no fetch**; on a miss it returns `unavailable` with a reason. `POST` generates on demand, fail-soft to `pending`), `factory.py` (`get_summary_provider` reading `research.summary.backend`). Enrichment only — influences nothing in the engine or checks path. The fence is enforced by `tests/test_web_fence.py`: the checks engine and every `engine/`/`execution/`/`strategies` module never import `src.research.summary`, the summary layer never writes to any database but its own, and the recommendations route does not call `generate_buy_candidates`. |

Config lives in `config/research.yaml` (loaded by `ResearchCfg` in `src/common/config.py`).
Secrets (`WEB_API_TOKEN`, `SEC_CONTACT_EMAIL`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`) live in `.env`, never in the YAML. The
canonical line-item → us-gaap concept map lives in `config/research_concepts.yaml` (loaded
directly by `src/research/ingest/concepts.py::load_concept_map` — adding a mapping is a
config change, not a code change, which matters because the long tail of filers is where
concept resolution breaks).

---

### `src/common/` — Shared building blocks

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `schemas.py` | Pydantic data models that all modules use to pass data between each other (`TradeCandidate`, `PositionSnapshot`, `ScoreCard`, `RiskVerdict`, `ClaudeReview`, etc.). The enrichment-evaluation shapes also live here: `VerdictOutcome`, `BaselineDecision`, `VerdictRecord` (one ledger entry), and `ScoreBucket`/`SignalCorrelation`/`ScoreOutcomeReport` (score-vs-outcome analysis, N22). **P3-P4 M4 Task 4.2 adds the P&L engine's six read-side types**: `PnlLeg`, `CampaignPnl`, `PnlBucket`, `PnlSummary`, `EquityPoint`, `EquityCurve` (see the data-flow table below) — `PnlLeg.outcome` reuses `VerdictOutcome` so the reporting layer and the outcome ledger share one outcome vocabulary. **Trade ledger types** (docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-05-trade-ledger.md, Tasks 1-12): `LedgerContract`/`ParsedExecution`/`ParsedCashEvent`/`ParsedCorporateAction`/`ParsedFxRate`/`LedgerParseError`/`ParsedStatement` (what every feed — CSV, Flex, live — normalises to before `src/ledger/ingest.py` writes it) and `LedgerImportResult`; the read-side builder types `LedgerClose`/`LedgerTrade`/`LedgerOrphan`/`LedgerStockLot`/`LedgerStockDisposal`/`LedgerCashItem`/`LedgerTicker`/`LedgerBasisPoint`/`LedgerTickerDetail`/`LedgerMonth`/`LedgerCurvePoint`/`LedgerBucket`/`LedgerSummary`/`LedgerBook` that `src/reporting/trade_ledger.py::build_book` returns; and the three `Literal` vocabularies `LedgerSourceKind` (`exec`/`order`), `LedgerSource` (`csv`/`flex`/`live`), `LedgerOutcome` (`Open`/`Pending`/`Expired`/`Assigned`/`Called away`/`Exercised`/`Bought back`/`Sold`/`Rolled`) declared once and reused everywhere a ledger outcome or feed needs to be named — including the `ledger_annotate` command payload's `outcome_override`. |
| `config.py` | Loads and validates `config/*.yaml` and `.env`. `config_path` resolves the four private files (`PRIVATE_CONFIG_FILES`) to the operator's copy or its committed `*.example.yaml` |
| `logging.py` | Structured logging setup — colourised console output (green INFO, yellow WARNING, red ERROR) and a plain rotating file log; `setup_logging()` is the single call-site used by all entry points. A `_NoiseFilter` on both handlers truncates oversized `ib_async.wrapper` error strings and collapses runs of near-identical messages (e.g. repeated "Can't find EId with tickerId:..." per option contract during `/scan`) to the first few occurrences plus a "(suppressed N further repeats)" summary, so a chain scan doesn't produce multi-MB logs. The file handler's path is `logs/test.log` instead of the configured `logging.file` (default `logs/system.log`) whenever `"pytest" in sys.modules` — this keeps mocked failure-injection from the test suite out of the production log, since it's imported ahead of any test module and thus ahead of every module-level `get_logger()` call. |
| `market_hours.py` | Self-contained US equity-market calendar + RTH gate (`today_et`, `is_rth`, `is_new_entry_window`, `session_close`, `is_market_holiday`, `is_early_close`, `seconds_until_next_aligned_mark`, `seconds_until_time`, `now_et_hhmm`). **`today_et()` is the single definition of "today"** for anything measured in trading days — DTE, earnings and ex-div windows, the journal's day key, and the `daily_cached` cache key. `date.today()` returns the *server's* local date, which for an operator outside US/Eastern is routinely a day ahead of the exchange: that mismatch printed two different DTEs inside one roll alert (`DTE=12 <= threshold=14` beside `DTE 13`), shifted the earnings-blackout window in the risk engine, and mis-keyed the settled-bar check in `price_data`. `OptionQuote.dte` has always been ET-anchored; everything else now agrees with it. Reserve `date.today()` for genuinely local concerns (log filenames, wall-clock scheduling). Computes NYSE full-day holidays and the 13:00 ET early-close sessions for any year — no external calendar dependency. The single source of truth for "is the market open"; both the execution bridge and the intraday loop call it instead of keeping their own weekday-only check. `is_new_entry_window` additionally enforces the configurable `entry_cutoff` time (default 15:00 ET) so no new positions are surfaced in the last hour. `seconds_until_next_aligned_mark(interval_minutes)` returns the ET wall-clock delay until the next `:00`/`:15`/`:30`/`:45`-style mark, used by the intraday loop so its cycles land on 9:30, 9:45, 10:00, ... instead of drifting with process restarts. `seconds_until_time(hh, mm)` returns the ET wall-clock delay until the next occurrence of a specific HH:MM (today if still ahead, else tomorrow) — used by the premarket snapshot loop to fire at 09:00 ET daily. `now_et_hhmm()` returns the current time as `"HH:MM ET"`, used by the scan-started message and the appended per-cycle status lines. |
| `cache.py` | `@daily_cached` — a thread-safe, process-local memoizer keyed by `(args, today)`. Wraps the yfinance hot paths (`get_fundamental_stats`, `_compute_hv30`, `price_data.get_ohlcv`) and the sentiment sources (`sentiment._fetch_stocktwits`, `_fetch_news`, `fetch_sentiment`) so the 15-min intraday loop fetches each symbol at most once per calendar day. `clear_all()` is called by the test harness between cases. |
| `assignment_risk.py` | **M0 Task 0.3 — the one assignment-risk predicate.** `is_assignment_risk(*, position, delta, dte, delta_threshold, dte_threshold)` is a plain-values function (floats/ints, not a `PositionSnapshot` or `OptionQuote`) so neither caller has to construct the other's domain type: `src/monitor/triggers.py::check_assignment_risk` (a trading process) delegates the yes/no decision to it and keeps owning the `RollAlert` it builds; `src/api/routers/options.py`'s `GET /options/shorts` (the web layer, which may not import the monitor) reads the same function directly. Before this module the two callers disagreed — the monitor used the real config (`monitor.assignment_alert_delta`/`assignment_alert_dte`, 0.70/21) while the API hardcoded `0.70`/`7` under a comment incorrectly claiming 7 was "the monitor's default". `assignment_risk_thresholds(cfg)` returns `(delta_threshold, dte_threshold)` from config so neither caller hardcodes either number again. Missing `delta` or `dte` is data-unavailable and returns `False`, never a fabricated `True`; a non-negative `position` (not a short) also returns `False`. |
| `universe.py` | **M7 Task 7.2 — the universe composition layer.** `effective_universe()` composes `universe_overrides` rows (`src/storage/universe_overrides.py`) onto `config/universe.yaml` (`get_config().universe`): only `would_own` and `watchlist` are overridable (`OVERRIDABLE_LISTS`) — every other key (`indexes`, `actively_wheeling`, `sectors`, `strike_bands`, `leveraged_etfs`) passes through byte-for-byte, so `sectors` (risk_engine's concentration limits) can never move from the web, even from an override row bypassing the API. Passthrough containers are copied one level deep (`dict(v)`/`list(v)`), not aliased (M7 final-review Fix 5) — the returned dict never shares object identity with `get_config().universe`'s own `lru_cache`-shared containers. A composed list is YAML file order minus removed symbols, plus added symbols appended in `created_at` order. A `remove` for a symbol in `actively_wheeling` is ignored when composing `would_own` specifically (you can't stop being willing to own something you're actively wheeling). Reads go through `src.storage.db.session_scope()`, not the API's write-scoped `trading_session`/`get_command_engine` fence — this module only reads. Results are cached in-process for `_TTL_SECONDS` (60s, a plain `(dict, time.monotonic())` module-level tuple — no caching library); `invalidate_universe_cache()` is the escape hatch a command drain calls so its own process sees an edit immediately rather than up to 60s later. **Fail-safe:** any exception reading the overrides table (locked SQLite, missing table, anything) is caught broadly, logs a warning, and returns the YAML base untouched — never raises, never empties the universe. Separate from `get_config()` deliberately: `get_config()` is `functools.lru_cache`'d with no invalidation and is called from the risk engine, so composing inside it would both import a storage module into `config.py` and never become visible without a process restart. |
| `books.py` | Which book a contract belongs to: `spreads_underlyings()` / `is_spreads_underlying()`. The only way wheel code (portfolio, rules engine, ledger ingest) learns the spreads book exists. Book = underlying, because positions carry no order tag. |

**Rule:** Modules never pass raw IBKR objects to each other — they always convert to these shared
schemas first. This keeps modules independent and testable.

---

### `src/ledger/` — Trade ledger ingest (the only writer of the broker-truth tables)

**docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-05-trade-ledger.md, Tasks 1-12 — built and committed on
`feat/trade-ledger`.** Every execution the IBKR account ever recorded — not just what this
system itself placed — normalises to one `ParsedStatement` shape regardless of feed, and lands
through one writer. Same tier discipline as `src/reporting/`: unreachable from
`engine/`/`execution/`/`strategies/`, imports nothing from `src.claude` directly (transitively only via `src.execution.reconciliation`'s helper in `live.py`). The web
pages (`web/app/ledger/*`) and the fence tests (`tests/test_web_fence.py::test_the_trading_path_never_imports_the_ledger`
and its two companions) are built — see `CLAUDE.md`'s "`src/ledger/` fence" paragraph and `STATUS.md`.

```
 IBKR TWS ──(commissionReportEvent hook, any hour; + RTH-only reqExecutions sweep)──────┐
 IBKR Flex Web Service ──(src/orchestrator/eod_report.py step 7b, AFTER the Telegram ───┼─► src/ledger/ingest.py ──► broker_* tables
                           send — R6, spec §2 amended; also scripts/ledger_flex_pull.py │              │
                           for a manual/backfill pull)                                 │              │
 CSV upload — python -m scripts.ledger_import, or                                      │              │
   POST /commands {kind: "ledger_import"} ──► drain ───────────────────────────────────┘              │
 POST /commands {kind: "ledger_annotate" | "ledger_ca_reviewed"} ──► drain ──────────► trade_annotations /
                                                                                       corporate-action reviewed flag
                                                                                        │
                                     src/reporting/trade_ledger.py::build_book (read-only)
                                          │                                  │
                          src/api/routers/ledger.py (GET /ledger/*)   src/ledger/sheets_mirror.py
                                          │                                  │
                    web/app/ledger/* (overview, trades, ticker,         Google Sheet tabs named in
                    import — uploads/edits go via POST /commands)       ledger.sheets_tabs (or 3 "(auto)" tabs)
```

The plan's original architecture diagram (spec §2) ran the Flex pull on its own nightly launchd
schedule, parallel to EOD. **R6 amended that**: `run_flex_pull` is called from inside
`src/orchestrator/eod_report.py`'s step 7b instead — after the Telegram send, so a slow Flex poll
(up to `ledger.flex_poll_timeout_seconds`, default 600s) never delays the operator's EOD report —
and its own failure is caught and logged, never failing the EOD run. `scripts/ledger_flex_pull.py`
still exists as a standalone CLI for a manual pull or a one-off historical backfill query.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `contracts.py` | Shared parsing helpers: `parse_option_symbol` (`"AMD 29AUG25 157.5 P"` → a `LedgerContract`), `stock_contract`, `parse_et_timestamp`/`parse_ibkr_date` (IBKR statement timestamps are US/Eastern wall-clock, normalised to UTC), `et_date` (the ledger's one trade-date definition — the US/Eastern calendar date of a timestamp), `parse_number` (`"1,385"` → `1385.0`). |
| `activity_csv.py` | IBKR Activity Statement CSV → `ParsedStatement`. The file is multi-section (column 0 = section, column 1 = Header/Data/SubTotal/Total/Notes); every Data row zips against its section's most recent Header. Rows are **order-level** (`DataDiscriminator "Order"`), never execution-level — no `execId`. `NotAnActivityStatement` is raised for a PDF or a non-statement CSV. `number_occurrences` distinguishes genuinely identical rows within one file (an `occurrence_idx`) so repeats of the same contract/time/qty/price in one import still survive dedupe as separate rows. |
| `flex.py` | The IBKR Flex Web Service client (`fetch_statement` — SendRequest → poll GetStatement with backoff, retrying IBKR's `1018`/`1019` "not ready yet" codes) + Flex XML parser (`parse_flex_xml`). `_merge_codes` folds a Flex `<Trade>`'s `openCloseIndicator` into `notes` so the reporting layer's pure-close rule (reads `codes` alone) sees it. Every error path redacts the token-bearing request URL before it can reach a log line or a stored status (`_redacted_http_summary`, `_request`'s docstring) — a Flex failure is recorded as `"failed: HTTP 500"`, never with the token in it. `run_flex_pull(query_id=None, dry_run=False)` is the single entrypoint `scripts/ledger_flex_pull.py` and the EOD step call; it returns `None` (not an error) when `IBKR_FLEX_TOKEN`/`IBKR_FLEX_QUERY_ID` aren't set. Option Exercises/Assignments/Expirations is not read in v1 — those still arrive as Trades rows with `openCloseIndicator`/`notes` codes. |
| `live.py` | Converts `ib_async` `Fill`/`Execution`/`CommissionReport` objects to `ParsedExecution` — the one place in the ledger that touches a raw ib_async object. Two feeds: `attach_live_hook` subscribes `ib.commissionReportEvent` (fires once the commission is known, at any hour); `live_sweep_loop` periodically reuses the exec process's own bounded `reconciliation._req_executions_bounded` to catch anything the event missed, but **the periodic sweep itself only runs during RTH** (`is_rth()`) — the commission-report hook is unaffected by that gate. Fills for any account other than the one the ledger is locked to (e.g. a paper session running while the ledger tracks the real account) are dropped before `ingest` is ever called — no trace, not even a failed run row. Fully best-effort: a failure here is logged and never propagates into order handling. |
| `ingest.py` | **The only writer of the `broker_*` tables.** `ingest(statement, source, filename=None)` is one transaction per call: upserts executions by a dedupe key (`exec:<exec_id>` when the feed carries one, else a hash of contract+time+qty+price+occurrence), then runs the **order/exec twin pass** (`supersede_twins`) over every `(contract, ET date)` an import touched — an order-level CSV/Flex row and the matching exec-level Flex/live rows are the same fill reported twice by different feeds; the lower-priority (order-level) row is marked `superseded_by` the exec-level survivor, and its codes (`O`/`C`/`A`/`Ep` — live fills carry none) are copied onto the survivor if it has none, so arrival order never matters and nothing is ever double-counted. `_tag_spreads` runs first and marks `book="spreads"` on rows whose underlying belongs to the daily credit-spread book (`src/common/books.py` — SPY/SPX/XSP, never in the wheel universe), so CSV/Flex rows with no order id are tagged too; `_tag_books` then marks `book="system"` on rows whose `ib_order_id` matches a known trading-system `OrderRow` (vs. `"manual"` for everything else) and never retags a spreads row, because order ids are unique only per clientId and the spreads service (clientId 30) can reuse a wheel order's id. The run's `counts` carry both (`"spreads"`, `"system"`). A `source="live"` ingest that neither inserts nor changes anything leaves no `ledger_import_runs` row at all (pure no-op, never clutters the table); every csv/flex run keeps its run row regardless of outcome. The account lock (R8): the first CSV/Flex import with no `ledger.account` configured locks the ledger to that statement's account; a later import for a different account fails `account_mismatch`; a live fill for an unmatched/no-account-yet account is silently skipped (no trace), so a paper session can never pollute — or claim — the real account's ledger. |
| `annotations.py` | The only writer of `trade_annotations` and the corporate-action `reviewed` flag — called only from the drain handlers (`src/notify/command_drain.py`), never the API process directly. `annotate(session, order_key, fields, updated_by)` applies only the keys present in `fields` (an explicit `notes=""` clears the note; an omitted `notes` leaves it untouched); `mark_corporate_action_reviewed(session, ca_id)` returns `False` for an unknown id. Both bump the ledger generation counter. |
| `sheets_mirror.py` | One-way mirror into the operator's Google Sheet: writes the tabs named in `ledger.sheets_tabs` (role → gid: `options` trades, `buy_and_hold` open-lot holdings via `trade_ledger.buy_hold_rows`, `tickers`, `summary`) as a full rewrite each sync, never touching a tab that isn't listed; with an empty map it falls back to three tabs it owns — `Ledger (auto)`, `Tickers (auto)`, `Summary (auto)`. `GspreadWriter.write_tab` takes a tab title (created if missing) or a gid (must already exist). Generation-driven (`src/ledger/state.py`'s counter, bumped by every ingest/annotation): `sync_if_due` syncs only when the generation is ahead of the last-synced one, throttled to at most once per `ledger.sheets_min_interval_seconds`. `GspreadWriter` is the real writer (needs the spreadsheet shared with the service-account email); failures are scrubbed of the configured sheet id / credentials path (`_scrub_secrets`) before they're logged or stored in `system_settings`, then retried on the next poll — never fatal, never block ingestion. Unrealized P&L is deliberately not mirrored (no live marks in the sheet). |
| `state.py` | `system_settings` keys shared across the ledger: the change-generation counter (`bump_generation`, read by the Sheets mirror), the locked account (`ledger_account`/`lock_account`, R8), and the Flex/Sheets feed-status keys `GET /ledger/imports` reads. |

---

### `src/reporting/` — The P&L engine (web tier, read-only)

**P3-P4 M4.** The third, read-only analytics tier: downstream of everything, upstream of nothing.
It reads the whole book (including enrichment-side tables), which is why it sits on the read side
of the tier line exactly as `src/api/` and `src/research/` do — `src/reporting/` imports nothing
from `src.claude`, and `src/engine/`, `src/execution/` and `src/strategies/` may never import
`src.reporting`. Both asserted in `tests/test_web_fence.py`.

The one inversion the fence allows: `src/claude/eval/reconcile.py` imports the neutral read-only
accounting module from here — the fence stops `eval/` reaching the **engine**, not `eval/`
importing a read-only module. The dependency was deliberately inverted in M4 Task 4.1 so the
reconciler and the reporting layer share **one** accounting rule by construction rather than by
coincidence.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `legs.py` | The **paired realised-P&L accounting rule**, moved verbatim out of `src/claude/eval/reconcile.py` (M4 Task 4.1): `fill_economics(fills)` splits SELL/BUY fills into credit/debit dollars, commissions (nulls summed as zero, flagged via `commissions_complete`), and the qty-weighted entry premium; `classify_outcome(...)` is the outcome decision tree (`closed_early` / `assigned` / `expired_worthless` / `still_open` / `user_rejected` / `risk_rejected` / `not_filled`) with `realized = credit − debit − commissions`. `reconcile.py` imports these — a second implementation must never appear. |
| `pnl.py` | The read-only P&L builders over the trading DB: `build_legs` (one `PnlLeg` per candidate with fills — `net_pnl` is `None` while open, never `0.0`), `build_campaigns` (wheel-cycle threads, stock leg read from `CampaignRow`, never recomputed; `snapshot=None` means every unrealised field is `None`, never zero), `build_summary` (pure aggregation; **raises `ValueError` on a mixed paper/live leg list** rather than returning a total that means nothing), `equity_curve` (one point per journal row, `gaps` for missed trading days, never interpolated; the journal's `realized_pnl` column is surfaced under its true name `premium_cashflow`). |
| `trade_ledger.py` | **The whole-account trade-ledger builder** (docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-05-trade-ledger.md, Tasks 1-12) — read-only, like `pnl.py`, and a different view from it: `pnl.py` is the *system-performance* view (only orders this system placed); `trade_ledger.py` is the *whole-account* view (every execution the broker ever recorded, including manual TWS trades and pre-system history). `build_book(session, today, snapshot, marks_as_of=None)` is the single entrypoint everything else renders from — the trades table, ticker pages, CSV export, and the Google Sheets mirror all read the same `LedgerBook` so they can never disagree. Reads `BrokerExecutionRow`/`BrokerCashEventRow`/`BrokerCorporateActionRow`/`FxRateRow`/`TradeAnnotationRow` (never writes any of them — `src/ledger/` is the sole writer) and groups executions into orders (`group_orders`, keyed on contract/ET-date/sign/\|qty\|/ordinal, deliberately excluding price so a same-size same-day re-price can't re-key and detach an annotation), FIFO-matches opens against closes into `LedgerTrade`s (outcome, rolls, mixed-close detection), carries unmatched closes as `LedgerOrphan`s, tracks stock lots and wheel-adjusted cost basis (`LedgerStockLot`/`LedgerStockDisposal`), rolls trades up per ticker (`LedgerTicker`/`LedgerTickerDetail`), and produces the portfolio `LedgerSummary` (USD totals via `FxRateRow`, `fx_incomplete` flagged rather than silently zeroed when a rate is missing). **Unrealized P&L uses the position snapshot only when its account matches the ledger's locked account** — a paper-account snapshot (v1's only snapshot source) never prices the real account's open ledger trades, so `unrealized` reads `None` ("n/a") rather than a paper-account number mislabeled as real (`capital_utilised` is computed from ledger data and always has a value; the summary `unrealized_usd` is also `None` whenever any open ticker lacks a mark). **Ticker roll-ups exclude orphan closes** while the summary's `total_realized_usd` includes their IBKR realized P&L, so the ticker rows need not sum to the summary total; the summary total also includes interest and fees while the P&L curve does not. **Three books** (`LedgerBookName` — `system`, `manual`, `spreads`): an order is `spreads` if any of its executions is, else `system` if any is, else `manual` (`_merge_book`); spreads trades are bucketed under the `"Spread"` strategy in `LedgerSummary.by_strategy` instead of CSP/CC, and appear as their own `by_book` row. Also exposes the sheet-row formatters (`SHEET_HEADER`, `sheet_row`, `ticker_row`, `summary_rows` — shared verbatim by the CSV export and the Google Sheets mirror so the two can't drift) and `filter_trades`/`TradeSort` (the `/ledger/trades` query logic). |

---

### `src/ops/` — The out-of-process watchdog and the Gateway restarter

**Scan-loop remediation Task 5.** Everything else in this repo that watches for trouble —
the intraday monitor's triggers, the approval service's own heartbeat write — runs *inside* one
of the always-on processes `scripts/start.py` supervises. That's a blind spot: if the whole stack
(or the Mac it runs on) stops, nothing is left running to notice or alert. The 2026-09-15..22
outage went unnoticed for exactly this reason. `src/ops/watchdog.py` is the fix: a **one-shot**
script (`scripts/watchdog.py`, `python -m scripts.watchdog`) meant to be scheduled by launchd or
cron, entirely outside the supervised process tree, with no IBKR connection and no clientId. On
macOS, **Task 6**'s `./ibkr install` schedules it as its own `com.ibkr.watchdog` launchd agent
(`StartInterval = watchdog.interval_seconds`), deliberately a *separate* agent from
`com.ibkr.supervisor` (which runs `scripts.start`) rather than a thread inside it — see
"Process architecture" below and `scripts/launchd.py`.

Every check is a pure function over its inputs (an ISO timestamp, a fixed `now`, RTH/trading-day
booleans, thresholds) — testable without a DB or a live stack. `run_checks(now, cfg)` composes
seven of them:

| Check | Fails when |
|---|---|
| `supervisor` | No process matches `pgrep -f "scripts.start"` |
| `gateway_port` | TCP connect to `127.0.0.1:{ibkr_port}` refused or times out (2s) |
| `command_drain` | `command_drain_heartbeat` older than `watchdog.heartbeat_max_age_minutes` |
| `monitor` | `monitor_heartbeat` stale by the same limit — **checked only during RTH** |
| `scan_loop` | During RTH, `scan_grace_minutes` past the open, `intraday_scan_completed` older than `scan_max_age_minutes` — **quiet while halted (`is_halted()`) or past `scheduler.entry_cutoff`** (final review M1: the loop skips its new-entry scan in both, so the marker goes stale by design; read from the DB flag and config, no IBKR) |
| `eod` | On a trading day, `scheduler.eod_report` + `watchdog.eod_grace_minutes` past, `eod_completed` isn't dated today in ET. Means the EOD run *reached its end*, not that every step inside it (the IV backfill, the Telegram send) succeeded — `iv_history` below covers IV staleness on its own |
| `iv_history` | Any universe ∪ held symbol's newest `iv_history` observation is more than `watchdog.iv_max_stale_trading_days` trading sessions old (or missing) — reported as one message listing every offending symbol |

`decide_alerts(checks, state, now, realert_minutes=...)` is the alert state machine (pure — it
makes no Telegram call itself). It sends on an ok→fail transition, re-sends every
`realert_minutes` while a check keeps failing (**except `iv_history`, which re-sends at most once
per ET calendar day** — the brief calls it "evaluated once per day", and the shared
`realert_minutes` default of 60 would otherwise re-fire it roughly hourly all day, every day it
stays stale; `_alert_due` special-cases that one check name against the ET date of its own last
alert instead of an elapsed-minutes window), and sends a "✅ recovered" notice on fail→ok — so one
initial outage alert doesn't spam every 5 minutes, but a still-broken stack doesn't go silent for
hours either.

`decide_alerts` returns `(alerts, candidate_state)`: `alerts` is the list of `(check_name,
message)` pairs due to send this cycle, and `candidate_state` is what `data/watchdog_state.json`
(gitignored, like `data/eod_scheduler_state.json`) *would* become if every one of those alerts is
actually delivered — it is not committed on trust. `main()` only promotes a check's
`candidate_state` entry into what it writes once `send_telegram` for that check's message
returns `True`; a failed send leaves that check's entry exactly as it was **before** the cycle,
so the very next run (5 minutes later, not a full `realert_minutes` or ET-day later) tries again
instead of the outage being silently marked "alerted" when nothing was ever delivered — fixed in
review round 1 (finding 1), which found the original version stamped `last_alert` unconditionally
regardless of whether `send_telegram` succeeded. `send_telegram` posts **plain text with no
`parse_mode`** through the raw Bot API, deliberately bypassing `src/notify/formatters.py`'s
MarkdownV2 escaping — a bug in that escaping must never be able to silence the one channel that
reports the stack itself is down. `main()` never raises: any unexpected exception is caught and
turned into its own "watchdog crashed: …" Telegram alert before returning a non-zero exit code.

**Limitation, by design:** none of this can detect the Mac being powered off or asleep — no
watchdog process runs at all in that case, so there's nothing to alert. Setting
`watchdog.deadman_url` to an external dead-man-switch service (e.g. healthchecks.io) closes that
gap: the watchdog GETs it on every run where every check passes, and *that* external service is
what notices the pings stopping.

Read-only with respect to trading state — it writes only its own state file, never a
`system_settings` row, never an order, never config.

#### `gateway_control.py` — automatic Gateway restart on an Error 10197 block (2026-10-02)

Unlike the watchdog, this runs **inside** `approval_service` — it is called from the intraday
loop's pre-scan health-probe branch when the probe's observed codes include **10197** (another
login on the IBKR username holds the live market-data entitlement the paper account shares). A
Gateway stuck on 10197 does not regain the data when the other session logs out — only a fresh
login clears it (2026-10-02: ~16h of blocked scans until a manual restart), and the loop's usual
recovery (`_force_scan_reconnect`, dropping the scan socket) never re-authenticates Gateway.

`GatewayRestarter.restart()` stops and relaunches the `com.ibkr.gateway` launchd job, so IBC logs
back in from `.env` unattended: it finds the job pid via `launchctl print`, SIGTERMs its process
group (the JVM is a grandchild in that group), waits up to `gateway_recovery.stop_timeout_seconds`
for the group and the job to be gone, SIGKILLs if not, then runs a plain `launchctl kickstart` and
waits up to `port_wait_seconds` for the API port. **Never `kickstart -k`**: it relaunches while the
old JVM is still exiting, `start_gateway.sh`'s duplicate-instance guard exits 0, and Gateway is
left down (verified live 2026-10-02). It refuses — returning a plain-text reason the loop puts in
the 🛑 *Scan blocked* message — when `gateway_recovery.enabled` is false, within
`cooldown_minutes` of the last restart, once `max_restarts_per_day` (per ET day) is spent, or
when the `com.ibkr.gateway` agent isn't loaded (a hand-started Gateway can't log itself back
in). One instance lives in `bot_data`, so the cooldown/cap state spans cycles (in-memory; resets
with the approval service). When a restart was attempted the loop skips the scan-socket reconnect
— the restart already dropped every socket and `AutoReconnect` is on it. The real `launchctl` /
`os.killpg` calls sit behind module-level `_run_cmd`/`_killpg`, which `tests/conftest.py`'s
autouse `_forbid_real_gateway_restart` replaces suite-wide (a loop test once restarted the live
Gateway). No IBKR API connection, no clientId; nothing in `engine/`/`execution/`/`strategies/`
imports it.

---

### `web/` — The Next.js research and portfolio console

Next.js research console (App Router, TypeScript, Tailwind v4). Dark-only token system in
`app/globals.css`; IBM Plex Sans/Mono; `cmdk` command palette (`⌘K`) over the SEC symbol directory;
left rail driven by `GET /nav`. Reads only the web API on port 8787 — holds no broker connection of
its own.

Ticker page at `/stock/[symbol]` renders a price chart (lightweight-charts candlesticks + volume +
SMA 50/200), technicals panel, sentiment panel, news panel, and normalised SEC statements with
filing traceability; polls while a section is pending. M5 adds the checks section
(`components/checks/`): `CheckRibbon` (one segment per check, state carried by `data-state` + hatch
texture not colour alone, score line reads "X of evaluable" never "X of total"), `ChecksSection`
(click-to-expand `<button aria-expanded>` per category, renders leveraged-ETF decay warnings), and
`CheckRow` (statement + actual + threshold + state; unknown renders `n/a`, never `0`) — rendered as
the first section below the price chart. M6 adds the landing page (`app/page.tsx`: search, then
`components/home/WatchlistTable.tsx`, then `SectorGrid.tsx`/`SectorCard.tsx` ordered by IV rank) and
`app/universe/page.tsx` (originally a read-only `universe.yaml` browser — superseded by the M7
rewrite below). M7 adds `components/stock/SummaryPanel.tsx` — the AI summary panel (Generate button
when nothing is cached, distinct labelled regions for thesis/bulls/bears/watch/caveats, model +
`data_as_of` attribution line, caveats always visible) wired in as the last ticker-page section.

P3 M3 adds `app/portfolio/page.tsx` and `components/portfolio/` — the portfolio console: positions
grouped by underlying, campaigns wheel history, expiry calendar, with account balances above a tab
bar. P4 M5 adds `app/pnl/page.tsx` and `components/pnl/` — the P&L console: every trade opened and
closed grouped by campaign thread, the summary breakdowns, and the equity curve (gaps render as
gaps, no animation), with a CSV export that carries the same filters. `app/options/` (P2) is the
options console: `page.tsx` (approvals, assessed, orders/fills/shorts, controls) and
`[approvalId]/page.tsx` (approval detail — review, ideal-zone bar, alternatives) — every
approve/reject/promote/roll/halt/resume/autonomy action goes through `ConfirmAction` →
`submitCommand`/`useCommandStatus` → `CommandReceipt`, the same intent-queue round-trip every write
action in the console uses. `app/universe/page.tsx` (P2-M7) consumes `GET /universe`'s `lists[]`
shape through `components/universe/{UniverseList,OverrideBadge,AddSymbol}.tsx` — add/remove
controls on `would_own`/`watchlist` only, `indexes`/`actively_wheeling` read-only.

`app/ledger/` + `components/ledger/` is the trade-ledger console (reads `GET /ledger/*`; its two
edit actions and the CSV upload go through the command queue like every other write). Pages:
`page.tsx` (overview — `LedgerOverview` with `Tile` KPI tiles, `Charts` cumulative-P&L curve and
monthly bars, `TickerTable`), `trades/page.tsx` (`TradesView` — one composed query string drives
both the fetch and the CSV-export link, with a Book filter of All/System/Manual/Spreads; `TradesTable`, `OutcomePill` — label always printed,
colour secondary — and `TradePanel`, the note/tag/outcome-override editor submitting
